> AGENT

Acquainted. Key points on the per-file approval workflow:

**Per-file review checkpoint** — after each file edit I summarize (2–5 lines) and stop. No follow-on commands (cargo fmt/clippy/test/install, dogfooding, next file) until you give the go-ahead.

**"lg" / "go" / "continue" / "do it"** approves *the next unit only*, not the whole remaining plan. A green light on direction is not a green light to skip per-step checkpoints.

**Exceptions** that ride along with the change (no separate review pause):
- Refactors moving code across files — both halves reviewed together.
- `Cargo.toml` version bumps tied to a specific code change.

**Review before commit block** — when a unit of work is done, I summarize and stop. I don't pre-emptively lay out the `vc-x1 push` invocation. Starting the next step (`do step 6`, `next`) implies the previous step is approved and I commit it before proceeding.

**Multi-step ladder** — first edit of each step is flipping that step's marker in `notes/todo.md` from queued to `(current)` *before* any code/doc work, so In Progress reflects reality.

**Project state I'm seeing**: branch is on `init-clone-refactor` cycle. `0.41.1-5` (init reshape) just landed; ladder in `notes/todo.md:14-22` shows `0.41.1-6 init POR detection + upgrade paths` is next, then `-7` test_helpers migration, then `0.41.1` close-out.

Ready when you are.

1	# Chores-08.md
2	
3	General chores notes for the 0.41.1 init+clone redesign cycle.
4	
5	This file opens at 08 (skipping 07) because chores-07.md is the
6	0.42.0-cycle file living on `main`. The 0.41.1 cycle branched
7	off `6747a27` (the 0.41.0 close-out commit), where chores-07.md
8	doesn't exist. Starting at 08 here avoids any rebase collision
9	on chores-07 when `main` later rebases on top of
10	`init-clone-refactor`.
11	
12	For the broader forking model and partner-bookmark technique
13	that drove this branch's existence, see
14	[`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md).
15	
16	## init + clone redesign (0.41.1)
17	
18	Empirical validation 2026-04-27 against `vc-x1 0.42.0-3` (see
19	[`vc-x1-init.md`](vc-x1-init.md)) surfaced cosmetic anomalies
20	and a substantive design gap. Init's flag surface
21	(`--repo-local`, `--repo-remote`, `--owner`, `--dir`,
22	`[NAME]`) carries 6+ mutually-exclusive preflight checks just
23	to prevent impossible combinations from being typed. Clone
24	already implements a unified-positional alternative (URL /
25	`owner/name` shorthand); this cycle extends that pattern to
26	init, adds POR support to both, and unifies their CLI
27	surfaces.
28	
29	Branched off `6747a27` (0.41.0 close-out). On close-out, the
30	in-flight 0.42.0 work on `main` rebases on top.
31	
32	### Command structure
33	
34	```
35	vc-x1 init  <TARGET> [NAME] [--scope code,bot|por] [--account <a>] [--repo <cat>[=<val>]] [--private] [--dry-run]
36	vc-x1 clone <TARGET> [NAME] [--scope code,bot|por]                                                    [--dry-run]
37	```
38	
39	Init carries `--account`, `--repo`, and `--private`;
40	clone has none of them. Clone's TARGET is the source
41	(URL/path); init's TARGET is the workspace name/path
42	combined with config-resolved remote.
43	
44	`<TARGET>` accepts:
45	
46	- **URL** — `git@host:owner/name(.git)?`,
47	  `https://...(.git)?` (detected by `://` or SSH
48	  `<host>:<path>` shape with `@`).
49	- **`owner/name` shorthand** — single `/`, no path prefix;
50	  resolved to `git@github.com:owner/name.git`.
51	- **Path-prefixed** — `./X`, `../X`, `/X`, `~/X`, `~`,
52	  bare `.`, or bare `..`. Path IS the target; last component
53	  is the workspace name (consumer canonicalizes `.` / `..`
54	  to a real basename via `canonicalize` + `file_name`).
55	- **Bare alphanumeric NAME** — for **init** only, becomes
56	  the workspace name; the remote URL is resolved via the
57	  user config's `--repo` chain
58	  ([`User config`](#user-config-0411-3-redesigned-in-0411-4)).
59	  Missing required config keys produce the
60	  step-specific errors documented there. For **clone**,
61	  bare NAME is an error (genuinely ambiguous — "missing
62	  `./`?" / "missing `/name` suffix?"); clone has no
63	  config-driven defaults.
64	
65	`[NAME]`:
66	
67	- **init**: URL / `owner/name` forms → overrides the derived
68	  workspace name (workspace at `cwd/<NAME>`). Path forms →
69	  error if combined (path already specifies the destination).
70	- **clone**: all forms (URL, `owner/name`, Path-as-source) →
71	  destination dir name in cwd. Without `[NAME]`, derived
72	  from the source via `derive_name`. For clone, TARGET is
73	  the *source* (URL or local repo path) and `[NAME]` is
74	  the *destination* — symmetric with `git clone <src>
75	  [<dest>]`.
76	
77	`--scope`:
78	
79	- `code,bot` (default): both repos, dual-repo layout.
80	- `por`: single repo, no `.vc-config.toml` written.
81	- `code` / `bot` standalone: error. Reason — these are
82	  config-lookup keywords; init has no config to look up
83	  against, and clone's simplification omits them too. The
84	  manual decomposition (clone code as POR, clone bot as
85	  POR, place at `.claude/`, run `vc-x1 symlink`) covers the
86	  rare cases where a user wants the dual layout composed
87	  by hand.
88	
89	`--private`: only meaningful for init when creating a new
90	GitHub repo. Sets visibility at `gh repo create`. Silently
91	warn-and-ignore if the remote already exists. Errors on
92	clone (clone never creates a remote).
93	
94	`--account <name>` (init only): override
95	`[default].account`. Determines which `[account.<name>]`
96	section is consulted for `--repo` resolution.
97	
98	`--repo <cat>[=<val>]` (init only): pick the repo target
99	for this run.
100	- `--repo` absent → use account's `repo.default` →
101	  category's value.
102	- `--repo <cat>` → use `<cat>`'s configured value.
103	- `--repo <cat>=<val>` → use literal `<val>`.
104	
105	Built-in categories: `remote` (value is a URL prefix —
106	init appends `/<NAME>.git`) and `local` (value is the
107	parent dir for fixture bare repos at
108	`<parent>/remote-{code,claude}.git`). Replaces the old
109	`--owner` / `--repo-local` / `--repo-remote` /
110	`--fixture` flag set with one unified mechanism.
111	
112	`--dry-run`: validate the operation as far as possible
113	without side effects. Print what would be done.
114	
115	### Operations
116	
117	**Init** is *smart* — it detects the target dir's state and
118	chooses the right operation.
119	
120	| Target state | `--scope=code,bot` | `--scope=por` |
121	|---|---|---|
122	| empty / nonexistent | create dual-repo from scratch | create POR |
123	| POR (jj+git, no config) | upgrade to dual | error (already POR) |
124	| POR (git only, no `.jj/`) | bootstrap `jj git init`, then upgrade | error (already POR-shape) |
125	| code POR + `.claude/` peer POR (configs missing) | write configs only | error |
126	| dual-repo (configs present) | error (already there) | error (downgrade) |
127	| single-repo (illegal shape) | error | error |
128	| anything else (random files, etc.) | error with diagnostic | error with diagnostic |
129	
130	**Clone** is *dumb* — it just clones sources (URLs or local
131	repo paths) into target dirs.
132	
133	| `--scope=por` | clone the source into `<target_dir>` |
134	| `--scope=code,bot` | clone code source into `<target_dir>`; derive bot source (`<source>.claude`); clone into `<target_dir>/.claude/`; create symlink via `vc-x1 symlink` |
135	
136	For both clone modes: `<target_dir>` must NOT exist
137	(`vc-x1 clone` errors if anything is there). Use
138	`vc-x1 sync` to update an existing checkout.
139	
140	Path-form for clone is symmetric with `git clone /local/bare.git`
141	— useful for local fixtures, CI scratch dirs, integration
142	tests against bare repos. Init/clone differ on what TARGET
143	*means* (init: destination; clone: source) but share the
144	same `parse_target` acceptance set.
145	
146	`.vc-config.toml` files for the dual-repo case are *tracked
147	content in the remotes*, not files clone writes. They ride
148	along with the clone. If a remote doesn't have them
149	committed, `vc-x1 init <path> --scope=code,bot` from inside
150	the cloned dir handles the config-only upgrade case (see
151	init's state table above).
152	
153	`--scope=code,bot` is implemented as in-process composition
154	of the `--scope=por` primitive (called twice: once for code
155	URL, once for derived bot URL) plus a `symlink::create`
156	call. No subprocess overhead.
157	
158	If init or clone fails partway through, manual cleanup is
159	required (no rollback).
160	
161	### Example layouts (local repos)
162	
163	cwd = `~/prgs`
164	
165	`vc-init test-repos/tf1 --scope=code,bot`
166	`vc-init test-repos/tf1`
167

169	close-out).
170	
171	`vc-init ../test-repos/tf1 --scope=code,bot`
172	`vc-init ../test-repos/tf1`
173	
174	TODO: add actual output of ls or tree cmds (fill at
175	close-out).

179	- Verify `jj` is installed (`jj --version`); friendly error
180	  with install link if missing.
181	- For init URL / `owner/name` forms: probe with
182	  `git ls-remote <url>`. Exists → clone. Doesn't → create
183	  via `gh repo create` (errors if host isn't GitHub or
184	  `gh` missing).
185	- For clone URL / `owner/name` forms: probe with
186	  `git ls-remote <url>`. Exists → clone. Doesn't → error
187	  (clone is dumb, no auto-create).

285	### User config (0.41.1-3, redesigned in 0.41.1-4)
286	
287	User-global config at `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml`
288	(XDG-compliant; honors `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME`). Backs init's
289	account- and repo-target resolution. No magic fallbacks:
290	missing file or missing key → predictable error pointing
291	at the exact key to set.
292	
293	`-3` shipped a flat first-cut schema (`[default]
294	repo-remote-provider`, `[github] owner`); `-4` replaces it
295	with the account/category structure below before init
296	becomes the first consumer in `-5`. Since `-3` had no
297	consumers, the rewrite is non-breaking.
298	
299	Schema:
300	
301	```toml
302	[default]
303	account = "home"      # default --account when absent
304	debug   = "trace"     # default --debug value when used without arg
305	
306	[account.home]
307	repo.default          = "remote"                       # default --repo cat when absent
308	repo.category.remote  = "git@github.com:winksaville"   # value for --repo remote (no =val)
309	repo.category.local   = "~/test-fixtures"              # value for --repo local (no =val)
310	
311	[account.work]
312	repo.default          = "remote"
313	repo.category.remote  = "git@github.com:anthropic"
314	repo.category.local   = "/work/fixtures"
315	```
316	
317	Three layers — each level has its own default-finding key:
318	
319	| CLI                       | Step 1: account                | Step 2: category                  | Step 3: value                                    |
320	|---|---|---|---|
321	| (no `--repo`/`--account`) | `[default].account`            | `[account.<a>.repo].default`      | `[account.<a>.repo.category].<cat>`              |
322	| `--account <a>`           | `<a>` (CLI)                    | `[account.<a>.repo].default`      | `[account.<a>.repo.category].<cat>`              |
323	| `--repo <cat>`            | `[default].account` (or CLI)   | `<cat>` (CLI)                     | `[account.<a>.repo.category].<cat>`              |
324	| `--repo <cat>=<val>`      | `[default].account` (or CLI)   | `<cat>` (CLI)                     | `<val>` (CLI, literal)                           |
325	
326	Values are **literal targets**, not section-name
327	pointers. For `category = "remote"`, the value is a URL
328	prefix (init appends `/<NAME>.git`); for `category =
329	"local"`, it's the parent dir for fixture bares. Built-in
330	categories `remote` and `local` are recognized; any other
331	category name errors in `-5` (forward-compat: future
332	cycles can add a `kind` field for user-defined categories).
333	
334	Resolution errors (each step has its own message):
335	
336	- Step 1 missing → `no account specified; set [default].account, use --account <name>, or write a top-level [repo] section`.
337	- Step 2 missing → `no default category for account '<a>'; set [account.<a>.repo].default or use --repo <cat>`.
338	- Step 3 missing → `no value for --repo <cat> in account '<a>'; set [account.<a>.repo.category].<cat> or use --repo <cat>=<val>`.
339	
340	#### Single-account shorthand: top-level `[repo]`
341	
342	`[account.<name>]` boilerplate is overhead when there's
343	only one account. The loader accepts a top-level `[repo]`
344	section as a single-account shorthand:
345	
346	```toml
347	[repo]
348	default          = "remote"
349	category.remote  = "git@github.com:winksaville"
350	category.local   = "~/test-fixtures"
351	```
352	
353	3-4 lines for a complete config — no `[default].account`,
354	no `[account.<name>]`. Resolution: when no `--account` is
355	given and no `[default].account` is set, the loader uses
356	the top-level `[repo]` block as the implicit account.
357	
358	Mixing top-level `[repo]` **and** `[account.*]` sections
359	is rejected at load time (ambiguous which one init should
360	consult — error: `mixing top-level [repo] with [account.*]
361	is ambiguous`).
362	
363	Top-level error format uses `[repo.category].<cat>` rather
364	than `[account.<a>.repo.category].<cat>`.
365	
366	Per-workspace `.vc-config.toml` override (ancestor walk)
367	is deferred — chicken-and-egg at init time, and the
368	global file covers the dominant use case.
369	
370	Module shape:
371	
372	- `pub struct UserConfig { default_account, default_debug, top_level_repo: Option<AccountConfig>, accounts: HashMap<String, AccountConfig> }`.
373	- `pub struct AccountConfig { repo_default, repo_category: HashMap<String, String> }`.
374	- `pub struct RepoSelector { category, value: Option<String> }` — parsed `--repo` form.
375	- `load()` / `load_from(path)` — load + group dotted keys.
376	- `resolve_repo(cfg, account_override, repo_cli) -> (cat, value)` — three-step chain.
377	
378	`-4` lands the rewritten module standalone; `-5` (init
379	reshape) is the first consumer.
380	
381	#### Refactoring opportunities (post-0.41.1)
382	
383	Two cleanups surfaced while sizing the module that we
384	deliberately deferred to keep `-3` scoped:
385	
386	- **Unify `.vc-config.toml` accessors onto Pattern B.**
387	  The tree has two patterns for reading TOML config:
388	  - Pattern A (`desc_helpers.rs`, `fix_desc.rs`,
389	    `validate_desc.rs`): call site does
390	    `toml_simple::toml_load(path)` and passes the
391	    resulting `HashMap<String, String>` to map-typed
392	    accessor fns (`other_repo_from_config(&map)`,
393	    `ochid_prefix_from_config(&map)`).
394	  - Pattern B (`push::resolve_state_layout`, new
395	    `config::load_from`): function takes a path,
396	    returns a typed struct with the fields it cares
397	    about; conversion baked in.
398	  Pattern B is more discoverable and testable. A
399	  `WorkspaceConfig` struct with `load_from(path)` could
400	  replace the Pattern-A helpers across desc_helpers /
401	  fix_desc / validate_desc. ~50 LOC, mechanical.
402	- **Layered config precedence** — once `WorkspaceConfig`
403	  is typed, layering user → workspace → CLI becomes
404	  natural (workspace can override `[github].owner` for a
405	  specific project). Init can't use this layer because
406	  it runs *before* a workspace exists, but post-init
407	  commands could.
408	
409	Both candidates for the 0.41.2 cycle. See `notes/todo.md`.
410	
411	### init reshape (0.41.1-5)
412	
413	Init's flag surface replaced with `<TARGET> [NAME] --account
414	--repo --scope`. Six mutually-exclusive flag checks gone;
415	dispatch is now `parse_target` + the config `--repo` chain.
416	
417	**InitArgs surface:**
418	
419	- Dropped: `--owner`, `--dir`, `--repo-local`,
420	  `--repo-remote`, the old `Vec<Side>` scope.
421	- Added: `target` positional (URL, `owner/name`, path, or
422	  bare NAME), optional `[NAME]`, `--account`,
423	  `--repo <cat>[=<val>]`, `--scope code,bot|por` (typed
424	  `ScopeKind`).
425	
426	**Dispatch (`plan_init(args, cfg)`):**
427	
428	- `Target::Url(u)` / `OwnerName(o, n)` → `plan_from_url`.
429	  URL is explicit; `--account` and `--repo` are rejected
430	  ("config not consulted").
431	- `Target::Path(p)` → `plan_from_path`. Path IS the
432	  destination; basename names the repo; remote via the
433	  config chain.
434	- `Target::BareName(n)` → `plan_from_bare_name`.
435	  Destination at `cwd/<n>`; remote via the config chain.
436	- Path/BareName route through `config::resolve_repo` to
437	  `plan_remote` (URL prefix + `/<NAME>.git`) or
438	  `plan_local` (`<parent>/remote-{code,claude}.git` for
439	  dual; `<parent>/remote.git` for POR).
440	
441	**Shared `src/args.rs`:**
442	
443	Cross-subcommand CLI types/parsers lifted from init/clone:
444	`ScopeKind { CodeBot, Por }` (replaces `CloneScope` + a
445	private `InitScope`), `parse_scope_kind` (subcommand-
446	agnostic error wording), `parse_repo_arg`. 0.42.0 sum-type
447	cycle is expected to extend `ScopeKind` with `Single(_)` /
448	path-form variants.
449	
450	**`config::resolve_repo` short-circuit:**
451	
452	`--repo <cat>=<val>` fast-paths — fully self-contained, no
453	account lookup. Lets config-less invocations and test
454	fixtures work without touching disk.
455	
456	**Bug fix (dogfood-surfaced):**
457	
458	`parse_target` mis-classified `host:owner/name` (SSH form
459	missing the `git@` prefix) as `OwnerName(host:owner, name)`,
460	producing a doubled-up URL
461	`git@github.com:host:owner/name.git`. Fix: reject `:` in
462	either half of `owner/name` shorthand. Catch-all error
463	gained a "looks like an SSH URL missing the 'git@' prefix;
464	did you mean 'git@…'?" suggestion.
465	
466	**Debug logging:**
467	
468	`debug!` (visible at `-v`) added for config-and-CLI
469	provenance:
470	
471	- `init` entry — InitArgs fields.
472	- `parse_target` — input → variant.
473	- `plan_init` — final plan summary (project_dir, name,
474	  code_url, provisioner, slugs, bare paths).
475	- `config::load_from` — file hit/miss + parsed summary.
476	- `config::resolve_repo` — per-step source annotation
477	  (`--account CLI` / `[default].account` / `top-level
478	  [repo]` / `--repo CLI value` /
479	  `[account.<a>.repo.category].<cat>`).
480	
481	**Cleanups:**
482	
483	Dropped: `gh_whoami` (only consumer was old
484	`plan_default_github`); `Scope::is_bot_only` and
485	`Scope::is_empty` (only consumers were `Vec<Side>`
486	validation); `normalize_remote` (only consumer was old
487	`plan_external_remote`).
488	
489	**Tests:**
490	
491	319 passing. `init.rs` test module rewritten end-to-end
492	against the new `plan_init(args, cfg)` signature; new
493	`args_for(target)` builder + four `cfg_*` config builders;
494	new tests cover URL/OwnerName/Path/BareName dispatch,
495	account override, top-level `[repo]` shorthand,
496	`--repo cat=val` short-circuit, and all error paths
497	(URL+`--account`, URL+`--repo`, Path+`[NAME]`,
498	BareName+`[NAME]`, bare-name+empty config, unknown
499	category, `--scope=por`+comma template).
500	
501	`test_helpers::Fixture::new_opts` migrated to path TARGET +
502	`--repo local=<base>`; `sync` and `push` integration tests
503	confirm the same on-disk layout.
504	
505	**Deferred:**
506	
507	`init_one` / `init_dual` extraction — `init_with_symlink`
508	operates on `InitPlan` (shape unchanged), so the extraction
509	is a refactor not blocking functionality. Fold into -6
510	alongside POR detection if natural; or its own step.
511	
512	**WIP ladder:**
513	
514	`0.41.1-5.0` dispatch reshape → `-5.1` tests + bug fix +
515	debug logging + CLAUDE.md per-file review subsection +
516	todo rebase note → `-5.2` `gh_whoami` / `Scope` deletions +
517	this chores entry → `-5` done marker.
518	
519	### Decisions made during design
520	
521	- **Version + cycle line.** This work + the sync `--check`
522	  fix land on the 0.41.x line. Init+clone = 0.41.1. Sync
523	  fix = a separate cycle (likely 0.41.2). Then rebase the
524	  in-flight 0.42.0 work on top of both.
525	- **Path-prefix vocabulary.** `./NAME` and the standard
526	  prefixes (`../`, `/`, `~/`, `~`), plus bare `.` and `..`
527	  (POSIX cwd / parent — unambiguous). Bare alphanumeric
528	  `NAME` is an error — explicit prefix required.
529	- **`--private` on existing remote.** Warn and ignore;
530	  visibility was set at create time.
531	- **Cosmetic anomalies** from `notes/vc-x1-init.md` —
532	  addressed at close-out, not deferred.
533	- **`--scope=code` and `--scope=bot` for clone.** Dropped
534	  from the menu. Manual decomposition (two `--scope=por`
535	  clones + `vc-x1 symlink`) covers the use case.
536	- **Composition over duplication.** `--scope=code,bot` is
537	  implemented as in-process composition of the
538	  `--scope=por` primitive — single source of truth for
539	  the actual clone/init operation, thin wrapper for the
540	  dual case.
541	- **Branch fork mechanics.** Code-side `init-clone-refactor`
542	  bookmark created at `6747a27`; bot-side partner bookmark
543	  at current `.claude` `main`. Main left alone as recovery
544	  anchor. See [`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md)
545	  for the full discussion.
546	
547	# References
548	
549	[1]: forks-multi-user.md

Sub-bullets added at `notes/todo.md:21-26`. First sub-task (`init_one`/`init_dual` extraction) marked `(current)` since that's what I'd start on next.

**Rationale for order:**
1. **Refactor first** — pure extraction with no behavior change. Sets clean seams that the upgrade paths plug into. Deferred from `-5` per `chores-08.md:506-510`.
2. **`detect_state` standalone** — read-only inspection, easy to unit-test against fixture dirs without touching dispatch.
3. **Wire detection in** — gate brings detection online; `Empty` keeps current behavior, all other states error with a diagnostic message. Sets up the slots that 4/5/6 fill in.
4. **`PorWithPeerPor`** — simplest upgrade (both repos exist; just write configs + symlink).
5. **`PorJjGit`** — bot-side `init_one` + ochid-link. Real upgrade work.
6. **`PorGitOnly`** — small delta on top of `PorJjGit` (just prepends a `jj git init`).

Per the per-file checkpoint, stopping here for review. On approval I'll start sub-task 1 — `init_one`/`init_dual` extraction.

1344	    run(

1357	        debug!("enable jj tracking of session-side main against origin");

```
fn split_slug(slug: &str) -> Result<(&str, &str), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    let parts: Vec<&str> = slug.split('/').collect();
    if parts.len() != 2 || parts[0].is_empty() || parts[1].is_empty() {
        return Err(format!("unexpected GitHub slug '{slug}' (want 'owner/name')").into());
    }
    Ok((parts[0], parts[1]))
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::{Cli, Commands};
    use clap::Parser;

    fn parse(args: &[&str]) -> InitArgs {
        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from(args).unwrap();
        match cli.command {
            Commands::Init(a) => a,
            _ => panic!("expected New"),
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn defaults() {
        let args = parse(&["vc-x1", "init", "owner/repo"]);
        assert_eq!(args.target, "owner/repo");
        assert!(args.name.is_none());
        assert!(args.account.is_none());
        assert!(args.repo.is_none());
        assert_eq!(args.scope, ScopeKind::CodeBot);
        assert!(!args.private);
        assert!(!args.dry_run);
        assert_eq!(args.push_retries, 5);
        assert_eq!(args.push_retry_delay, 3);
        assert!(args.use_template.is_none());
    }

14	pub struct InitArgs {
15	    /// Target — URL, owner/name shorthand, path, or bare NAME.
16	    ///
17	    /// - URL: `git@host:owner/name(.git)?`, `https://...(.git)?`
18	    ///   — used as-is; config not consulted.
19	    /// - owner/name shorthand: resolves to
20	    ///   `git@github.com:owner/name.git`; config not consulted.
21	    /// - Path: `./X`, `../X`, `/X`, `~/X`, `~`, `.`, `..` — is the
22	    ///   directory path; remote resolved via `--repo` chain.
23	    /// - Bare NAME: becomes NAME.git; remote resolved via
24	    ///   `--repo` chain.
25	    #[arg(value_name = "TARGET", verbatim_doc_comment)]
26	    pub target: String,
27	
28	    /// Repo directory name override (URL / owner/name forms only).
29	    ///
30	    /// - URL / owner/name forms: repo created at `cwd/<NAME>`
31	    ///   instead of the URL-derived name.
32	    /// - Path / bare-NAME forms: error if given (TARGET already
33	    ///   names the repo).
34	    #[arg(value_name = "NAME", verbatim_doc_comment)]
35	    pub name: Option<String>,
36	
37	    /// Account name — picks `[account.<a>]` from user config.
38	    ///
39	    /// - Without this flag, `[default].account` (or top-level
40	    ///   `[repo]` shorthand) is used.
41	    /// - Meaningful only with Path or bare-NAME targets — URL /
42	    ///   owner/name targets supply the remote directly.
43	    #[arg(long, value_name = "NAME", verbatim_doc_comment)]
44	    pub account: Option<String>,
45	
46	    /// Repo target — `<cat>` or `<cat>=<val>`.
47	    ///
48	    /// - Built-in categories: `remote` (URL prefix; init appends
49	    ///   `/<NAME>.git`) and `local` (parent dir for fixture bare
50	    ///   repos at `<parent>/remote-{code,claude}.git`).
51	    /// - `--repo <cat>` looks up the value via the account chain.
52	    /// - `--repo <cat>=<val>` uses the literal value, no config
53	    ///   lookup needed.
54	    /// - Meaningful only with Path or bare-NAME targets.
55	    #[arg(
56	        long,
57	        value_name = "CAT[=VAL]",
58	        value_parser = parse_repo_arg,
59	        verbatim_doc_comment
60	    )]
61	    pub repo: Option<RepoSelector>,
62	
63	    /// ScopeKind — `code,bot` (dual, default) or `por` (single).
64	    #[arg(
65	        long,
66	        short,
67	        value_name = "SCOPE",
68	        value_parser = parse_scope_kind,
69	        default_value = "code,bot",
70	        verbatim_doc_comment
71	    )]
72	    pub scope: ScopeKind,
73	
74	    /// Create private GitHub repos (default: public).
75	    ///
76	    /// - Only meaningful when the resolved provisioner is

78	    ///   value points at GitHub).
79	    #[arg(long, verbatim_doc_comment)]

81	
82	    /// Dry run — show what would be done without executing

84	    pub dry_run: bool,
85	
86	    /// Max push retries after repo creation [default: 5]

89	
90	    /// Seconds between push retries [default: 3]
91	    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 3)]
92	    pub push_retry_delay: u64,
93	
94	    /// Seed repos from template directories.
95	    ///
96	    /// Value is `CODE[,BOT]`. Default bot path is `<CODE>.claude`
97	    /// (file-name concat, not path join — templates are siblings).
98	    ///
99	    /// - With `--scope=por`: only `CODE` is used; passing `,BOT`
100	    ///   is fatal (no session side to seed).
101	    /// - Non-hidden contents copied recursively; hidden entries

103	    ///   own hidden files.
104	    /// - If a copied tree has a `README.md`, its first line is
105	    ///   rewritten to `# <repo-name>`.
106	    #[arg(long, value_name = "CODE[,BOT]", verbatim_doc_comment)]
107	    pub use_template: Option<String>,
108	}

- New **`init_dual`** (dual orchestrator) just below — code+session interleaved: both through step 5, cross-ref ochids in step 6, step-7 clean preserves `.claude/` on the code side, both pushed (step 8 then 9), both re-init'd, symlink in step 11. Three duplicate `session_*` unwraps from the original dual path are consolidated to top-of-fn.

> DEVELOPER

looking at init.rs there is init_one() and init_dual() both large functions. I was expecting there would be synergy between them and was expecting init_dual() to possibly invoke init_one() twice. Logically the process should be create the bot repo, create code repo then symlink. So there should be alot of shared code, I'm sure there is but I think it could be improved. thoughts, no code?

> AGENT

You're right that the duplication is heavy — `init_dual` re-states most of `init_one`'s body verbatim. The reason I didn't compose them naively is one specific constraint:

**Step 6 (ochid cross-reference) is bidirectional and interleaved.** Code's initial commit needs `ochid: /.claude/<sessionchid>`, and session's initial commit needs `ochid: /<codechid>`. Each side needs the *other's* chid before its own commit text can be finalized. Today's flow handles this with placeholder ochids on both step-5 commits, then rewrites both via `jj describe @-` in step 6 — which has to happen *after* both commits exist and *before* step 7 strips jj state. End-to-end-per-side composition can't accommodate this without either an amend-after-push or a callback hook.

So three approaches, ranked by how literally they match your mental model:

**A) `init_dual` calls `init_one` twice (literal).** Each `init_one(target)` runs the full lifecycle through push, with a placeholder ochid in its initial commit. Then `init_dual` re-attaches jj on both, amends both initial commits with real cross-refs, and force-pushes both. Matches your mental model exactly. **Cost:** two pushes per side, force-push semantics, amending an already-published commit.

**B) Split `init_one` into two halves with a seam.** `init_one_create(target, config, template, ochid_strategy) -> chid` covers steps 1–5; `init_one_finalize(target, exclude_from_clean) -> chid_final` covers steps 7–10. `init_dual` becomes: `create(code, placeholder); create(bot, placeholder); cross_ref(both); finalize(code, exclude=.claude); finalize(bot); symlink`. Six logical lines. `init_one` itself is a thin two-line composition. **Cost:** one extra fn per side; not literally "init_one twice" but *very close* — the user-facing primitive is still the create+finalize pair.

**C) Phase decomposition.** Four phases (`create`, `cross_ref`, `strip+push`, `reinit`) instead of two halves. More granular. `init_one` becomes a 4-line composition; `init_dual` interleaves them. Maximum reuse, but more pieces.

I'd recommend **B**. It eliminates the duplication, makes the primitive callable for the upgrade paths in sub-tasks 4/5/6 (PorJjGit needs `init_one_create(.claude, session-shape)` + a partial finalize), and avoids the force-push ugliness of A. The literal "init_one twice" framing is sacrificed only because of the cross-ref's bidirectional nature — and B keeps the spirit (one primitive, called per side) while honoring the constraint.

Want me to go with B? If so, the current sub-task 1 commit becomes a stepping stone — I'd land another refactor pass before moving to sub-task 2 (`detect_state`).

5	//! collide. Lifted out of the `sync` test module (originally inline at
6	//! `sync.rs:521–560`) so `push`'s tests (0.37.0) and any future
7	//! subcommand's tests can sit on the same harness without
8	//! copy-paste drift.
9	//!
10	//! Test-only — the whole module is gated at its declaration site via
11	//! `#[cfg(test)] mod test_helpers;`.
12	
13	use std::fs;
14	use std::path::PathBuf;
15	use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU64, Ordering};
16	use std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};
17	
18	use crate::args::ScopeKind;
19	use crate::common::write_file;
20	use crate::config::RepoSelector;
21	use crate::init::{InitArgs, init_with_symlink};
22	
23	/// Per-process counter so same-nanosecond tempdir collisions yield
24	/// distinct paths when tests run in parallel.
25	static COUNTER: AtomicU64 = AtomicU64::new(0);
26	
27	/// Build a unique tempdir path for a test fixture.
28	///
29	/// Combines a nanosecond timestamp with a per-process atomic counter
30	/// so parallel tests and same-nanosecond collisions both yield
31	/// distinct paths. Prefix is `vc-x1-test-<tag>-<ts>-<n>` so callers
32	/// from different subcommand tests stay discoverable in `$TMPDIR`.
33	pub fn unique_base(tag: &str) -> PathBuf {
34	    let ts = SystemTime::now()
35	        .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
36	        .map(|d| d.as_nanos())
37	        .unwrap_or(0); // OK: clock error → 0 is harmless for unique tempdir naming
38	    let n = COUNTER.fetch_add(1, Ordering::SeqCst);
39	    std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("vc-x1-test-{tag}-{ts}-{n}"))
40	}
41	
42	/// Owned dual-repo fixture with RAII cleanup.
43	///
44	/// Builds a fresh throwaway dual-repo set under a unique tempdir
45	/// by driving `init::init_with_symlink` with a path TARGET and
46	/// `--repo local=<base>` (so config lookup is skipped via the
47	/// resolve_repo short-circuit). `create_symlink=false` suppresses
48	/// the `~/.claude/projects/` side effect. Exposes the two repo
49	/// paths (`work` and `work/.claude`). The tempdir tree is removed
50	/// when the value is dropped, so a panicking test still cleans up
51	/// after itself.
52	pub struct Fixture {
53	    /// Root tempdir that owns both repos and their bare-git remotes.
54	    pub base: PathBuf,
55	    /// Code repo path (`<base>/work`).
56	    pub work: PathBuf,
57	    /// Bot session repo path (`<base>/work/.claude`).
58	    pub claude: PathBuf,
59	}
60	
61	impl Fixture {
62	    /// Build a fresh fixture in a unique tempdir.
63	    pub fn new(tag: &str) -> Self {
64	        Self::new_opts(tag, false, None)
65	    }
66	
67	    /// Build a fresh fixture with optional pending changes and a
68	    /// template seed.
69	    ///
70	    /// - `with_pending` — after init, write a small file into each
71	    ///   repo so `@` carries uncommitted changes (useful for
72	    ///   finalize / push tests).
73	    /// - `use_template` — `CODE[,BOT]` forwarded to init.
74	    pub fn new_opts(tag: &str, with_pending: bool, use_template: Option<String>) -> Self {
75	        let base = unique_base(tag);
76	        // init refuses to reuse an existing project_dir, so `base`
77	        // must not exist yet. `unique_base` already guarantees that.
78	        //
79	        // Path TARGET = `<base>/work` (workspace destination); the
80	        // basename ("work") becomes the repo name. `--repo local=<base>`
81	        // sets the bare-repo parent so the layout mirrors the old
82	        // `--repo-local <base>` + NAME=`work` shape:
83	        //   <base>/work/                  (code repo)
84	        //   <base>/work/.claude/          (bot session repo)
85	        //   <base>/remote-code.git        (code bare origin)
86	        //   <base>/remote-claude.git      (bot bare origin)
87	        let work_path = base.join("work");
88	        let args = InitArgs {
89	            target: work_path.to_string_lossy().into_owned(),
90	            name: None,
91	            account: None,
92	            repo: Some(RepoSelector {
93	                category: "local".to_string(),
94	                value: Some(base.to_string_lossy().into_owned()),
95	            }),
96	            scope: ScopeKind::CodeBot,
97	            private: false,
98	            dry_run: false,
99	            push_retries: 5,
100	            push_retry_delay: 3,
101	            use_template,
102	        };
103	        init_with_symlink(&args, false).expect("build test fixture via init");
104	
105	        let work = base.join("work");
106	        let claude = work.join(".claude");
107	
108	        if with_pending {
109	            write_file(&work.join("TODO.md"), "# TODO\n- first feature\n")
110	                .expect("write pending TODO.md");
111	            write_file(
112	                &claude.join("session-notes.md"),
113	                "# Session notes\n- simulated pending work\n",
114	            )
115	            .expect("write pending session-notes.md");
116	        }
117	
118	        Fixture { base, work, claude }
119	    }
120	
121	    /// Convenience: return both repo paths as a `Vec<PathBuf>`
122	    /// suitable for `sync_repos` (or any other `&[PathBuf]` API).
123	    pub fn repos(&self) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
124	        vec![self.work.clone(), self.claude.clone()]
125	    }
126	}
127	
128	impl Drop for Fixture {
129	    /// Remove the fixture tree on drop. Best-effort; a failure here
130	    /// doesn't fail the test.
131	    fn drop(&mut self) {
132	        let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(&self.base);
133	    }
134	}
135	
```

- POR (`--scope=por`) execution — **zero tests**. `Fixture` only builds dual. The whole POR primitive (which becomes `init_one`) has been validated only by manual dogfooding in `-5`.
- The 58 `#[test]` blocks in `init.rs` are all `plan_init` dispatch + `InitArgs` parsing — none touch the execution path.

The POR gap is the real issue. Refactoring `init_one` without a test pinning its end-state is exactly the fragile case to avoid. Same logic for the bot-side `init_one_create` call that the upgrade paths in sub-tasks 4/5/6 will need — that surface has no test today.

Strong workflow — pinning behavior with tests *upstream* of the refactor in history makes "did the refactor preserve behavior" provable rather than hopeful. If `single-dual-1` rebases green, you have direct evidence; with tests downstream, a passing suite only confirms the test was *written to match the refactor's output*, which is a weaker signal.

**Mechanics I'd run:**

1. `jj bookmark set single-dual-1 -r @ -R .` — preserve the lift (current `@` has `init.rs` edit + `notes/todo.md` edits, all uncommitted; bookmarking captures all of it).
2. `jj new @- -R .` — fresh `@` on `f5ec4d8` (0.41.1-5 close-out, pre-refactor).
3. On the new `@`:

5. `jj rebase -s single-dual-1 -d @ -R .` — bring the lift onto the test commit.

Sorry, I was unclear. To clarify what "bundled" meant:

For sub-steps -6.2 / -6.3 / -6.4, the test suite is **the same set of tests at every commit** — no new tests added. The only check at each step is "tests still green." That's a weaker signal-per-commit than steps that *also* add new tests (like -6.0, where the new tests themselves are the deliverable).

So I was asking: do you want each internal extraction in its own commit (3 separate "tests stay green" gates), or all three extractions in one commit (1 "tests stay green" gate, less push overhead)?

**Either way, the gate is identical:** tests must be green when each commit lands. Tests staying green is non-negotiable — that's not what bundling affects. Bundling only affects how many *commits* sit between "literal lift" and "fully DRY'd init_dual," not whether the test suite is enforced.

3 commits gives bisectability — if a regression sneaks in later, `git bisect` can point to which specific extraction caused it. 1 commit doesn't, but cuts ~10 min of push overhead.

Default to 3 separate commits unless you'd rather skip the bisectability for speed?

> DEVELOPER

separate, and as we refactor I expect new tests will be created.

> AGENT

Understood — separate commits, new tests at each refactor seam. That makes each commit stronger (new test for the new helper, plus the existing suite as the regression net).

  "old_string": "impl Drop for Fixture {\n    /// Remove the fixture tree on drop. Best-effort; a failure here\n    /// doesn't fail the test.\n    fn drop(&mut self) {\n        let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(&self.base);\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "impl Drop for Fixture {\n    /// Remove the fixture tree on drop. Best-effort; a failure here\n    /// doesn't fail the test.\n    fn drop(&mut self) {\n        let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(&self.base);\n    }\n}\n\n/// Owned single-repo (POR) fixture with RAII cleanup.\n///\n/// Sibling of `Fixture` for `--scope=por` flows. Drives\n/// `init::init_with_symlink` with `ScopeKind::Por` and a path\n/// TARGET; `--repo local=<base>` steers the bare origin to\n/// `<base>/remote.git` (vs. dual's `remote-code.git` /\n/// `remote-claude.git`). No `.claude/` peer, no symlink.\n///\n/// Field shape differs from `Fixture` — there is no `claude` peer\n/// path — so it's a distinct type rather than an `Option<PathBuf>`\n/// on `Fixture` (the latter would force every dual-using caller to\n/// `.unwrap()` or pattern-match).\npub struct FixturePor {\n    /// Root tempdir that owns the repo and its bare-git remote.\n    pub base: PathBuf,\n    /// POR repo path (`<base>/work`).\n    pub work: PathBuf,\n}\n\nimpl FixturePor {\n    /// Build a fresh POR fixture in a unique tempdir.\n    pub fn new(tag: &str) -> Self {\n        let base = unique_base(tag);\n        // Path TARGET = `<base>/work`; basename (\"work\") becomes\n        // the repo name. `--repo local=<base>` sets the bare-repo\n        // parent, producing the layout:\n        //   <base>/work/        (POR repo)\n        //   <base>/remote.git   (bare origin)\n        let work_path = base.join(\"work\");\n        let args = InitArgs {\n            target: work_path.to_string_lossy().into_owned(),\n            name: None,\n            account: None,\n            repo: Some(RepoSelector {\n                category: \"local\".to_string(),\n                value: Some(base.to_string_lossy().into_owned()),\n            }),\n            scope: ScopeKind::Por,\n            private: false,\n            dry_run: false,\n            push_retries: 5,\n            push_retry_delay: 3,\n            use_template: None,\n        };\n        init_with_symlink(&args, false).expect(\"build test fixture via init (POR)\");\n\n        let work = base.join(\"work\");\n        FixturePor { base, work }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Drop for FixturePor {\n    /// Remove the fixture tree on drop. Best-effort; a failure here\n    /// doesn't fail the test.\n    fn drop(&mut self) {\n        let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(&self.base);\n    }\n}"
}

writes the APP_ONLY config + .gitignore variants\n    /// — `path = \"/\"` with no `other-repo` field, and `.gitignore`\n    /// has no `/.claude` exclusion.\n    #[test]\n    fn > DEVELOPER

Is FixturePor only for testing, I would think this struct is already available elsewhere or if not it would be useful elsewhere and shouldn't be in "test_helpers.rs", thoughts?

> AGENT

Good question to push on. My read: `FixturePor` is genuinely test-only, not a misplaced general abstraction.

What it actually does:
1. Allocate a unique tempdir (per-process atomic counter for parallel-test collision avoidance).
2. Drive `init_with_symlink` once with `ScopeKind::Por`.
3. RAII cleanup via `Drop` on test exit.

All three are test concerns. Production code never needs "build a throwaway POR and tear it down." `init` runs once, the user keeps the result; nothing in the binary cares about tempdir lifecycle. The struct's two fields (`base`, `work`) are just paths — no domain model worth elevating.

There's no analogous struct in production code today because production doesn't model "a POR repo" as a value — it operates on `PathBuf`s and resolves `Scope` from `.vc-config.toml` per-call. If we *did* introduce a `Workspace` / `PorRepo` domain type, `FixturePor` would become a thin RAII wrapper around it, but that's a separate (bigger) refactor — and CLAUDE.md's "don't introduce abstractions beyond what the task requires" suggests not pre-designing it.

The closest production analogue I can think of is a hypothetical `vc-x1 demo` or `vc-x1 scratch-repo` subcommand that spins up a temp POR for tutorial purposes. That's speculative; if/when it lands, promoting `FixturePor` out of test-helpers would be the natural move.

So I'd keep it in `test_helpers.rs` — but if you're seeing a non-test caller I'm missing, name it and I'll move it. Otherwise the gating stays.

  "new_string": "/// Initial-commit ochid policy used by `init_one_create`.\n///\n/// - `None` — POR: plain `Initial commit` message.\n/// - `Placeholder` — Dual: `Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /none`,\n///   rewritten via cross-ref `jj describe` once both sides have\n///   committed and their chids are known.\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]\nenum OchidStrategy {\n    None,\n    Placeholder,\n}\n\n/// Per-side create primitive: steps 1-5 (mkdir, git/jj init,\n/// configs, optional template, initial commit) on `target`.\n/// Returns the chid of the new initial commit (`jj @-`).\n///\n/// - `side_label` — narration tag (`\"code\"`, `\"bot\"`); appears in\n///   step `info!()` lines.\n/// - `config` / `gitignore` — file contents written at\n///   `target/.vc-config.toml` and `target/.gitignore`. Caller\n///   picks the variant (`VC_CONFIG_APP_ONLY` / `VC_CONFIG_CODE` /\n///   `VC_CONFIG_SESSION`, etc.).\n/// - `template` — optional source dir. When present, copied\n///   recursively (non-hidden only) and any `README.md`'s first\n///   line is rewritten to `# <name>`.\n/// - `name` — repo name used by the README rewrite.\n/// - `ochid_strategy` — initial-commit message policy.\n///\n/// Per-side narration: `info!()` is emitted once per call rather\n/// than once per step across sides — dual orchestrators get two\n/// narration passes (code, then bot) instead of one interleaved.\n#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]\nfn init_one_create(\n    target: &Path,\n    side_label: &str,\n    config: &str,\n    gitignore: &str,\n    template: Option<&Path>,\n    name: &str,\n    ochid_strategy: OchidStrategy,\n) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    info!(\n        \"Step 1: Creating {side_label} directory at {}\",\n        target.display()\n    );\n    if let Some(parent) = target.parent() {\n        mkdir_p(parent)?;\n    }\n    mkdir_p(target)?;\n\n    info!(\"Step 2: Initializing {side_label} repo (git + jj)...\");\n    debug!(\"seed {side_label} dir with an empty git repo\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"init\"], target)?;\n    debug!(\"colocate jj atop the {side_label} git repo\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"], target)?;\n\n    info!(\"Step 3: Writing {side_label} config files...\");\n    write_file(&target.join(\".vc-config.toml\"), config)?;\n    write_file(&target.join(\".gitignore\"), gitignore)?;\n\n    match template {\n        Some(t) => {\n            info!(\n                \"Step 4: Copying {side_label} template: {} -> {}\",\n                t.display(),\n                target.display()\n            );\n            copy_template_recursive(t, target)?;\n            rewrite_readme_first_line(target, name)?;\n        }\n        None => {\n            info!(\"Step 4: (skipped — no {side_label} template)\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    let msg = match ochid_strategy {\n        OchidStrategy::None => \"Initial commit\",\n        OchidStrategy::Placeholder => \"Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /none\",\n    };\n    info!(\"Step 5: Committing {side_label}...\");\n    debug!(\"{side_label} side: jj commit (ochid_strategy={ochid_strategy:?})\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"commit\", \"-m\", msg], target)?;\n\n    let chid = jj_chid(\"@-\", target)?;\n    debug!(\"{side_label} initial chid = {chid}\");\n    Ok(chid)\n}\n\n/// Create-from-empty primitive for `--scope=por` (single repo).\n///\n/// Composes:\n/// - `init_one_create` (steps 1-5, `OchidStrategy::None`).\n/// - Step 7: bookmark + `git clean -xdf`.\n/// - Step 9: provision remote + push.\n/// - Step 10: re-init jj + bookmark tracking.\n///\n/// No cross-reference ochid, no session repo, no symlink.\nfn init_one(\n    args: &InitArgs,\n    plan: &InitPlan,\n    templates: Option<(PathBuf, Option<PathBuf>)>,\n    visibility: &str,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let code_template = templates.as_ref().map(|(c, _)| c.as_path());\n\n    // Steps 1-5 via the per-side primitive.\n    let code_chid = init_one_create(\n        &plan.project_dir,\n        \"code\",\n        VC_CONFIG_APP_ONLY,\n        GITIGNORE_APP_ONLY,\n        code_template,\n        &plan.name,\n        OchidStrategy::None,\n    )?;\n\n    // Step 6: skipped — no cross-reference in single-repo\n    info!(\"Step 6: (skipped — no cross-reference in single-repo)\");\n    let hash = run(\"git\", &[\"rev-parse\", \"HEAD\"], &plan.project_dir)?;\n    debug!(\"code repo: chid={code_chid} hash={hash}\");\n\n    // Step 7: Set bookmark (creates git branch), then remove jj\n    info!(\"Step 7: Setting bookmarks and removing jj...\");\n    debug!(\"place code-side main bookmark at the initial commit\");\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"bookmark\", \"set\", \"main\", \"-r\", \"@-\"],\n        &plan.project_dir,\n    )?;\n    debug!(\"strip jj state from code side (no .claude in single-repo)\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"clean\", \"-xdf\"], &plan.project_dir)?;\n    debug!(\"re-attach code HEAD to main after clean\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"checkout\", \"main\"], &plan.project_dir)?;\n\n    // Step 8: skipped — no session side in single-repo\n    info!(\"Step 8: (skipped — no session side in single-repo)\");\n\n    // Step 9: provision remote and push code\n    run_remote_step(\n        \"Step 9\",\n        \"code\",\n        plan,\n        &plan.code_url,\n        plan.gh_code_slug.as_deref(),\n        plan.code_bare_path.as_deref(),\n        visibility,\n        &plan.project_dir,\n        args,\n    )?;\n\n    // Step 10: Re-initialize jj\n    info!(\"Step 10: Re-initializing jj on the code repo...\");\n    debug!(\"re-colocate jj atop the now-remote-linked code repo\");\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"--quiet\", \"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"],\n        &plan.project_dir,\n    )?;\n    debug!(\"restore code-side main bookmark after re-init\");\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"bookmark\", \"set\", \"main\", \"-r\", \"@-\"],\n        &plan.project_dir,\n    )?;\n    debug!(\"enable jj tracking of code-side main against origin\");\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"bookmark\", \"track\", \"main\", \"--remote=origin\"],\n        &plan.project_dir,\n    )?;\n    crate::common::verify_tracking(&plan.project_dir, \"main\")?;\n    let code_chid_final = jj_chid(\"@-\", &plan.project_dir)?;\n\n    // Step 11: skipped — no .claude symlink in single-repo\n    info!(\"Step 11: (skipped — no .claude symlink in single-repo)\");\n\n    info!(\"\");\n    info!(\"Done! Project created at {}\", plan.project_dir.display());\n    info!(\n        \"  Code repo:    {}  (chid={code_chid_final})\",\n        plan.code_url\n    );\n\n    debug!(\"init: exit\");\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Create-from-empty orchestrator for `--scope=code,bot` (dual).\n///\n/// Composes:\n/// - `init_one_create` for code side (`OchidStrategy::Placeholder`).\n/// - `init_one_create` for session side (`OchidStrategy::Placeholder`).\n/// - Step 6: cross-reference ochids using both returned chids.\n/// - Step 7: bookmark + clean both (code-side clean preserves\n///   `.claude/`).\n/// - Steps 8/9: provision remotes + push session, then code.\n/// - Step 10: re-init + bookmark tracking on both.\n/// - Step 11: install symlink (when `create_symlink`).\nfn init_dual(\n    args: &InitArgs,\n    plan: &InitPlan,\n    templates: Option<(PathBuf, Option<PathBuf>)>,\n    visibility: &str,\n    create_symlink: bool,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n    let session_dir = plan.session_dir.as_ref().unwrap(); // OK: scope=code,bot ⇒ session_dir set\n    #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n    let session_name = plan.session_name.as_ref().unwrap(); // OK: scope=code,bot ⇒ session_name set\n    #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n    let session_url = plan.session_url.as_deref().unwrap(); // OK: scope=code,bot ⇒ session_url set\n\n    let (code_template, bot_template) = match templates.as_ref() {\n        Some((c, b)) => (Some(c.as_path()), b.as_deref()),\n        None => (None, None),\n    };\n\n    // Steps 1-5 per side: code first, then bot.\n    let code_chid = init_one_create(\n        &plan.project_dir,\n        \"code\",\n        VC_CONFIG_CODE,\n        GITIGNORE_CODE,\n        code_template,\n        &plan.name,\n        OchidStrategy::Placeholder,\n    )?;\n    let session_chid = init_one_create(\n        session_dir,\n        \"bot\",\n        VC_CONFIG_SESSION,\n        GITIGNORE_SESSION,\n        bot_template,\n        session_name,\n        OchidStrategy::Placeholder,\n    )?;\n\n    // Step 6: ochid cross-references (rewrites both placeholders)\n    info!(\"Step 6: Setting ochid cross-references...\");\n    let code_desc = format!(\"Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /.claude/{session_chid}\");\n    let session_desc = format!(\"Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /{code_chid}\");\n    debug!(\"code side: rewrite initial commit's ochid to point at session chid\");\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"describe\", \"@-\", \"-m\", &code_desc],\n        &plan.project_dir,\n    )?;\n    debug!(\"session side: rewrite initial commit's ochid to point at code chid\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"describe\", \"@-\", \"-m\", &session_desc], session_dir)?;\n\n    debug!(\"surface post-describe git hashes for the debug log\");\n    let hash = run(\"git\", &[\"rev-parse\", \"HEAD\"], &plan.project_dir)?;\n    debug!(\"code repo: chid={code_chid} hash={hash}\");\n    let hash = run(\"git\", &[\"rev-parse\", \"HEAD\"], session_dir)?;\n    debug!(\".claude:   chid={session_chid} hash={hash}\");\n\n    // Step 7: Set bookmarks, remove jj (code-side clean preserves .claude/)\n    info!(\"Step 7: Setting bookmarks and removing jj...\");\n    debug!(\"place code-side main bookmark at the initial commit\");\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"bookmark\", \"set\", \"main\", \"-r\", \"@-\"],\n        &plan.project_dir,\n    )?;\n    debug!(\"place session-side main bookmark at the initial commit\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"bookmark\", \"set\", \"main\", \"-r\", \"@-\"], session_dir)?;\n    debug!(\"strip jj state from session side before its git push\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"clean\", \"-xdf\"], session_dir)?;\n    debug!(\"strip jj state from code side, preserving nested .claude/\");\n    run(\n        \"git\",\n        &[\"clean\", \"-xdf\", \"--exclude\", \".claude\"],\n        &plan.project_dir,\n    )?;\n    debug!(\"re-attach session HEAD to main after clean\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"checkout\", \"main\"], session_dir)?;\n    debug!(\"re-attach code HEAD to main after clean\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"checkout\", \"main\"], &plan.project_dir)?;\n\n    // Steps 8/9: provision remotes and push.\n    run_remote_step(\n        \"Step 8\",\n        \"session\",\n        plan,\n        session_url,\n        plan.gh_session_slug.as_deref(),\n        plan.session_bare_path.as_deref(),\n        visibility,\n        session_dir,\n        args,\n    )?;\n    run_remote_step(\n        \"Step 9\",\n        \"code\",\n        plan,\n        &plan.code_url,\n        plan.gh_code_slug.as_deref(),\n        plan.code_bare_path.as_deref(),\n        visibility,\n        &plan.project_dir,\n        args,\n    )?;\n\n    // Step 10: Re-initialize jj on both repos\n    info!(\"Step 10: Re-initializing jj on both repos...\");\n    debug!(\"re-colocate jj atop the now-remote-linked code repo\");\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"--quiet\", \"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"],\n        &plan.project_dir,\n    )?;\n    debug!(\"restore code-side main bookmark after re-init\");\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"bookmark\", \"set\", \"main\", \"-r\", \"@-\"],\n        &plan.project_dir,\n    )?;\n    debug!(\"enable jj tracking of code-side main against origin\");\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"bookmark\", \"track\", \"main\", \"--remote=origin\"],\n        &plan.project_dir,\n    )?;\n    crate::common::verify_tracking(&plan.project_dir, \"main\")?;\n    let code_chid_final = jj_chid(\"@-\", &plan.project_dir)?;\n\n    debug!(\"re-colocate jj atop the now-remote-linked session repo\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"--quiet\", \"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"], session_dir)?;\n    debug!(\"restore session-side main bookmark after re-init\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"bookmark\", \"set\", \"main\", \"-r\", \"@-\"], session_dir)?;\n    debug!(\"enable jj tracking of session-side main against origin\");\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"bookmark\", \"track\", \"main\", \"--remote=origin\"],\n        session_dir,\n    )?;\n    crate::common::verify_tracking(session_dir, \"main\")?;\n    let session_chid_final = jj_chid(\"@-\", session_dir)?;\n\n    // Step 11: Create Claude Code symlink (opt-out for tests)\n    let sl_opt = if create_symlink {\n        info!(\"Step 11: Creating Claude Code symlink...\");\n        Some(symlink::install(&plan.project_dir)?)\n    } else {\n        info!(\"Step 11: (skipped — symlink disabled by caller)\");\n        None\n    };\n\n    info!(\"\");\n    info!(\"Done! Project created at {}\", plan.project_dir.display());\n    info!(\n        \"  Code repo:    {}  (chid={code_chid_final})\",\n        plan.code_url\n    );\n    info!(\"  Session repo: {session_url}  (chid={session_chid_final})\");\n    if let Some(sl) = sl_opt.as_ref() {\n        info!(\n            \"  Symlink:      {} -> {}\",\n            sl.symlink_path.display(),\n            sl.abs_target.display()\n        );\n    }\n\n    debug!(\"init: exit\");\n    Ok(())\n}"
}

> AGENT

Gates green: fmt clean (one auto-format applied), clippy silent, **322 tests pass** (320 existing + 2 new init_one_create direct tests).

**-6.2 changes summary:**

`src/init.rs`:
- Added `enum OchidStrategy { None, Placeholder }` — encodes the initial-commit message policy.
- Added `init_one_create(target, side_label, config, gitignore, template, name, ochid_strategy) -> Result<chid>` — the per-side primitive for steps 1-5 (mkdir → git/jj init → configs → optional template → initial commit). Per-side `info!()` narration (e.g. `Step 1: Creating code directory at /tmp/...`) so dual mode emits two passes.
- `init_one` collapsed: steps 1-5 → one `init_one_create(... OchidStrategy::None)` call. Body shrank ~40 lines.
- `init_dual` collapsed: steps 1-5 across both sides → two `init_one_create(... OchidStrategy::Placeholder)` calls (code, then bot). Body shrank ~70 lines.
- Two new direct unit tests: `init_one_create_strategy_none_writes_plain_commit` (POR shape, plain "Initial commit") and `init_one_create_strategy_placeholder_writes_ochid_none` (CODE shape, `ochid: /none` placeholder).

`Cargo.toml`: 0.41.1-6.1 → 0.41.1-6.2.

**Behavior change to flag:** user-visible step output for dual mode shifts. Pre-6.2: one pass with interleaved log lines per step (`Step 1: Creating project directories...` once, both sides mkdir, `Step 2: ...` once, etc.). Post-6.2: two narration passes (all five steps for code, then all five for bot). Same actions in same order; just the `info!()` ordering changes. Since user-visible logging isn't covered by tests and the underlying sequence is unchanged, this is a cosmetic-only delta.

Stopping for review. On approval I'll add the chores-08.md -6.2 subsection.

> DEVELOPER

Would create_a_repo or create_repo be better

> AGENT

Yes, `create_repo` is better. The `init_one_` prefix was carrying weight that the call site already provides (callers are `init_one` / `init_dual`); dropping it leaves a concise verb-noun.

`create_a_repo` (with the article) reads like prose; Rust convention is to omit articles. Stick with `create_repo`.

**Bonus:** it pairs cleanly with what's coming in -6.3:
- `create_repo` (this one): steps 1-5 (local state through initial commit).
- `finalize_repo` (-6.3): steps 7, 9, 10 (bookmark, clean, push, re-init).

Symmetric, predictable. Reads as a lifecycle pair.

**One thing to flag:** there's already a "create" verb in init's vocabulary — `Provisioner::GhCreate` and `gh repo create` create the *remote*. `create_repo` would mean local creation. The doc comment makes this clear ("creates `target`'s local state through initial commit"), and the orchestrators handle remote creation via `run_remote_step`. Mild ambiguity but I think the call sites make it obvious.

Want me to rename now (before -6.2 commits)? It's a 5-line change: rename the function, update both call sites in `init_one` / `init_dual`, update both test names. The doc comment updates with it.

> DEVELOPER

yes, and init_dual -> create_dual ?

> AGENT

Yes — `init_dual → create_dual`. For symmetry I'd also suggest `init_one → create_one` since they're a pair (single vs dual repo orchestrators). Same renaming pass:

- `init_one_create` → `create_repo` (per-side local primitive).
- `init_one` → `create_one` (POR orchestrator).
- `init_dual` → `create_dual` (dual orchestrator).

The outer `init` and `init_with_symlink` entry points stay as-is (they still represent the `vc-x1 init` command). The internal renames just emphasize that these are *creation* helpers, not new init commands.

One alternative worth flagging: `init_one` → `create_por` instead of `create_one`. Pros: connects directly to the user-facing `--scope=por` keyword. Cons: asymmetric with `create_dual` (which doesn't echo the `code,bot` keyword). I lean `create_one` for the symmetric pair, but `create_por` is defensible.

Which do you prefer for the POR orchestrator: `create_one` or `create_por`?

> DEVELOPER

I prefer create_repo and let's make to more general, change side_label info_label and make config and gitignore Option<&str> and finally move it to repo_utils.rs, thoughts?

> AGENT

All three changes are improvements. Reasoning:

**`info_label` over `side_label`:** drops the "side" connotation that belongs to the dual-orchestrator's vocabulary. A general utility's label is just for narration. ✓

**`Option<&str>` for config/gitignore:** lets callers opt out of writing those files. Real use cases:
- Upgrade paths in -6.7/-6.8 where `.vc-config.toml` exists already and the helper shouldn't clobber.
- A future `vc-x1 scratch-repo` or test scratch fixtures that just want a bare git+jj repo with an initial commit.
- The two existing call sites (init_one/init_dual) just pass `Some(VC_CONFIG_*)` and `Some(GITIGNORE_*)` — no behavior change.

I'd add one test: `with_no_config_files_writes_neither` (both None, assert files absent) — locks in the new None semantics.

**New `src/repo_utils.rs` module:** clean split. `repo_url.rs` is URL/name shaping; `repo_utils.rs` is repo creation/lifecycle. `create_repo` and `OchidStrategy` (becoming `pub`) belong there. The two unit tests move with it (renamed to drop the `init_one_` prefix).

**Two questions before I execute:**

1. **Orchestrators (`init_one` / `init_dual`)**: rename to `create_one` / `create_dual` for symmetry with the helper, or leave them? I lean toward leaving them as `init_one` / `init_dual` for now — they're internal to `init.rs` and "init_*" still describes their orchestration role (steps 7-11 are init-cycle work, not generic creation). The helper is the truly-generic piece that earns the move.

2. **Module name**: you wrote `repo_utils.rs`. Confirm? Alternative I considered: `repo_create.rs` (more focused; would house just `create_repo` + `OchidStrategy`). `repo_utils.rs` is broader and would also house `finalize_repo` (-6.3) and `cross_ref_ochids` (-6.4) when they extract.

Proceeding plan: yes to your three changes, leave orchestrator names alone, use `repo_utils.rs`. OK?

> DEVELOPER

1. rename and possibly create_one isn't needed
2. I'm thinking they'll be other fns add so repo_utils.rs, later if it's the only item in the module we'll rename

> AGENT

Confirmed: rename `init_dual → create_dual`; leave `init_one` (likely subsumes into a 2-line composition by -6.4). Plan executing now.

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/repo_utils.rs",
  "content": "//! Repo lifecycle helpers — creation, finalization, cross-references.\n//!\n//! Sibling of `repo_url` (URL/name shaping); this module hosts\n//! the local-state mechanics that don't depend on URL form. As of\n//! 0.41.1-6.2 it carries `create_repo` (steps 1-5 of the init\n//! lifecycle) and the `OchidStrategy` policy enum; `finalize_repo`\n//! and `cross_ref_ochids` are scheduled to land in -6.3 and -6.4.\n\nuse std::path::Path;\n\nuse log::{debug, info};\n\nuse crate::common::{mkdir_p, run, write_file};\nuse crate::init::{copy_template_recursive, jj_chid_atminus, rewrite_readme_first_line};\n\n/// Initial-commit ochid policy used by `create_repo`.\n///\n/// - `None` — POR: plain `Initial commit` message.\n/// - `Placeholder` — Dual: `Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /none`,\n///   rewritten via cross-ref `jj describe` once both sides have\n///   committed and their chids are known.\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]\npub enum OchidStrategy {\n    None,\n    Placeholder,\n}\n\n/// Per-side create primitive: steps 1-5 (mkdir, git/jj init,\n/// configs, optional template, initial commit) on `target`.\n/// Returns the chid of the new initial commit (`jj @-`).\n///\n/// - `info_label` — narration tag (`\"code\"`, `\"bot\"`, `\"scratch\"`,\n///   etc.); appears in `info!()` lines.\n/// - `config` / `gitignore` — optional file contents. `Some(s)`\n///   writes `target/.vc-config.toml` (or `.gitignore`); `None`\n///   skips the write entirely. Useful for upgrade paths that\n///   leave config files in place, or for scratch repos that want\n///   a bare git+jj init without project-specific files.\n/// - `template` — optional source dir. When present, copied\n///   recursively (non-hidden only) and any `README.md`'s first\n///   line is rewritten to `# <name>`.\n/// - `name` — repo name used by the README rewrite.\n/// - `ochid_strategy` — initial-commit message policy.\n///\n/// Per-side narration: `info!()` is emitted once per call rather\n/// than once per step across sides — dual orchestrators get two\n/// narration passes (code, then bot) instead of one interleaved.\n#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]\npub fn create_repo(\n    target: &Path,\n    info_label: &str,\n    config: Option<&str>,\n    gitignore: Option<&str>,\n    template: Option<&Path>,\n    name: &str,\n    ochid_strategy: OchidStrategy,\n) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    info!(\n        \"Step 1: Creating {info_label} directory at {}\",\n        target.display()\n    );\n    if let Some(parent) = target.parent() {\n        mkdir_p(parent)?;\n    }\n    mkdir_p(target)?;\n\n    info!(\"Step 2: Initializing {info_label} repo (git + jj)...\");\n    debug!(\"seed {info_label} dir with an empty git repo\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"init\"], target)?;\n    debug!(\"colocate jj atop the {info_label} git repo\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"], target)?;\n\n    match (config, gitignore) {\n        (None, None) => {\n            info!(\"Step 3: (skipped — no {info_label} config files requested)\");\n        }\n        _ => {\n            info!(\"Step 3: Writing {info_label} config files...\");\n            if let Some(c) = config {\n                write_file(&target.join(\".vc-config.toml\"), c)?;\n            }\n            if let Some(g) = gitignore {\n                write_file(&target.join(\".gitignore\"), g)?;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    match template {\n        Some(t) => {\n            info!(\n                \"Step 4: Copying {info_label} template: {} -> {}\",\n                t.display(),\n                target.display()\n            );\n            copy_template_recursive(t, target)?;\n            rewrite_readme_first_line(target, name)?;\n        }\n        None => {\n            info!(\"Step 4: (skipped — no {info_label} template)\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    let msg = match ochid_strategy {\n        OchidStrategy::None => \"Initial commit\",\n        OchidStrategy::Placeholder => \"Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /none\",\n    };\n    info!(\"Step 5: Committing {info_label}...\");\n    debug!(\"{info_label} side: jj commit (ochid_strategy={ochid_strategy:?})\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"commit\", \"-m\", msg], target)?;\n\n    let chid = jj_chid_atminus(target)?;\n    debug!(\"{info_label} initial chid = {chid}\");\n    Ok(chid)\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    /// `OchidStrategy::None` produces a plain `Initial commit`\n    /// message (POR-shape).\n    #[test]\n    fn strategy_none_writes_plain_commit() {\n        let base = crate::test_helpers::unique_base(\"create-repo-strategy-none\");\n        let target = base.join(\"work\");\n        std::fs::create_dir_all(&base).expect(\"mkdir base\");\n\n        let chid = create_repo(\n            &target,\n            \"code\",\n            Some(crate::init::VC_CONFIG_APP_ONLY),\n            Some(crate::init::GITIGNORE_APP_ONLY),\n            None,\n            \"scratch\",\n            OchidStrategy::None,\n        )\n        .expect(\"create_repo with strategy None\");\n\n        assert!(!chid.is_empty(), \"chid returned\");\n        assert!(target.join(\".jj\").exists(), \"jj initialized\");\n        assert!(target.join(\".git\").exists(), \"git initialized\");\n\n        let cfg =\n            std::fs::read_to_string(target.join(\".vc-config.toml\")).expect(\"read config\");\n        assert!(cfg.contains(\"path = \\\"/\\\"\"));\n        assert!(!cfg.contains(\"other-repo\"));\n\n        let log = run(\"git\", &[\"log\", \"-1\", \"--format=%B\"], &target).expect(\"git log\");\n        assert_eq!(\n            log.trim(),\n            \"Initial commit\",\n            \"OchidStrategy::None uses a plain message\"\n        );\n\n        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&base);\n    }\n\n    /// `OchidStrategy::Placeholder` writes the `ochid: /none`\n    /// placeholder in the initial commit message — what dual mode\n    /// lands before the cross-ref rewrite in step 6.\n    #[test]\n    fn strategy_placeholder_writes_ochid_none() {\n        let base = crate::test_helpers::unique_base(\"create-repo-strategy-placeholder\");\n        let target = base.join(\"work\");\n        std::fs::create_dir_all(&base).expect(\"mkdir base\");\n\n        let _chid = create_repo(\n            &target,\n            \"code\",\n            Some(crate::init::VC_CONFIG_CODE),\n            Some(crate::init::GITIGNORE_CODE),\n            None,\n            \"scratch\",\n            OchidStrategy::Placeholder,\n        )\n        .expect(\"create_repo with strategy Placeholder\");\n\n        let cfg =\n            std::fs::read_to_string(target.join(\".vc-config.toml\")).expect(\"read config\");\n        assert!(cfg.contains(\"other-repo = \\\".claude\\\"\"));\n\n        let log = run(\"git\", &[\"log\", \"-1\", \"--format=%B\"], &target).expect(\"git log\");\n        assert!(log.contains(\"Initial commit\"));\n        assert!(\n            log.contains(\"ochid: /none\"),\n            \"OchidStrategy::Placeholder leaves a placeholder ochid trailer\"\n        );\n\n        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&base);\n    }\n\n    /// Both `config` and `gitignore` set to `None` skips the file\n    /// writes entirely — no `.vc-config.toml`, no `.gitignore` in\n    /// the resulting tree (only the `.jj/` and `.git/` from init).\n    #[test]\n    fn no_config_or_gitignore_writes_neither_file() {\n        let base = crate::test_helpers::unique_base(\"create-repo-no-files\");\n        let target = base.join(\"work\");\n        std::fs::create_dir_all(&base).expect(\"mkdir base\");\n\n        let _chid = create_repo(\n            &target,\n            \"scratch\",\n            None,\n            None,\n            None,\n            \"scratch\",\n            OchidStrategy::None,\n        )\n        .expect(\"create_repo with no config files\");\n\n        assert!(target.join(\".jj\").exists(), \"jj still initialized\");\n        assert!(target.join(\".git\").exists(), \"git still initialized\");\n        assert!(\n            !target.join(\".vc-config.toml\").exists(),\n            \"no .vc-config.toml when config=None\"\n        );\n        assert!(\n            !target.join(\".gitignore\").exists(),\n            \"no .gitignore when gitignore=None\"\n        );\n\n        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&base);\n    }\n}\n"
}

Good question — let me analyze before continuing.

`repo_url.rs` content: `Target` enum + `parse_target` + `derive_name` + `resolve_url` + `derive_session_url`. **Concern:** classifying and shaping target strings (URLs, paths, owner/name shorthand). Fairly self-contained, ~390 lines including 31 tests.

`repo_utils.rs` content (so far): `OchidStrategy` + `create_repo`, planned to grow with `finalize_repo` + `cross_ref_ochids`. **Concern:** local repo lifecycle mechanics (subprocess calls to git/jj, file writes).

**They're cousin concerns at different abstraction layers.** Target shaping is pure (no I/O); lifecycle is impure (subprocess + filesystem). I'd recommend keeping them separate because:
1. Repo-URL helpers are reusable in contexts that don't create repos (e.g., `vc-x1 chid`, `validate-desc`, etc., which work on existing repos).
2. Lifecycle helpers always operate on a `&Path` to a real dir; pure URL parsing doesn't.
3. Both are `repo_` prefixed so they sort together in file listings — discoverability is fine without merging.

That said, if you want consolidation, two clean options:

**A. Merge into `repo_utils.rs`** — single module, both concerns coexist. Costs ~30 lines of doc structure to make the two areas navigable (subsection headers).

**B. Sub-module structure** — `src/repo/url.rs` + `src/repo/utils.rs` + `src/repo/mod.rs`. Heavier; only worth it if a third repo-related module shows up.

I lean toward keeping them separate (option C: status quo), but defer to you. If you want them merged, A is simpler than B.

(Currently paused mid-edit on init.rs; want to know your direction before I continue, since the import path changes either way.)

You're right — `repo_url.rs` has the parameter `repo: &str` in `derive_name` (line 118) and `resolve_url` (line 132), but those parameters take URL/target strings, not "repo" objects. The naming is misleading.

**Findings from grep:**
- `repo_url.rs:118` — `pub fn derive_name(repo: &str)` → param should be `url`.
- `repo_url.rs:132` — `pub fn resolve_url(repo: &str)` → param should be `url`.
- Module name `repo_url.rs` itself is misleading — the content is target/URL parsing, not repo-specific.
- A few doc comment phrases like "repo URL" / "repo created at" that should drop "repo" where the URL is the actual subject.

**Module name candidates:**
- `url.rs` — short, idiomatic Rust; the dominant concept (3/4 functions return or operate on URLs).
- `url_parsing.rs` — your suggestion; narrows to "parsing" but `derive_*` and `resolve_*` aren't really parsing.
- `target.rs` — pivots on the central `Target` enum.

I lean `url.rs` — the cleanest single-noun form, and the `Target` enum is just the parser's output type.

**What stays "repo":**
- `--repo <cat>[=<val>]` CLI flag and `RepoSelector` struct — these select a *repo target*, conceptually correct (the value resolves to where a repo lives).
- `info!("Code repo: ...")` / `gh repo create` text — referring to actual repos at runtime.
- `bare repo`, `GitHub repo` in error messages — same.

**Scoping question for -6.2:** we haven't committed yet, so we can either:

**A. Bundle into -6.2** — one commit covers (a) create_repo extraction, (b) `init_dual → create_dual`, (c) `repo_url → url` rename + `repo` params → `url`. Coherent "module structure & naming" theme. Bigger diff (touches `init.rs`, `clone.rs`, `main.rs`, both module files), but in a tightly-scoped way.

**B. Split** — finish -6.2 as planned (create_repo + create_dual), commit. Then a separate -6.2.5 for the `repo_url → url` rename. Smaller, focused commits.

I'd lean **A (bundle)**: the rename is mechanical (mostly imports), and the "naming sweep" reads better as one commit. The diff stays scannable because the renames are find-and-replace.
