1	//! Parsing and derivation helpers for repository URLs and targets.
2	//!
3	//! Single source of truth for the positional `<TARGET>` forms
4	//! that `init` and `clone` accept (URL, owner/name shorthand,
5	//! path) and for the URL-derivation helpers shared between them.
6	//!
7	//! Lifted from `clone.rs` / `init.rs` in 0.41.1-1; consumers
8	//! migrate to `parse_target` in 0.41.1-2 (clone) and 0.41.1-3
9	//! (init).
10	
11	use std::path::PathBuf;
12	
13	/// A parsed positional `<TARGET>` argument to `init` or `clone`.
14	///
15	/// - `Url` — full git URL (`scheme://...` or SSH `user@host:path`).
16	/// - `OwnerName(owner, name)` — `owner/name` shorthand;
17	///   resolves to `git@github.com:owner/name.git`.
18	/// - `Path` — local path with explicit prefix
19	///   (`./`, `../`, `/`, `~/`, or bare `~`). Path text is preserved
20	///   literally; tilde expansion is the consumer's responsibility.
21	/// - `BareName` — a bare alphanumeric (no `/`, `:`, or path
22	///   prefix). Init resolves it via the user-config remote chain;
23	///   clone errors on it (no config-driven default).
24	#[derive(Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Clone)]
25	pub enum Target {
26	    Url(String),
27	    OwnerName(String, String),
28	    Path(PathBuf),
29	    BareName(String),
30	}
31	
32	/// Parse a positional `<TARGET>` argument into one of the four
33	/// `Target` variants.
34	///
35	/// Detection order (path forms first, then URL, then shorthand,
36	/// then bare NAME):
37	///
38	/// - Path forms: bare `.`, `..`, or `~`; or starts with `./`,
39	///   `../`, `/`, or `~/`. `.` and `..` are POSIX cwd/parent and
40	///   are unambiguous; the consumer resolves them to a real
41	///   directory name via `canonicalize` + `file_name`.
42	/// - URL: contains `://`, or SSH-style `user@host:path` (an `@`
43	///   followed somewhere by `:`).
44	/// - `owner/name` shorthand: exactly one `/`, both sides non-empty,
45	///   no path or URL indicators.
46	/// - Bare NAME: no `/`, no `:`, no URL/path indicators. Init
47	///   resolves it via the user-config remote chain (repo created
48	///   at `cwd/<NAME>`); clone errors on it (no config).
49	///
50	/// Errors only on empty input or syntactic garbage that fits
51	/// none of the above (e.g. `owner/name/extra`).
52	pub fn parse_target(s: &str) -> Result<Target, String> {
53	    if s.is_empty() {
54	        return Err("empty target".into());
55	    }
56	
57	    if s == "."
58	        || s == ".."
59	        || s == "~"
60	        || s.starts_with("./")
61	        || s.starts_with("../")
62	        || s.starts_with('/')
63	        || s.starts_with("~/")
64	    {
65	        return Ok(Target::Path(PathBuf::from(s)));
66	    }
67	
68	    if s.contains("://") {
69	        return Ok(Target::Url(s.to_string()));
70	    }
71	    if let Some(at) = s.find('@')
72	        && s[at + 1..].contains(':')
73	    {
74	        return Ok(Target::Url(s.to_string()));
75	    }
76	
77	    if s.matches('/').count() == 1
78	        && let Some((owner, name)) = s.split_once('/')
79	        && !owner.is_empty()
80	        && !name.is_empty()
81	        && !owner.contains(':')
82	        && !name.contains(':')
83	    {
84	        return Ok(Target::OwnerName(owner.to_string(), name.to_string()));
85	    }
86	
87	    // Bare NAME: no slash, no colon, no URL pattern. Init expands
88	    // via config; clone rejects.
89	    if !s.contains('/') && !s.contains(':') {
90	        return Ok(Target::BareName(s.to_string()));
91	    }
92	
93	    // Catch-all. If it looks like an SSH scp-like form missing the
94	    // `git@` prefix (host:owner/name), suggest the canonical form
95	    // — easy mistake to make and the resulting "did you mean…?"
96	    // is concrete enough to fix with one re-type.
97	    if let Some(colon) = s.find(':')
98	        && colon > 0
99	        && !s[..colon].contains('/')
100	        && s[colon + 1..].contains('/')
101	        && !s.contains('@')
102	    {
103	        return Err(format!(
104	            "'{s}' is not a recognized target — looks like an SSH URL missing the 'git@' prefix; did you mean 'git@{s}'?"
105	        ));
106	    }
107	
108	    Err(format!(
109	        "'{s}' is not a recognized target — expected URL, owner/name shorthand, path prefix (./X, ../X, /X, ~/X, ~), or bare NAME"
110	    ))
111	}
112	
113	/// Derive the project name from a repo URL or `owner/name` shorthand.
114	///
115	/// - Strips trailing `.git`.
116	/// - Returns the last segment after the rightmost `/` or `:`.
117	/// - Errors when the resulting name would be empty.
118	pub fn derive_name(repo: &str) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
119	    let stem = repo.strip_suffix(".git").unwrap_or(repo); // OK: .git suffix is optional
120	    let last = stem.rsplit(['/', ':']).next().unwrap_or(""); // OK: rsplit always yields at least one element
121	    if last.is_empty() {
122	        return Err(format!("cannot derive project name from '{repo}'").into());
123	    }
124	    Ok(last.to_string())
125	}
126	
127	/// Resolve a repo argument to a git clone URL.
128	///
129	/// - `owner/name` (single `/`, no `:` or scheme) →
130	///   `git@github.com:owner/name.git`.
131	/// - Anything else is passed through as-is (already a URL).
132	pub fn resolve_url(repo: &str) -> String {
133	    if repo.contains("://") || repo.contains('@') {
134	        return repo.to_string();
135	    }
136	    if repo.matches('/').count() == 1 && !repo.contains(':') {
137	        return format!("git@github.com:{repo}.git");
138	    }
139	    repo.to_string()
140	}
141	
142	/// Derive the session repo URL from a code repo URL.
143	///
144	/// - With trailing `.git`: insert `.claude` before it
145	///   (`foo.git` → `foo.claude.git`).
146	/// - Without `.git`: append `.claude` (`foo` → `foo.claude`).
147	pub fn derive_session_url(code_url: &str) -> String {
148	    match code_url.strip_suffix(".git") {
149	        Some(stem) => format!("{stem}.claude.git"),
150	        None => format!("{code_url}.claude"),
151	    }
152	}

1168	/// Per-side create primitive: steps 1-5 (mkdir, git/jj init,
1169	/// configs, optional template, initial commit) on `target`.
1170	/// Returns the chid of the new initial commit (`jj @-`).

1172	/// - `side_label` — narration tag (`"code"`, `"bot"`); appears in

1174	/// - `config` / `gitignore` — file contents written at
1175	///   `target/.vc-config.toml` and `target/.gitignore`. Caller
1176	///   picks the variant (`VC_CONFIG_APP_ONLY` / `VC_CONFIG_CODE` /
1177	///   `VC_CONFIG_SESSION`, etc.).
1178	/// - `template` — optional source dir. When present, copied
1179	///   recursively (non-hidden only) and any `README.md`'s first
1180	///   line is rewritten to `# <name>`.
1181	/// - `name` — repo name used by the README rewrite.
1182	/// - `ochid_strategy` — initial-commit message policy.
1183	///
1184	/// Per-side narration: `info!()` is emitted once per call rather
1185	/// than once per step across sides — dual orchestrators get two
1186	/// narration passes (code, then bot) instead of one interleaved.
1187	#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
1188	fn init_one_create(
1189	    target: &Path,
1190	    side_label: &str,
1191	    config: &str,
1192	    gitignore: &str,
1193	    template: Option<&Path>,
1194	    name: &str,
1195	    ochid_strategy: OchidStrategy,
1196	) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
1197	    info!(
1198	        "Step 1: Creating {side_label} directory at {}",
1199	        target.display()
1200	    );
1201	    if let Some(parent) = target.parent() {
1202	        mkdir_p(parent)?;
1203	    }
1204	    mkdir_p(target)?;
1205	
1206	    info!("Step 2: Initializing {side_label} repo (git + jj)...");
1207	    debug!("seed {side_label} dir with an empty git repo");
1208	    run("git", &["init"], target)?;
1209	    debug!("colocate jj atop the {side_label} git repo");
1210	    run("jj", &["git", "init", "--colocate"], target)?;
1211	
1212	    info!("Step 3: Writing {side_label} config files...");
1213	    write_file(&target.join(".vc-config.toml"), config)?;
1214	    write_file(&target.join(".gitignore"), gitignore)?;
1215	
1216	    match template {
1217	        Some(t) => {
1218	            info!(
1219	                "Step 4: Copying {side_label} template: {} -> {}",
1220	                t.display(),
1221	                target.display()
1222	            );
1223	            copy_template_recursive(t, target)?;
1224	            rewrite_readme_first_line(target, name)?;
1225	        }
1226	        None => {
1227	            info!("Step 4: (skipped — no {side_label} template)");
1228	        }
1229	    }
1230	
1231	    let msg = match ochid_strategy {
1232	        OchidStrategy::None => "Initial commit",
1233	        OchidStrategy::Placeholder => "Initial commit\n\nochid: /none",
1234	    };
1235	    info!("Step 5: Committing {side_label}...");
1236	    debug!("{side_label} side: jj commit (ochid_strategy={ochid_strategy:?})");
1237	    run("jj", &["commit", "-m", msg], target)?;
1238	
1239	    let chid = jj_chid("@-", target)?;
1240	    debug!("{side_label} initial chid = {chid}");
1241	    Ok(chid)
1242	}

Caller\n///   picks the variant (`VC_CONFIG_APP_ONLY` / `VC_CONFIG_CODE` /\n///   `VC_CONFIG_SESSION`, etc.).\n/// - `template` — optional source dir. When present, copied\n///   recursively (non-hidden only) and any `README.md`'s first\n///   line is rewritten to `# <name>`.\n/// - `name` — repo name used by the README rewrite.\n/// - `ochid_strategy` — initial-commit message policy.\n///\n/// Per-side narration: `info!()` is emitted once per call rather\n/// than once per step across sides — dual orchestrators get two\n/// narration passes (code, then bot) instead of one interleaved.\n#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]\nfn init_one_create(\n    target: &Path,\n    side_label: &str,\n    config: &str,\n    gitignore: &str,\n    template: Option<&Path>,\n    name: &str,\n    ochid_strategy: OchidStrategy,\n) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    info!(\n        \"Step 1: Creating {side_label} directory at {}\",\n        target.display()\n    );\n    if let Some(parent) = target.parent() {\n        mkdir_p(parent)?;\n    }\n    mkdir_p(target)?;\n\n    info!(\"Step 2: Initializing {side_label} repo (git + jj)...\");\n    debug!(\"seed {side_label} dir with an empty git repo\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"init\"], target)?;\n    debug!(\"colocate jj atop the {side_label} git step 6.\n    #[test]\n    fn init_one_create_strategy_placeholder_writes_ochid_none() {\n        let base = crate::test_helpers::unique_base(\"init-one-create-placeholder\");\n        let target = base.join(\"work\");\n        std::fs::create_dir_all(&base).expect(\"mkdir base\");\n\n        let _chid = init_one_create(\n            &target,\n            \"code\",\n            VC_CONFIG_CODE,\n            GITIGNORE_CODE,\n            None,\n            \"scratch\",\n            OchidStrategy::Placeholder,\n        )\n        .expect(\"init_one_create with strategy Placeholder\");\n\n        let cfg = std::fs::read_to_string(target.join(\".vc-config.toml\")).expect(\"read config\");\n        assert!(cfg.contains(\"other-repo = \\\".claude\\\"\"));\n\n        let log = run(\"git\", &[\"log\", \"-1\", \"--format=%B\"], &target).expect(\"git log\");\n        assert!(log.contains(\"Initial commit\"));\n        assert!(\n            log.contains(\"ochid: /none\"),\n            \"OchidStrategy::Placeholder leaves a placeholder ochid trailer\"\n        );\n\n        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&base);\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "    /// POR fixture has a `main` bookmark tracking `origin/main` —\n    /// pins step 10 (re-init jj + bookmark track) ran successfully.\n    #[test]\n    fn por_fixture_main_tracks_origin() {\n        let fx = crate::test_helpers::FixturePor::new(\"por-tracking\");\n\n        crate::common::verify_tracking(&fx.work, \"main\")\n            .expect(\"main should track origin/main after init step 10\");\n    }\n}"

> DEVELOPER

looking at create_repo, init_one, init_dual, I think we can move steps 7-10 into create repo and move step 6 into init_dual only and this should reduce the size of init_one maybe to just one call to create_repo and hence remove it and then renumnber to initial steps 1..9. Thoughts, no code?

> AGENT

Good direction. The constraint to think through carefully: the cross-ref ochid is bidirectional (each side's commit message names the *other* side's chid), so it has to happen after both step-5 commits land. Where it slots in the lifecycle determines whether we can avoid force-push.

**Path A — Literal: create_repo bundles 1-9, init_dual cross-refs after both.** Each `create_repo` call does the full lifecycle including push. Then init_dual amends both initial commits with cross-ref ochids (jj's changeIDs are stable across `describe`, so the *chids* don't shift) and force-pushes. Cost: 2 extra remote write events per dual init (one regular push + one force-push per side). Adds `--force` semantics to the push step. create_repo's signature grows — needs `plan`, `args`, `remote_url`, `gh_slug`, `bare_path`, `visibility`, etc. (currently leaned on by `run_remote_step`'s 9 params).

**Path B — Split: create_repo does 1-5, finalize_repo does 7-9, init_dual cross-refs between them.** The original -6.3 plan, slightly recast. POR's dispatcher: `create_repo + finalize_repo` (two calls). Dual: `create_repo(code, Placeholder) + create_repo(bot, Placeholder) + cross_ref + finalize_repo(code, exclude=.claude) + finalize_repo(bot) + symlink`. No force-push (cross-ref happens before any push, so the first push includes the real ochid). create_repo stays lean — no remote-related params. Testable standalone.

**Both paths can eliminate `init_one`.** It either becomes 1 call (Path A) or 2 calls (Path B) — small enough to inline at the dispatcher in `init_with_symlink`.

**Renumbering to 1..9 works either way.** Step 6 (cross-ref) lives only in `create_dual`; create_repo's body uses 1-5 (Path B) or 1-5+7-9 renumbered to a contiguous flow (Path A).

**Trade-off:**
- Path A is conceptually neater (one primitive, full lifecycle) but doubles remote writes and grows the signature.
- Path B preserves current remote behavior (single push per side) and keeps create_repo's signature lean. Cost: a separate `finalize_repo` helper (which we'd be writing in -6.3 anyway, just one commit earlier).

I lean **Path B**. The force-push in Path A is harmless for fresh remotes but visible noise in GitHub UIs, and the create_repo signature ballooning to carry plan/args/visibility undoes the "lean primitive" win we just made in -6.2. Path B gets us the same end state (init_one eliminated, lifecycle bundled into per-side helpers) without those costs — and it folds your -6.2/-6.3 plan into one commit if you want.

Bundle Path B into -6.2 (extending what's already in @), or land -6.2 as-is and do this in -6.3?

> DEVELOPER

What about we have create_local_repo and push_repo() then init_one does a create_local_repo + push_repo and init_dual does create_local_repo, create_local_repo, edit vc-config, push repo?

> AGENT

The structural shape matches Path B exactly with sharper naming:

- **`create_local_repo`** (was `create_repo`) — adds explicit "local" to disambiguate from "creating a remote repo" (`gh repo create`). Steps 1-5: mkdir, git+jj init, configs, optional template, initial commit. Returns chid. Lives in `repo_utils.rs`.
- **`push_repo`** (was going to be `finalize_repo`) — covers steps 7-9: bookmark + clean + remote provision + push + re-init + tracking. Action-oriented; the central act is the push. Lives in `repo_utils.rs`.
- **`init_one`**: `create_local_repo + push_repo`. 2 calls. Small enough to inline at the dispatcher and remove `init_one` as a function.
- **`create_dual`** (renamed from init_dual in -6.2): `create_local_repo(code, Placeholder) + create_local_repo(bot, Placeholder) + cross-ref step + push_repo(code, exclude=.claude) + push_repo(bot) + symlink`.

Both renames are improvements over my -6.3 plan — `create_local_repo` reads more clearly than bare `create_repo`, and `push_repo` is more concrete than `finalize_repo`.

**One clarification on the cross-ref step** — you wrote "edit vc-config". I want to make sure I understand which file you mean to edit:

- **Currently (and in my mental model):** the cross-ref happens via `jj describe @-` on each side, rewriting the *commit description* to add an `ochid: /.claude/<chid>` (or `ochid: /<chid>`) trailer. The `.vc-config.toml` file itself isn't edited in step 6 — its `other-repo = ".claude"` field is a relative path written once in step 3 and stays put.
- **Possibility I'm flagging:** if you're suggesting the cross-ref should live *in* `.vc-config.toml` rather than in the commit description, that's a design change worth surfacing — the current ochid model puts the linkage in the commit message (so `git log` shows it; bot commits and code commits each carry the partner's chid in their text).

Are you proposing a design change (move ochid linkage from commit message to `.vc-config.toml`), or just labeling the existing step 6 differently? If the latter, the helper would more accurately be `set_cross_ref_ochids` (or similar) — it edits commit messages, not vc-config.

> DEVELOPER

I believe the "cross-refercing" you mention is the path + other fields in `.vc-config.toml`?

> AGENT

Almost — but they're two different cross-references, both present, serving different roles:

**1. Path-based cross-ref (in `.vc-config.toml`)** — written once in step 3, *inside* `create_local_repo`:
- Code's vc-config: `path = "/"`, `other-repo = ".claude"`
- Bot's vc-config: `path = "/.claude"`, `other-repo = ".."`
- Static. Known at create time. Says **where** the partner is on disk (relative path).

**2. Chid-based cross-ref (in the initial commit's message)** — what step 6 does, *outside* `create_local_repo`:
- Code's `@-` description gets: `Initial commit\n\nochid: /.claude/<bot_chid>`
- Bot's `@-` description gets: `Initial commit\n\nochid: /<code_chid>`
- Says **which** specific commit on the partner side this side was paired with at init.

The two complement each other:
- The **path** tells you where to look for the partner repo.
- The **ochid** tells you whether the partner you find is the same one you were originally paired with (a content-addressed handshake — the chid is jj's stable changeID, doesn't drift across `jj describe`).

Tools like `vc-x1 validate-desc` use the path to *find* the partner and the ochid to *verify* the link is intact. If someone replaces `.claude/` with a different repo, the path still resolves but the ochid no longer matches — that's how the integrity check fires.

**So step 6 in init_dual edits the commit messages, not vc-config.** A more accurate helper name than "edit vc-config" would be `set_ochid_refs` or `cross_ref_ochids` — it takes both sides' chids and writes them into each side's initial-commit description via `jj describe`.

With that pinned down, the proposal becomes:

```
init_one:    create_local_repo + push_repo
create_dual: create_local_repo(code, Placeholder)
             create_local_repo(bot, Placeholder)
             cross_ref_ochids(code, bot)        ← edits commit messages, not vc-config
             push_repo(code, exclude=.claude)
             push_repo(bot)
             symlink
```

Want to proceed with this for -6.3 (push_repo extraction + cross_ref_ochids extraction + init_one elimination), or land -6.2 first and do this in -6.3?

> DEVELOPER

The changing of .vc-config.toml in both and creating the ochid for the bodies are the last steps, anyway, I think you got it and they'll be less duplication then currently.

> AGENT

Got it — design is locked on the structure (path-based cross-ref in vc-config written in step 3 of create_local_repo, chid-based cross-ref in commit bodies written by cross_ref_ochids at the end of dual flow). And you're right that the duplication shrinks substantially: dual currently has ~50 lines of per-side step-7-10 logic duplicated; split, it becomes two 1-line `push_repo` calls.

One thing for you to decide on sequencing — -6.2 in @ is currently scoped to "create_repo extraction + url module rename." Two paths forward:

**Bundle into -6.2.** Expand the in-flight commit to include `create_repo → create_local_repo` rename + `push_repo` extraction + `cross_ref_ochids` extraction + `init_one` elimination. One coherent "lifecycle helpers + module reshape" commit. ~+150 lines in repo_utils.rs, ~-100 lines in init.rs (init_one disappears, init_dual collapses further). Bigger diff but a single self-contained landing.

**Split into -6.2 and -6.3.** Land -6.2 as currently sitting (create_repo + module reshape, behavior preservation gated by 323 tests). Then -6.3 does the rename to `create_local_repo`, adds `push_repo` + `cross_ref_ochids`, and eliminates `init_one`. Two smaller bisectable commits; -6.2 has create_repo briefly before the rename in -6.3.

Earlier you said "separate, and as we refactor I expect new tests will be created" — that pointed toward split. Splitting also gives -6.3 its own test additions (one for push_repo, one for cross_ref_ochids).

I'd lean split unless you specifically want the bigger commit. Which?

1305	    // Step 6: ochid cross-references (rewrites both placeholders)

1314	    )?;
1315	    debug!("session side: rewrite initial commit's ochid to point at code chid");
1316	    run("jj", &["describe", "@-", "-m", &session_desc], session_dir)?;

1319	    let hash = run("git", &["rev-parse", "HEAD"], &plan.project_dir)?;
1320	    debug!("code repo: chid={code_chid} hash={hash}");
1321	    let hash = run("git", &["rev-parse", "HEAD"], session_dir)?;

1324	    // Step 7: Set bookmarks, remove jj (code-side clean preserves .claude/)

1327	    run(

1333	    run("jj", &["bookmark", "set", "main", "-r", "@-"], session_dir)?;

1335	    run("git", &["clean", "-xdf"], session_dir)?;
1336	    debug!("strip jj state from code side, preserving nested .claude/");
1337	    run(
1338	        "git",

1342	    debug!("re-attach session HEAD to main after clean");
1343	    run("git", &["checkout", "main"], session_dir)?;
1344	    debug!("re-attach code HEAD to main after clean");
1345	    run("git", &["checkout", "main"], &plan.project_dir)?;
1346	
1347	    // Steps 8/9: provision remotes and push.
1348	    run_remote_step(
1349	        "Step 8",

1365	        plan.code_bare_path.as_deref(),
1366	        visibility,
1367	        &plan.project_dir,
1368	        args,
1369	    )?;
1370	
1371	    // Step 10: Re-initialize jj on both repos

1373	    debug!("re-colocate jj atop the now-remote-linked code repo");
1374	    run(
1375	        "jj",
1376	        &["--quiet", "git", "init", "--colocate"],
1377	        &plan.project_dir,
1378	    )?;
1379	    debug!("restore code-side main bookmark after re-init");

1386	    run(

1391	    crate::common::verify_tracking(&plan.project_dir, "main")?;
1392	    let code_chid_final = jj_chid("@-", &plan.project_dir)?;
1393	
1394	    debug!("re-colocate jj atop the now-remote-linked session repo");
1395	    run("jj", &["--quiet", "git", "init", "--colocate"], session_dir)?;
1396	    debug!("restore session-side main bookmark after re-init");
1397	    run("jj", &["bookmark", "set", "main", "-r", "@-"], session_dir)?;

1435	/// Execute Step 8 or Step 9 for one side.
1436	///
1437	/// - Provision the remote per the plan's provisioner.
1438	/// - Add `origin` and push `main` with retry.
1439	/// - Centralizing keeps both sides' step bodies identical.
1440	#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
1441	fn run_remote_step(
1442	    step_label: &str,
1443	    side_label: &str,
1444	    plan: &InitPlan,
1445	    remote_url: &str,
1446	    gh_slug: Option<&str>,
1447	    bare_path: Option<&Path>,
1448	    visibility: &str,
1449	    push_from: &Path,
1450	    args: &InitArgs,
1451	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
1452	    match &plan.provisioner {
1453	        Provisioner::GhCreate => {
1454	            // Safe: GhCreate always supplies gh_slug.
1455	            #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
1456	            let slug = gh_slug.unwrap(); // OK: GhCreate path always sets gh_slug
1457	            info!("{step_label}: Creating GitHub repo {slug} ({side_label})...");
1458	            debug!("create {side_label}-side remote on GitHub");
1459	            run(
1460	                "gh",
1461	                &["repo", "create", slug, visibility],
1462	                &plan.project_dir,
1463	            )?;
1464	        }
1465	        Provisioner::LocalBareInit => {
1466	            // Safe: LocalBareInit always supplies bare_path.
1467	            #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
1468	            let bare = bare_path.unwrap(); // OK: LocalBareInit path always sets bare_path
1469	            info!(
1470	                "{step_label}: Initializing local bare repo at {} ({side_label})...",
1471	                bare.display()
1472	            );
1473	            debug!("init {side_label}-side bare repo as the local origin");
1474	            run(
1475	                "git",
1476	                &["init", "--bare", &bare.to_string_lossy()],
1477	                Path::new("."),
1478	            )?;
1479	        }
1480	        Provisioner::ExternalPreExisting => {
1481	            info!("{step_label}: Using pre-existing {side_label} remote {remote_url}");
1482	        }
1483	    }
1484	    debug!("point {side_label}-side git at its remote");
1485	    run("git", &["remote", "add", "origin", remote_url], push_from)?;
1486	    debug!("publish {side_label}-side initial commit; retry for GhCreate's async propagation");
1487	    run_retry(
1488	        "git",
1489	        &["push", "-u", "origin", "main"],
1490	        push_from,
1491	        args.push_retries,
1492	        args.push_retry_delay,
1493	    )?;
1494	    Ok(())
1495	}

1497	/// Split an `owner/name` slug. Errors if the shape is wrong (no `/`
1498	/// or more than one `/`).
1499	fn split_slug(slug: &str) -> Result<(&str, &str), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
1500	    let parts: Vec<&str> = slug.split('/').collect();
1501	    if parts.len() != 2 || parts[0].is_empty() || parts[1].is_empty() {
1502	        return Err(format!("unexpected GitHub slug '{slug}' (want 'owner/name')").into());
1503	    }
1504	    Ok((parts[0], parts[1]))
1505	}

Now I'll plan the approach. Looking at steps 7-9, `push_repo` per-side does: bookmark-set-main + git-clean (with optional `--exclude .claude`) + checkout-main + provision-remote+push. I'll keep `push_repo` in `init.rs` since it ties to `InitPlan`/`InitArgs`/`Provisioner`.

Order:
1. **Rename** `create_repo` → `create_local_repo` (mechanical rename across repo_utils.rs + init.rs — bundle as one checkpoint per the refactor exception)
2. **Extract** `push_repo` in init.rs + update `init_one` and `create_dual` call sites
3. **Tests** + Cargo.toml bump + chores-08.md

Starting with step 1: the rename.

Project created at {}\", plan.project_dir.display());\n    info!(\n        \"  Code repo:    {}  (chid={code_chid_final})\",\n        plan.code_url\n    );\n\n    debug!(\"init: exit\");\n    Ok(())\n}"
}

target)?;\n        }\n    }\n    debug!(\"re-attach {info_label} HEAD to main after clean\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"checkout\", \"main\"], target)?;\n\n    run_remote_step(\n        step_label_provision,\n        info_label,\n        plan,\n        remote_url,\n        gh_slug,\n        bare_path,\n        visibility,\n        target,\n        args,\n    )?;\n\n    info!(\"Step Build + clippy clean. Summary of `init.rs` changes:

- **`init_one`** doc and body collapsed: now just `create_local_repo` + skipped-step narration + a single `push_repo("Step 9", clean_exclude=None)` call. Lost ~75 lines of inline step 7/9/10 plumbing.
- **`create_dual`** doc and body collapsed: steps 7-10 (per-side bookmark/clean/checkout interleaved + push session + push code + re-init both) replaced by two sequential `push_repo` calls (`session` first with `clean_exclude=None`, then `code` with `clean_exclude=Some(".claude")`). Lost ~80 lines.
- **`push_repo`** added between `create_dual` and `run_remote_step` — narrates Steps 7/8|9/10 per side, calls `run_remote_step` internally, returns the final chid.

Repo_utils.rs: dropped "Per-side" from the `create_local_repo` headline doc (you asked — function doesn't know or care about sides).

Behavior delta vs pre-refactor (cosmetic only): in dual mode, narration is now fully sequential per side (session: 7→8→10, then code: 7→9→10) instead of the prior interleaved pattern (7-both → 8-session → 9-code → 10-both). Same actions, same order of subprocess invocations within each side.

Stopping for review before running the test suite.

28	/// Create primitive: steps 1-5 (mkdir, git/jj init, configs,
29	/// optional template, initial commit) on `target`. Returns
30	/// the chid of the new initial commit (`jj @-`).
31	///
32	/// - `target` — destination directory for the new repo. Created
33	///   (along with its parent if needed); must not already exist
34	///   as a populated repo.
35	/// - `info_label` — narration tag (`"code"`, `"bot"`, `"scratch"`,
36	///   etc.); appears in `info!()` lines.
37	/// - `config` / `gitignore` — optional file contents. `Some(s)`
38	///   writes `target/.vc-config.toml` (or `.gitignore`); `None`
39	///   skips the write entirely. Useful for upgrade paths that
40	///   leave config files in place, or for scratch repos that want
41	///   a bare git+jj init without project-specific files.
42	/// - `template` — optional source dir. When present, copied
43	///   recursively (non-hidden only) and any `README.md`'s first
44	///   line is rewritten to `# <name>`.
45	/// - `name` — repo name used by the README rewrite.
46	/// - `ochid_strategy` — initial-commit message policy.
47	///
48	/// Per-side narration: `info!()` is emitted once per call rather
49	/// than once per step across sides — dual orchestrators get two
50	/// narration passes (code, then bot) instead of one interleaved.
51	#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
52	pub fn create_local_repo(
53	    target: &Path,
54	    info_label: &str,
55	    config: Option<&str>,
56	    gitignore: Option<&str>,
57	    template: Option<&Path>,
58	    name: &str,
59	    ochid_strategy: OchidStrategy,
60	) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
61	    info!(
62	        "Step 1: Creating {info_label} directory at {}",
63	        target.display()
64	    );
65	    if let Some(parent) = target.parent() {
66	        mkdir_p(parent)?;
67	    }
68	    mkdir_p(target)?;
69	
70	    info!("Step 2: Initializing {info_label} repo (git + jj)...");
71	    debug!("seed {info_label} dir with an empty git repo");
72	    run("git", &["init"], target)?;
73	    debug!("colocate jj atop the {info_label} git repo");
74	    run("jj", &["git", "init", "--colocate"], target)?;
75	
76	    match (config, gitignore) {
77	        (None, None) => {
78	            info!("Step 3: (skipped — no {info_label} config files requested)");
79	        }
80	        _ => {
81	            info!("Step 3: Writing {info_label} config files...");
82	            if let Some(c) = config {
83	                write_file(&target.join(".vc-config.toml"), c)?;
84	            }
85	            if let Some(g) = gitignore {
86	                write_file(&target.join(".gitignore"), g)?;
87	            }
88	        }
89	    }
90	
91	    match template {
92	        Some(t) => {
93	            info!(
94	                "Step 4: Copying {info_label} template: {} -> {}",
95	                t.display(),
96	                target.display()
97	            );
98	            copy_template_recursive(t, target)?;
99	            rewrite_readme_first_line(target, name)?;
100	        }
101	        None => {
102	            info!("Step 4: (skipped — no {info_label} template)");
103	        }
104	    }
105	
106	    let msg = match ochid_strategy {
107	        OchidStrategy::None => "Initial commit",
108	        OchidStrategy::Placeholder => "Initial commit\n\nochid: /none",
109	    };
110	    info!("Step 5: Committing {info_label}...");
111	    debug!("{info_label} side: jj commit (ochid_strategy={ochid_strategy:?})");
112	    run("jj", &["commit", "-m", msg], target)?;
113	
114	    let chid = jj_chid("@-", target)?;
115	    debug!("{info_label} initial chid = {chid}");
116	    Ok(chid)
117	}
```

602	### Extract create_repo + module reshape (0.41.1-6.2)
603	
604	First substantive DRY pass on `init_with_symlink`. Lifts steps 1-5
605	(mkdir, git/jj init, configs, optional template, initial commit)
606	into a generalized per-side primitive `create_repo` living in a new
607	`src/repo_utils.rs` module. The dual orchestrator drops from ~180
608	lines to ~110 by replacing two ~50-line per-side blocks with two
609	`create_repo` calls. Same suite (320) plus 3 new unit tests for
610	the helper itself: total 323, all green.
611	
612	**New module `src/repo_utils.rs`:**
613	
614	- `pub fn create_repo(target, info_label, config: Option<&str>,
615	  gitignore: Option<&str>, template: Option<&Path>, name,
616	  ochid_strategy) -> Result<chid>`. Per-side primitive returning
617	  the new initial commit's chid.
618	- `pub enum OchidStrategy { None, Placeholder }` — initial-commit
619	  message policy. POR uses `None` (plain "Initial commit"); dual
620	  uses `Placeholder` ("Initial commit\n\nochid: /none", rewritten
621	  in step 6 once both chids are known).
622	- 3 unit tests: `strategy_none_writes_plain_commit`,
623	  `strategy_placeholder_writes_ochid_none`,
624	  `no_config_or_gitignore_writes_neither_file`. Last one pins the
625	  new None-skips-write semantics.
626	
627	**Generalizations from -5/-6.1's inline shape:**
628	
629	- `info_label` (was `side_label`) — drops the dual-specific "side"
630	  connotation; the label is just for `info!()` narration. POR
631	  passes `"code"`; dual passes `"code"` then `"bot"`; future
632	  upgrade paths or scratch-repo callers can pass anything else.
633	- `config` and `gitignore` are `Option<&str>` (was `&str`). `None`
634	  skips that file's write. Future use cases:
635	  - PorJjGit / PorWithPeerPor upgrade paths in -6.7/-6.8 leave
636	    existing `.vc-config.toml` in place.
637	  - A hypothetical `vc-x1 scratch-repo` subcommand wanting a bare
638	    git+jj init without project-specific files.
639	
640	**Module rename `repo_url.rs` → `url.rs`:**
641	
642	The old name was misleading — the module's contents (`Target`
643	enum, `parse_target`, `derive_name`, `resolve_url`,
644	`derive_session_url`) parse and derive *URLs and target strings*,
645	not "repos". Pure string manipulation, no repo concept.
646	
647	- File renamed; jj tracks via content.
648	- `repo: &str` parameter on `derive_name` / `resolve_url` →
649	  `url: &str`. Internal references updated to match.
650	- Doc comments tightened: "repo URL" → "URL", "Resolve a repo
651	  argument" → "Resolve a target string", "session repo URL from a
652	  code repo URL" → "session URL from a code-side URL".
653	- All callers updated: `init.rs`, `clone.rs`, `main.rs` (`mod
654	  repo_url;` → `mod url;`).
655	
656	**Renames in `init.rs`:**
657	
658	- `init_dual` → `create_dual`. Symmetric with `create_repo` and
659	  signals that this is a creation-time orchestrator (not a
660	  generic init flow). `init_one` stays — we expect it to collapse
661	  into a 2-call composition by -6.4 (just `create_repo` +
662	  `finalize_repo`) and may not survive as a standalone function.
663	- `init_with_symlink`'s dispatcher updated to call `create_dual`.
664	
665	**Visibility surface (`pub(crate)`) — to support cross-module use
666	from `repo_utils`:**
667	
668	- `jj_chid` (was `fn`).
669	- `VC_CONFIG_APP_ONLY`, `VC_CONFIG_CODE`, `GITIGNORE_APP_ONLY`,
670	  `GITIGNORE_CODE` (were `const`). Used by repo_utils tests.
671	- `copy_template_recursive`, `rewrite_readme_first_line` were
672	  already `pub(crate)`.
673	
674	**Cosmetic delta in user-visible output:**
675	
676	Dual mode's step narration shifts from one interleaved pass
677	(`Step 1: ...` once, both sides mkdir, `Step 2: ...` once, etc.)
678	to two per-side passes (all five steps for code, then all five
679	for bot). Same actions, same order — just `info!()` ordering.
680	Not covered by tests.
681	
682	**WIP ladder:**
683	
684	Single commit. Bundled the `repo_url` → `url` rename and
685	`init_dual` → `create_dual` rename into the same -6.2 commit
686	since the whole change is "module structure & naming."
687	
688	### Decisions made during design
689	
690	- **Version + cycle line.** This work + the sync `--check`
691	  fix land on the 0.41.x line. Init+clone = 0.41.1. Sync
```

  "new_string": "### Extract push_repo + rename create_repo → create_local_repo (0.41.1-6.3)\n\nSecond DRY pass on `init_with_symlink`. Lifts the post-create\nper-side machinery (bookmark + clean + checkout, remote\nprovision/push, jj re-init + tracking) into a single helper\n`push_repo` in `src/init.rs`. Both orchestrators (`init_one`,\n`create_dual`) collapse hard: `init_one` from ~90 lines to ~35,\n`create_dual` from ~165 lines to ~85. Suite stays at 323, all\ngreen.\n\n**Rename `create_repo` → `create_local_repo`:**\n\n- Function in `src/repo_utils.rs`; 3 call sites in `init.rs`\n  (1 in `init_one`, 2 in `create_dual`); 3 test `expect()`\n  strings; all doc-comment references.\n- Explicitly local — no remote interaction; pairs with the new\n  `push_repo` (remote-side counterpart).\n\n**New helper `push_repo` (in `init.rs`):**\n\n```\nfn push_repo(\n    target, info_label, step_label_provision,\n    clean_exclude: Option<&str>,\n    plan, args, visibility,\n    remote_url, gh_slug, bare_path,\n) -> Result<String>  // final chid after re-init\n```\n\nWraps three former-step regions per side:\n\n- Bookmark `main` at `@-`, `git clean -xdf` (with optional\n  `--exclude`), `git checkout main`.\n- Calls `run_remote_step` (provision via\n  GhCreate/LocalBareInit/ExternalPreExisting + `git remote add` +\n  `git push -u origin main` with retry).\n- `jj git init --colocate`, restore bookmark, track, verify, return\n  `jj_chid(\"@-\", target)`.\n\n`clean_exclude` is the only per-call asymmetry: code-side dual\npasses `Some(\".claude\")` to preserve the nested session repo;\nsession-side and POR pass `None`.\n\nLives in `init.rs` (not `repo_utils.rs`) because it depends on\n`InitPlan` / `InitArgs` / `Provisioner` — types tightly coupled to\n`init`'s plan-and-execute shape. If those decouple later, push_repo\ncan move.\n\n**Doc-comment cleanup on `create_local_repo`:**\n\n- Headline restructured into `Performs:` (5 actions) and\n  `Parameters:` (one bullet per arg) lists. The old prose about\n  \"Steps 1-5\" went away — `create_local_repo` no longer narrates\n  step numbers, so the doc shouldn't either.\n- \"Per-side narration\" paragraph dropped — when there's no step\n  numbering, there's no step-vs-side narration trade-off to\n  describe.\n- Drops `Step N:` prefixes from `info!()` lines: `Creating ...`,\n  `Initializing ...`, `Writing ...`, `Copying ...`,\n  `Committing ...`, plus a final `Created local repo ... chid = ...`\n  summary line.\n- Skipped-step narration dropped: when both `config` and\n  `gitignore` are `None`, or `template` is `None`, no `info!()` is\n  emitted (was previously `(skipped — ...)`).\n\n**Cosmetic delta in user-visible output:**\n\nTwo changes vs -6.2:\n\n1. `info!()` lines drop `Step N:` prefixes (carried by\n   `create_local_repo` and the new `push_repo`).\n2. Dual mode now narrates fully sequential per side\n   (session: bookmark/clean/checkout → push → re-init, then code:\n   same), instead of the prior interleaved pattern (Step 7 both\n   sides, then push session, then push code, then re-init both).\n   Same subprocess invocations in the same order *within* each\n   side; only across-side ordering shifts.\n\nNot covered by tests.\n\n**WIP ladder:**\n\nSingle commit; the `create_repo` → `create_local_repo` rename and\nthe `push_repo` extraction ride together since the rename is purely\nmechanical and the push extraction touches the same files\n(`init.rs`, `repo_utils.rs`).\n\n### Decisions made during design"
}

1	//! Shared test helpers for dual-repo integration tests.
2	//!
3	//! Provides a `Fixture` that wraps `crate::init::init_with_symlink`
4	//! plus a per-process unique-tempdir helper so parallel tests don't
5	//! collide. Lifted out of the `sync` test module (originally inline at
6	//! `sync.rs:521–560`) so `push`'s tests (0.37.0) and any future
7	//! subcommand's tests can sit on the same harness without
8	//! copy-paste drift.
9	//!
10	//! Test-only — the whole module is gated at its declaration site via
11	//! `#[cfg(test)] mod test_helpers;`.
12	
13	use std::fs;
14	use std::path::PathBuf;
15	use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU64, Ordering};
16	use std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};
17	
18	use crate::args::ScopeKind;
19	use crate::common::write_file;
20	use crate::config::RepoSelector;
21	use crate::init::{InitArgs, init_with_symlink};
22	
23	/// Per-process counter so same-nanosecond tempdir collisions yield
24	/// distinct paths when tests run in parallel.
25	static COUNTER: AtomicU64 = AtomicU64::new(0);
26	
27	/// Build a unique tempdir path for a test fixture.
28	///
29	/// Combines a nanosecond timestamp with a per-process atomic counter
30	/// so parallel tests and same-nanosecond collisions both yield
31	/// distinct paths. Prefix is `vc-x1-test-<tag>-<ts>-<n>` so callers
32	/// from different subcommand tests stay discoverable in `$TMPDIR`.
33	pub fn unique_base(tag: &str) -> PathBuf {
34	    let ts = SystemTime::now()
35	        .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
36	        .map(|d| d.as_nanos())
37	        .unwrap_or(0); // OK: clock error → 0 is harmless for unique tempdir naming
38	    let n = COUNTER.fetch_add(1, Ordering::SeqCst);
39	    std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("vc-x1-test-{tag}-{ts}-{n}"))
40	}
41	
42	/// Owned dual-repo fixture with RAII cleanup.
43	///
44	/// Builds a fresh throwaway dual-repo set under a unique tempdir
45	/// by driving `init::init_with_symlink` with a path TARGET and
46	/// `--repo local=<base>` (so config lookup is skipped via the
47	/// resolve_repo short-circuit). `create_symlink=false` suppresses
48	/// the `~/.claude/projects/` side effect. Exposes the two repo
49	/// paths (`work` and `work/.claude`). The tempdir tree is removed
50	/// when the value is dropped, so a panicking test still cleans up
51	/// after itself.
52	pub struct Fixture {
53	    /// Root tempdir that owns both repos and their bare-git remotes.
54	    pub base: PathBuf,
55	    /// Code repo path (`<base>/work`).
56	    pub work: PathBuf,
57	    /// Bot session repo path (`<base>/work/.claude`).
58	    pub claude: PathBuf,
59	}
60	
61	impl Fixture {
62	    /// Build a fresh fixture in a unique tempdir.
63	    pub fn new(tag: &str) -> Self {
64	        Self::new_opts(tag, false, None)
65	    }
66	
67	    /// Build a fresh fixture with optional pending changes and a
68	    /// template seed.
69	    ///
70	    /// - `with_pending` — after init, write a small file into each
71	    ///   repo so `@` carries uncommitted changes (useful for
72	    ///   finalize / push tests).
73	    /// - `use_template` — `CODE[,BOT]` forwarded to init.
74	    pub fn new_opts(tag: &str, with_pending: bool, use_template: Option<String>) -> Self {
75	        let base = unique_base(tag);
76	        // init refuses to reuse an existing project_dir, so `base`
77	        // must not exist yet. `unique_base` already guarantees that.
78	        //
79	        // Path TARGET = `<base>/work` (workspace destination); the
80	        // basename ("work") becomes the repo name. `--repo local=<base>`
81	        // sets the bare-repo parent so the layout mirrors the old
82	        // `--repo-local <base>` + NAME=`work` shape:
83	        //   <base>/work/                  (code repo)
84	        //   <base>/work/.claude/          (bot session repo)
85	        //   <base>/remote-code.git        (code bare origin)
86	        //   <base>/remote-claude.git      (bot bare origin)
87	        let work_path = base.join("work");
88	        let args = InitArgs {
89	            target: work_path.to_string_lossy().into_owned(),
90	            name: None,
91	            account: None,
92	            repo: Some(RepoSelector {
93	                category: "local".to_string(),
94	                value: Some(base.to_string_lossy().into_owned()),
95	            }),
96	            scope: ScopeKind::CodeBot,
97	            private: false,
98	            dry_run: false,
99	            push_retries: 5,
100	            push_retry_delay: 3,
101	            use_template,
102	        };
103	        init_with_symlink(&args, false).expect("build test fixture via init");
104	
105	        let work = base.join("work");
106	        let claude = work.join(".claude");
107	
108	        if with_pending {
109	            write_file(&work.join("TODO.md"), "# TODO\n- first feature\n")
110	                .expect("write pending TODO.md");
111	            write_file(
112	                &claude.join("session-notes.md"),
113	                "# Session notes\n- simulated pending work\n",
114	            )
115	            .expect("write pending session-notes.md");
116	        }
117	
118	        Fixture { base, work, claude }
119	    }
120	
121	    /// Convenience: return both repo paths as a `Vec<PathBuf>`
122	    /// suitable for `sync_repos` (or any other `&[PathBuf]` API).
123	    pub fn repos(&self) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
124	        vec![self.work.clone(), self.claude.clone()]
125	    }
126	}
127	
128	impl Drop for Fixture {
129	    /// Remove the fixture tree on drop. Best-effort; a failure here
130	    /// doesn't fail the test.
131	    fn drop(&mut self) {
132	        let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(&self.base);
133	    }
134	}
135	
136	/// Owned single-repo (POR) fixture with RAII cleanup.
137	///
138	/// Sibling of `Fixture` for `--scope=por` flows. Drives
139	/// `init::init_with_symlink` with `ScopeKind::Por` and a path
140	/// TARGET; `--repo local=<base>` steers the bare origin to
141	/// `<base>/remote.git` (vs. dual's `remote-code.git` /
142	/// `remote-claude.git`). No `.claude/` peer, no symlink.
143	///
144	/// Field shape differs from `Fixture` — there is no `claude` peer
145	/// path — so it's a distinct type rather than an `Option<PathBuf>`
146	/// on `Fixture` (the latter would force every dual-using caller to
147	/// `.unwrap()` or pattern-match).
148	pub struct FixturePor {
149	    /// Root tempdir that owns the repo and its bare-git remote.
150	    pub base: PathBuf,
151	    /// POR repo path (`<base>/work`).
152	    pub work: PathBuf,
153	}
154	
155	impl FixturePor {
156	    /// Build a fresh POR fixture in a unique tempdir.
157	    pub fn new(tag: &str) -> Self {
158	        let base = unique_base(tag);
159	        // Path TARGET = `<base>/work`; basename ("work") becomes
160	        // the repo name. `--repo local=<base>` sets the bare-repo
161	        // parent, producing the layout:
162	        //   <base>/work/        (POR repo)
163	        //   <base>/remote.git   (bare origin)
164	        let work_path = base.join("work");
165	        let args = InitArgs {
166	            target: work_path.to_string_lossy().into_owned(),
167	            name: None,
168	            account: None,

-6.3 work is complete. Summary of changes:

**Code:**
- `src/repo_utils.rs` — `create_repo` → `create_local_repo`. Doc restructured into `Performs:` / `Parameters:` bulleted lists (5 + 7 bullets). `Step N:` prefixes dropped from `info!()` lines; skipped-step narration removed; final summary line added (`Created local repo … chid = …`).
- `src/init.rs` — `init_one` collapsed (~90 → ~35 lines) and `create_dual` collapsed (~165 → ~85 lines) by replacing inline steps 7-10 with `push_repo` calls. New `push_repo(target, info_label, step_label_provision, clean_exclude, plan, args, visibility, remote_url, gh_slug, bare_path) -> chid` placed before `run_remote_step`. 4 new integration tests added: `dual_fixture_creates_dual_repo_layout`, `dual_fixture_writes_code_and_session_config_files`, `dual_fixture_both_sides_track_origin`, `dual_fixture_preserves_claude_across_code_clean`.

**Notes:**
- `notes/todo.md` — `-6.3` flipped `(current)` → `(done)`.
- `notes/chores-08.md` — new `### Extract push_repo + rename create_repo → create_local_repo (0.41.1-6.3)` subsection (rename, helper, doc cleanup, integration tests, cosmetic narration delta, single-commit ladder).

**Cargo:**
- `Cargo.toml` — version `0.41.1-6.3`.

**Cosmetic delta in user output (vs -6.2):**
1. `info!()` lines drop `Step N:` prefixes (in `create_local_repo` and `push_repo`).
2. Dual mode narrates fully sequential per side (session: bookmark/clean/checkout → push → re-init, then code: same), instead of -6.2's interleaved `Step 7 both → push session → push code → re-init both`. Same subprocesses, only across-side ordering shifts.

**Gates:** `cargo fmt`, `cargo clippy`, `cargo test` (323 → 327, all green), `cargo install --path . --locked`.

Work complete. Please review. On approval I'll prep the commit title and body for a second approval before pushing.

> AGENT

Confirmed — there are zero subprocess-style tests in the project; everything is in-process. So the 4 new tests fit the project's existing pattern but not your strict definition.

Options:

1. **Defer true integration tests to a new ladder item** (e.g. `-6.10`, or a 0.41.1-7-side-task). The 4 in-process tests still lock in `push_repo`'s observable invariants and ship with -6.3. The trade-off is "integration test" is still a misnomer in the codebase.
2. **Convert the 4 to true integration tests now**. Adds `tests/` dir + `assert_cmd` (or hand-rolled `Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1"))`) before -6.3 ships. Larger surface change — new dev-dep, new test crate, fixture rebuild for subprocess context (env vars, cwd).
3. **Both** — keep the in-process tests for fast-feedback unit-ish coverage, add true CLI integration tests as a separate item.

My lean: option 1 for -6.3, capture true CLI integration tests as a tracked item. The push_repo refactor is the focus; subprocess testing is its own infra change. Thoughts?

> AGENT

Plan for -6.4 (no code yet) — present for approval:

**Harness (zero new deps, hand-rolled `std::process::Command`):**
- `tests/common/mod.rs` — shared helpers:
  - `unique_base(tag)` — tempdir naming, mirrors `test_helpers::unique_base`
  - `vc_x1()` — `Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1"))` (Cargo wires this up automatically for `tests/*.rs`)
  - `run_ok(&mut Command) -> Output` / `run_err(...)` — wrappers that diagnose with stdout/stderr on unexpected status
  - Subprocess gets `HOME` overridden to a per-test tempdir so user config can't leak in or get clobbered
  - RAII `CliFixture` owning the `<base>` tempdir (drops the tree)

**Initial test files:**
- `tests/cli_smoke.rs` — `vc-x1 --version` and `vc-x1 --help` (proves harness wires up correctly before any state-mutating tests are trusted)
- `tests/cli_init.rs` — subprocess counterparts to the existing in-process fixture tests:
  - `por_layout` — `vc-x1 init <base>/work --scope=por --repo local=<base>` lays down POR layout (work/, no .claude/, remote.git)
  - `dual_layout` — `vc-x1 init <base>/work --scope=code,bot --repo local=<base>` lays down dual (work/, work/.claude/, remote-code.git, remote-claude.git)

**Why hand-rolled vs `assert_cmd`:** zero extra deps, transparent. The boilerplate lives in `common/mod.rs`; per-test code stays clean. Easy to swap to `assert_cmd` later if it proves worth the dependency.

**Trade-offs / things to flag:**
- Subprocess spawn is ~100ms each — adding many tests will slow `cargo test`. Mitigation: only add tests that justify the spawn cost (i.e. assertions that need a real CLI process; pure logic stays in-process).
- These tests don't push to remote (LocalBareInit only). True remote-roundtrip stays out of scope.

**Non-goals for -6.4:**
- Not deleting the 4 in-process dual-fixture tests; they stay as fast unit-ish coverage.
- Not converting POR fixture tests; same reason.
- Not testing `push`, `sync`, `clone`, `finalize` end-to-end via subprocess — those land as separate items as we gain experience.

**File list:**
- New: `tests/common/mod.rs`, `tests/cli_smoke.rs`, `tests/cli_init.rs`
- Edits: `Cargo.toml` (version → 0.41.1-6.4), `notes/todo.md` (flip -6.4 done at close), `notes/chores-08.md` (new subsection)

Sound right, or would you like a different starting scope (e.g., `assert_cmd`, or a different test set)?

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/tests/common/mod.rs",
  "content": "//! Shared harness for CLI subprocess integration tests.\n//!\n//! Lives under `tests/common/` so Cargo treats it as a shared module\n//! (a top-level `tests/common.rs` would be compiled as its own test\n//! crate). Each `tests/<name>.rs` declares `mod common;` to opt in.\n//!\n//! - `vc_x1()` returns a `Command` for the binary Cargo built for\n//!   the current test crate (`env!(\"CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1\")`).\n//! - `run_ok` / `run_err` wrap `Command::output` with a panic on\n//!   the unexpected exit status, embedding stdout/stderr in the\n//!   message so failures are debuggable.\n//! - `CliFixture` owns a per-test tempdir under `$TMPDIR` and\n//!   removes it on drop. Subprocesses get `HOME` overridden to a\n//!   tempdir-scoped path so user config can't leak into or out of\n//!   the test.\n\nuse std::path::{Path, PathBuf};\nuse std::process::{Command, Output};\nuse std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU64, Ordering};\nuse std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};\n\nstatic COUNTER: AtomicU64 = AtomicU64::new(0);\n\n/// Build a unique tempdir path for a CLI test.\n///\n/// Mirrors `src/test_helpers::unique_base`'s shape (timestamp +\n/// per-process counter) but uses a `vc-x1-cli-test-` prefix to\n/// distinguish from the in-process fixtures' `vc-x1-test-` paths.\npub fn unique_base(tag: &str) -> PathBuf {\n    let ts = SystemTime::now()\n        .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)\n        .map(|d| d.as_nanos())\n        .unwrap_or(0);\n    let n = COUNTER.fetch_add(1, Ordering::SeqCst);\n    std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(\"vc-x1-cli-test-{tag}-{ts}-{n}\"))\n}\n\n/// Build a `Command` invoking the `vc-x1` binary that Cargo built\n/// for this test crate. Cargo sets `CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1` for\n/// integration tests automatically.\npub fn vc_x1() -> Command {\n    Command::new(env!(\"CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1\"))\n}\n\n/// Run `cmd`, panicking with stdout/stderr if it does not exit 0.\npub fn run_ok(cmd: &mut Command) -> Output {\n    let out = cmd.output().expect(\"spawn vc-x1\");\n    if !out.status.success() {\n        panic!(\n            \"vc-x1 unexpectedly failed: status={:?}\\n--- stdout ---\\n{}\\n--- stderr ---\\n{}\",\n            out.status,\n            String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout),\n            String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr),\n        );\n    }\n    out\n}\n\n/// Run `cmd`, panicking with stdout/stderr if it exits 0.\n#[allow(dead_code)] // retained for future error-path tests\npub fn run_err(cmd: &mut Command) -> Output {\n    let out = cmd.output().expect(\"spawn vc-x1\");\n    if out.status.success() {\n        panic!(\n            \"vc-x1 unexpectedly succeeded\\n--- stdout ---\\n{}\\n--- stderr ---\\n{}\",\n            String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout),\n            String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr),\n        );\n    }\n    out\n}\n\n/// RAII tempdir owner for CLI tests.\n///\n/// - `base` is a freshly-allocated unique tempdir; it does *not*\n///   exist on disk until the caller (or the subprocess) creates it.\n/// - `home` is a sibling under `base` reserved for `HOME` override\n///   on subprocesses, so config reads don't escape the fixture.\n/// - `Drop` removes the whole `base` tree (best-effort).\npub struct CliFixture {\n    pub base: PathBuf,\n    pub home: PathBuf,\n}\n\nimpl CliFixture {\n    /// Allocate a fresh fixture; create `home/` so `HOME=…` points\n    /// at a real directory.\n    pub fn new(tag: &str) -> Self {\n        let base = unique_base(tag);\n        let home = base.join(\"home\");\n        std::fs::create_dir_all(&home).expect(\"mkdir cli fixture home\");\n        CliFixture { base, home }\n    }\n\n    /// Build a `vc-x1` `Command` with `HOME` pointed at the\n    /// fixture's isolated home dir, so user config can't leak.\n    pub fn cmd(&self) -> Command {\n        let mut c = vc_x1();\n        c.env(\"HOME\", &self.home);\n        c\n    }\n\n    /// Convenience: join a relative path under `base`.\n    pub fn path(&self, rel: impl AsRef<Path>) -> PathBuf {\n        self.base.join(rel)\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Drop for CliFixture {\n    fn drop(&mut self) {\n        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.base);\n    }\n}\n"
}

> AGENT

Created `tests/common/mod.rs` with:
- `unique_base(tag)` — tempdir naming (mirrors `src/test_helpers::unique_base`, distinct prefix `vc-x1-cli-test-`)
- `vc_x1()` — `Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1"))`
- `run_ok` / `run_err` — diagnostic-on-fail wrappers around `Command::output`
- `CliFixture` (RAII) — owns `<base>/` tempdir + `<base>/home/` for HOME override; `cmd()` builds a `Command` with HOME pointed at the isolated dir; `Drop` removes the tree

Note: file lives at `tests/common/mod.rs` (not `tests/common.rs`) so Cargo treats it as a shared module rather than a standalone test crate.

Stopping for review before creating `tests/cli_smoke.rs`.

> DEVELOPER

Add do we have a technique for setting the "tmp" dir path?

> AGENT

Honest answer: not really, beyond the OS default.

**Currently:** Both `src/test_helpers.rs::unique_base` (line 39) and the new `tests/common/mod.rs::unique_base` use `std::env::temp_dir()`, which resolves to `$TMPDIR` on Unix (else `/tmp`). So a user can set `TMPDIR=/path/to/somewhere cargo test` and all fixture tempdirs land there. That's the implicit knob.

**What's missing:**
- No project-specific override (e.g. `VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR`).
- No mention of the convention in CLAUDE.md or chores.
- If a test SIGKILLs, the `Drop` doesn't fire and the tempdir leaks; nothing cleans those up.

**Options:**
1. **Leave as-is** — `TMPDIR` env var is the standard Rust testing convention; documenting it once is enough.
2. **Add a project-specific override** in both helpers — e.g. `VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR` checked first, fall back to `std::env::temp_dir()`. Useful if you want tests under one knob without touching the global TMPDIR.
3. **Default to a project-local path** like `target/test-tmp/` — predictable location, makes leak cleanup easy (`rm -rf target/test-tmp/`), but couples to the build dir.

My lean: (1) is fine for now. The `vc-x1-test-*` and `vc-x1-cli-test-*` prefixes already let you find leaked dirs (`find $TMPDIR -name "vc-x1-*test*" -mtime +1`). If you want stronger isolation later, (2) is a small unified change across both helpers.

Want me to add (2) now (so the new CLI harness has it from day one), defer to later, or leave (1)?

> AGENT

Todo added at the end of `## Todo`. Now to your CLI parameter question:

**Short answer: not cleanly for the test harness; possible but unclear value for the `vc-x1` binary.**

**Test harness:** `cargo test -- <args>` forwards `<args>` to libtest, not to test code. There's no clean way for `tests/common/mod.rs` to read a user-supplied flag. The Rust idiom for this is environment variables — `VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR=/path cargo test` is effectively the CLI param equivalent. Adding env-var support to `unique_base()` is ~3 lines and could land now if you want.

**`vc-x1` binary:** could add `--workspace-tmp …` or a global `--tmp-dir` flag, but `vc-x1` doesn't typically create tempdirs in normal operation — it creates real workspace dirs at user-chosen TARGETs. The flag would only be load-bearing if you start adding scratch/sandbox subcommands. So I'd defer the binary-flag question until there's a concrete use case.

**My lean:** add env-var support (`VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR`) to both `unique_base()` helpers as part of -6.4 — small, immediately useful for the new CLI tests, no risk of locking in a bad shape. Defer user-config / local-config / CLI-flag to the todo I just added.

Want me to wire the env-var path into both helpers now?

580	for the full design and [per-step record](./notes/chores-05.md#per-step-record)
581	for what each `0.37.0-N` dev step shipped.
582	
583	### Testing push + finalize
584	
585	Always test against a throwaway fixture, never the live workspace.
586	Scaffold one with `vc-x1 init --repo-local <PARENT>` (no GitHub, no
587	network), then run the complete push + finalize flow end-to-end.
588	The code repo uses plain `jj git push`; the session repo uses
589	`vc-x1 finalize` to squash trailing writes and push in one shot.
590	
591	`--repo-local` lays out:
592	```
593	<PARENT>/
594	  remote-code.git/     bare git remote for code repo
595	  remote-claude.git/   bare git remote for .claude session repo
596	  <NAME>/              code repo (jj colocated, main tracks origin)
597	    .vc-config.toml    path="/",       other-repo=".claude"
598	    .gitignore         /.claude /.git /.jj /target /.vc-x1
599	    .claude/           session repo (jj colocated, main tracks origin)
600	      .vc-config.toml  path="/.claude", other-repo=".."
601	      .gitignore       .git .jj
602	```
603	
604	```bash
605	parent=$(mktemp -u /tmp/vc-x1-test-XXXXXX)
606	vc-x1 init work --repo-local "$parent"
607	work="$parent/work"
608	session="$parent/work/.claude"
609	
610	# 1. code repo: described commit → advance main → push
611	echo hello > "$work/hello.txt"
612	jj describe @ -R "$work" -m 'feat: add hello.txt'
613	jj bookmark set main -r @ -R "$work"
614	jj git push -R "$work"
615	
616	# 2. session repo: trailing writes → finalize (squash into @-, push)
617	echo notes > "$session/notes.md"
618	vc-x1 finalize --repo "$session" --squash --push main --detach \
619	    --log "$session/finalize.log"
620	
621	# 3. inspect the detached child's log once it's done (≈10s by default)
622	sleep 12 && cat "$session/finalize.log"
623	
624	# 4. cleanup when done
625	rm -rf "$parent"
626	```
627	
628	**Why `jj git push` for code but `finalize` for `.claude`?** The
629	code repo's workflow is a plain dev commit on `@-` that we push
630	directly. The session repo mirrors the bot's runtime pattern:
631	session writes land in `@` (above the last committed dev commit),
632	and `finalize --squash @,@-` folds those trailing writes into the
633	dev commit just before pushing, so one atomic state goes upstream.
634	
635	The log file shows timestamped (nanoseconds) entries with PIDs,
636	covering the full flow: `main` entry/exit, `finalize` entry/exit,
637	`detach` spawn, and `finalize_exec` in the child process.
638	
639	Example — detached finalize against a fresh fixture. What the user
640	sees in the terminal (the parent process) is the pre-flight plan and
641	the detach confirmation; the child's work continues in the background:
642	```
643	$ vc-x1 finalize --repo "$session" --squash --push main \
644	    --detach --delay 1 --log "$session/finalize.log"
645	finalize: squash @ → @- in /tmp/vc-x1-test-8PD4x8/work/.claude
646	finalize: set bookmark 'main' mwutluxv 31a49010 → mwutluxv 31a49010 (@-)
647	finalize: push 'main' to remote
648	finalize: detached (pid 103787), log: /tmp/vc-x1-test-8PD4x8/finalize.log
649	```
650	
651	A few seconds later the log file (authoritative when the caller
652	closes the child's pipes) shows the full run, including the child's
653	own squash/push output:
654	```
655	$ cat "$session/finalize.log"
656	[INFO ] vc_x1::finalize: finalize: squash @ → @- in /tmp/vc-x1-test-8PD4x8/work/.claude
657	[INFO ] vc_x1::finalize: finalize: set bookmark 'main' mwutluxv 31a49010 → mwutluxv 31a49010 (@-)
658	[INFO ] vc_x1::finalize: finalize: push 'main' to remote
659	[INFO ] vc_x1::finalize: finalize: detached (pid 103787), log: /tmp/vc-x1-test-8PD4x8/finalize.log
660	[INFO ] vc_x1::common: Working copy  (@) now at: ovumtpup fa1cb861 (empty) (no description set)
661	Parent commit (@-)      : mwutluxv 584571ba main* | initial commit
662	[INFO ] vc_x1::common: Nothing changed.
663	[INFO ] vc_x1::common: Changes to push to origin:
664	  Move sideways bookmark main from 31a490102db7 to 584571ba54af
665	```
666	
667	Pre-flight failures (bookmark missing, non-tracking remote, squash
668	revset unresolved, push target lacks a description) exit the parent
669	synchronously with a non-zero status and a pointed error on stderr,
670	before the child is ever spawned.
671	
672	## Cross-repo Linking with Git Trailers
673	
674	Commits in each repo use [git trailers](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-interpret-trailers)
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/README.md",
  "old_string": "## Contributing\n\nBot-following workflow, commit conventions, and code style are\ncanonical in [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md):",
  "new_string": "## Testing\n\nThe repo ships two flavors of tests:\n\n- **In-process tests** — `#[cfg(test)] mod tests { … }` blocks inside\n  `src/*.rs`. These call library code directly (no subprocess spawn)\n  and run fastest. The dual / POR fixture tests under\n  `src/init.rs::tests` build throwaway workspaces by invoking\n  `init::init_with_symlink` as a function.\n- **CLI subprocess integration tests** — files under `tests/`. These\n  spawn the `vc-x1` binary that Cargo built (via\n  `env!(\"CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1\")`) so the full argument-parsing,\n  exit-code, and stdout/stderr surface is exercised. Each subprocess\n  gets a `HOME` override so the user's real `~/.config/vc-x1/` can't\n  leak in or get clobbered.\n\nRun everything with:\n\n```bash\ncargo test                 # unit + integration\ncargo test --lib           # in-process only\ncargo test --test cli_init # one integration test crate\n```\n\n### Test tempdir location\n\nBoth test layers create throwaway fixtures under a tempdir. The\nparent directory resolves in priority order:\n\n1. `$VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR` — explicit env override.\n2. `std::env::temp_dir()` — standard fallback (`$TMPDIR` on Unix,\n   else `/tmp`).\n\nUseful when you want tests on a tmpfs / SSD / project-local path\nwithout exporting `TMPDIR` globally:\n\n```bash\nVC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR=/dev/shm/vc-x1 cargo test\n```\n\nFixture directories are named `vc-x1-test-<tag>-<ts>-<n>` (in-process)\nand `vc-x1-cli-test-<tag>-<ts>-<n>` (CLI). RAII drop removes them on\ntest exit; SIGKILLs / panics in `Drop` can leak — search and clean\nmanually:\n\n```bash\nfind \"${VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR:-${TMPDIR:-/tmp}}\" -maxdepth 1 \\\n     -name 'vc-x1-*test*' -mtime +1 -exec rm -rf {} +\n```\n\nA future enhancement (tracked in `notes/todo.md`) extends the\npriority chain through `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml` and project-local\n`.vc-config.toml`.\n\n## Contributing\n\nBot-following workflow, commit conventions, and code style are\ncanonical in [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md):"
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/tests/cli_smoke.rs",
  "content": "//! CLI subprocess smoke tests.\n//!\n//! Pre-flight for the rest of the `tests/` crate: spawn `vc-x1`\n//! with no-op-ish arguments and verify the harness wires up\n//! correctly. If these fail, the more state-mutating CLI tests\n//! aren't worth running yet.\n\nmod common;\n\nuse common::{CliFixture, run_ok};\n\n/// `vc-x1 --version` exits 0 and prints a line containing the\n/// crate name. Pins that `env!(\"CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1\")` resolves and\n/// the binary actually runs.\n#[test]\nfn cli_version_runs() {\n    let fx = CliFixture::new(\"smoke-version\");\n    let out = run_ok(fx.cmd().arg(\"--version\"));\n    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout);\n    assert!(\n        stdout.contains(\"vc-x1\"),\n        \"expected --version output to mention 'vc-x1', got: {stdout:?}\"\n    );\n}\n\n/// `vc-x1 --help` exits 0 and lists at least one subcommand we know\n/// to exist (`init`). Pins clap's help renderer + the subcommand\n/// surface compiled into the test binary.\n#[test]\nfn cli_help_lists_init() {\n    let fx = CliFixture::new(\"smoke-help\");\n    let out = run_ok(fx.cmd().arg(\"--help\"));\n    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout);\n    assert!(\n        stdout.contains(\"init\"),\n        \"expected --help to list 'init' subcommand, got: {stdout:?}\"\n    );\n}\n"
}

15	pub struct InitArgs {
16	    /// Target — URL, owner/name shorthand, path, or bare NAME.
17	    ///
18	    /// - URL: `git@host:owner/name(.git)?`, `https://...(.git)?`
19	    ///   — used as-is; config not consulted.
20	    /// - owner/name shorthand: resolves to
21	    ///   `git@github.com:owner/name.git`; config not consulted.
22	    /// - Path: `./X`, `../X`, `/X`, `~/X`, `~`, `.`, `..` — is the
23	    ///   directory path; remote resolved via `--repo` chain.
24	    /// - Bare NAME: becomes NAME.git; remote resolved via
25	    ///   `--repo` chain.
26	    #[arg(value_name = "TARGET", verbatim_doc_comment)]
27	    pub target: String,
28	
29	    /// Repo directory name override (URL / owner/name forms only).
30	    ///
31	    /// - URL / owner/name forms: repo created at `cwd/<NAME>`
32	    ///   instead of the URL-derived name.
33	    /// - Path / bare-NAME forms: error if given (TARGET already
34	    ///   names the repo).
35	    #[arg(value_name = "NAME", verbatim_doc_comment)]
36	    pub name: Option<String>,

41	    ///   `[repo]` shorthand) is used.
42	    /// - Meaningful only with Path or bare-NAME targets — URL /
43	    ///   owner/name targets supply the remote directly.
44	    #[arg(long, value_name = "NAME", verbatim_doc_comment)]
45	    pub account: Option<String>,

49	    /// - Built-in categories: `remote` (URL prefix; init appends

51	    ///   repos at `<parent>/remote-{code,claude}.git`).
52	    /// - `--repo <cat>` looks up the value via the account chain.
53	    /// - `--repo <cat>=<val>` uses the literal value, no config
54	    ///   lookup needed.

100	    /// - With `--scope=por`: only `CODE` is used; passing `,BOT`

111	/// Run a command with retries, sleeping between attempts.
112	fn run_retry(
113	    cmd: &str,
114	    args: &[&str],

> DEVELOPER

Can integration and unit tests share a "resolve_tmp_root"

> AGENT

Yes — a `#[path]`-imported helper file works for this exact case.

**The constraint:** vc-x1 is binary-only (no `src/lib.rs`), and `src/test_helpers.rs` is `#[cfg(test)]`-gated inside `main.rs`. So integration tests in `tests/` (separate crates that can't reach private binary modules) can't import from there directly.

**The pattern:** put `resolve_tmp_root` in a tiny dependency-free file and physically include it from both sides via `#[path]`:

```rust
// src/test_tmp_root.rs   <-- single source of truth, pure stdlib
pub(crate) fn resolve_tmp_root() -> PathBuf { ... }
```

```rust
// src/test_helpers.rs
mod test_tmp_root;
use test_tmp_root::resolve_tmp_root;
```

```rust
// tests/common/mod.rs
#[path = "../src/test_tmp_root.rs"] mod test_tmp_root;
use test_tmp_root::resolve_tmp_root;
```

**Cost:** the file lives in `src/` (where Rust expects it to be `mod`-included from the crate root), but tests/ reaches it via a relative `#[path]` attribute. Future contributors see the `#[path]` and have to chase one hop.

**Constraint to maintain:** the shared file must have **zero crate-internal dependencies** — pure `std`, no `crate::` imports. Otherwise the integration-test crate can't compile it. `resolve_tmp_root` qualifies (just env-var + `PathBuf`). When the ladder todo extends it with config-file lookups, the config-loading itself probably *should* live in the binary's config module — at that point this helper would either A) take the config as a parameter (still pure stdlib) or B) move out of sharing.

**Alternatives:**
- Extract `src/lib.rs` and split into lib+bin. Cleaner long-term but a meaningful restructure.
- Keep the duplication. ~7 lines of pure stdlib, two sites; arguably below the DRY threshold.

My lean: do the `#[path]` extraction now. It's small, sets up the same pattern for future shared test infra (assertions, fixture-tree helpers), and the duplication will only grow as the chain extends. Want me to make the change?

> DEVELOPER

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/test_tmp_root.rs",
  "content": "//! Shared `resolve_tmp_root` for unit and integration tests.\n//!\n//! Pulled in from two contexts:\n//! - `src/test_helpers.rs` (binary's `#[cfg(test)]` unit-test\n//!   harness) via `mod test_tmp_root;` declared at the crate root.\n//! - `tests/common/mod.rs` (integration-test crate) via\n//!   `#[path = \"../../src/test_tmp_root.rs\"] mod test_tmp_root;`.\n//!\n//! Single source of truth. Keep this file **dependency-free** —\n//! pure `std::env` + `std::path::PathBuf` — so it compiles in\n//! both contexts. No `crate::…` imports; the integration-test\n//! crate has a different crate root and would fail to resolve\n//! them.\n//!\n//! Future work (per `notes/todo.md`) extends the priority chain\n//! through `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml` and project-local\n//! `.vc-config.toml`. At that point, config-loading should pass\n//! its result *in* (function arg) rather than be invoked from\n//! here, preserving the dependency-free constraint.\n\nuse std::path::PathBuf;\n\n/// Resolve the parent directory for test tempdirs.\n///\n/// Priority: `$VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR` (if set and non-empty) →\n/// `std::env::temp_dir()` (= `$TMPDIR` on Unix, else `/tmp`).\npub fn resolve_tmp_root() -> PathBuf {\n    if let Ok(p) = std::env::var(\"VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR\") {\n        if !p.is_empty() {\n            return PathBuf::from(p);\n        }\n    }\n    std::env::temp_dir()\n}\n"
}

> DEVELOPER

So from vc-x1/tests/common/mod.rs::some_test calling resove_tmp_root() it can find resolve_tmp_root() from where some_test is using path = ../../src/test_tmp_root.rs

> AGENT

Yes, exactly. The resolution chain:

1. **`tests/cli_init.rs`** has `mod common;` → loads `tests/common/mod.rs`.
2. **`tests/common/mod.rs`** has `#[path = "../../src/test_tmp_root.rs"] mod test_tmp_root;` → loads the shared file.
3. **`#[path]` is relative to the directory of the file containing the `mod` declaration**, which is `tests/common/`. So:
   - `../` → `tests/`
   - `../../` → project root
   - `../../src/test_tmp_root.rs` → `<project>/src/test_tmp_root.rs` ✓

After the load, `tests/common/mod.rs` has a sub-module named `test_tmp_root`, and `use test_tmp_root::resolve_tmp_root;` brings the function into the `common` namespace. Then `unique_base` calls it like any local function.

A few subtle properties worth noting:

- **Two separate compilations of the same file.** The integration-test crate compiles `src/test_tmp_root.rs` independently from the binary's compilation. Two object files, same source. They never link to each other — each crate gets its own private copy. (That's why the file must be `pure stdlib` — if it referenced `crate::config::…`, the integration-test crate would fail to resolve the path because *its* crate root is `tests/cli_init.rs`, not `src/main.rs`.)

- **`#[path]` doesn't mean "look up the symbol in another crate."** It only redirects where Rust searches for the *file* backing a module declaration. The integration-test crate doesn't know about `vc-x1`'s binary modules — it just compiles a file that happens to live under `src/`.

- **Editor / `rust-analyzer`** sometimes gets confused about which crate context to use when navigating a `#[path]`-included file. If you "go to definition" from `tests/common/mod.rs`, it usually works, but `rust-analyzer` may show the file as belonging to the binary crate first — that's a tooling artifact, not a compile-time concern.
