  "old_string": "### Decisions made during design",
  "new_string": "### CLI subprocess integration tests + tempdir-root sharing (0.41.1-6.4)\n\nFirst true CLI integration tests for the project. Adds a `tests/`\ncrate that spawns the `vc-x1` binary that Cargo built (via\n`env!(\"CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1\")`) so argument parsing, exit codes, and\nstdout/stderr are exercised end-to-end — distinct from the\nin-process fixture tests under `src/init.rs::tests`, which call\n`init::init_with_symlink` as a Rust function.\n\nThis step bumps the test-strategy floor from \"in-process only\" to\n\"both layers\"; it doesn't yet retire any in-process tests. The\nin-process tests stay as fast unit-ish coverage; subprocess tests\nare added as state-mutating cases warrant a real binary spawn.\n\n**New harness — `tests/common/mod.rs`:**\n\n- `vc_x1() -> Command` — `Command::new(env!(\"CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1\"))`.\n- `run_ok` / `run_err` — wrap `Command::output`, panic with\n  stdout+stderr embedded if exit status doesn't match expectation.\n- `CliFixture` — RAII tempdir owner. Each subprocess invocation\n  gets `HOME` overridden to `<base>/home/` so the user's real\n  `~/.config/vc-x1/` can't leak in or get clobbered.\n- `unique_base(tag)` — mirrors `src/test_helpers::unique_base` but\n  uses a `vc-x1-cli-test-` prefix to distinguish from in-process\n  fixtures' `vc-x1-test-` paths.\n- Crate-level `#![allow(dead_code)]` — standard idiom for\n  `tests/common/`: each `tests/*.rs` compiles as its own crate, so\n  helpers used by some test crates but not others would otherwise\n  warn.\n\n**New test files:**\n\n- `tests/cli_smoke.rs` (2 tests):\n  - `cli_version_runs` — `vc-x1 --version` exits 0 and stdout\n    contains `\"vc-x1\"`. Pins `CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1` resolves and\n    the binary actually runs.\n  - `cli_help_lists_init` — `vc-x1 --help` lists the `init`\n    subcommand. Pins clap's subcommand surface compiled in.\n- `tests/cli_init.rs` (2 tests, counterparts to in-process\n  fixtures):\n  - `cli_init_por_creates_layout` — mirrors\n    `por_fixture_creates_single_repo_layout`.\n  - `cli_init_dual_creates_layout` — mirrors\n    `dual_fixture_creates_dual_repo_layout`.\n\n**Tempdir-root override (`$VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR`):**\n\nBoth `unique_base` helpers (in-process and CLI) resolve the parent\ndirectory in priority order:\n\n1. `$VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR` (if set and non-empty).\n2. `std::env::temp_dir()` (= `$TMPDIR` on Unix, else `/tmp`).\n\nUseful for steering tests onto a tmpfs / SSD / project-local path\nwithout exporting `TMPDIR` globally. Future work (per\n`notes/todo.md`) extends the chain through\n`~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml` and project-local\n`.vc-config.toml`.\n\n**Shared `resolve_tmp_root` via `#[path]`:**\n\n`vc-x1` is binary-only (no `lib.rs`), so the integration-test\ncrate can't import from `src/test_helpers.rs` (which is\n`#[cfg(test)]`-gated inside `main.rs`). To avoid duplicating the\n~10-line resolver, lifted it into `src/test_tmp_root.rs` (pure\nstdlib) and reach it from two contexts:\n\n- `src/main.rs` — `#[cfg(test)] mod test_tmp_root;` (sibling of\n  `test_helpers`); `src/test_helpers.rs` then\n  `use crate::test_tmp_root::resolve_tmp_root;`.\n- `tests/common/mod.rs` —\n  `#[path = \"../../src/test_tmp_root.rs\"] mod test_tmp_root;` +\n  `use test_tmp_root::resolve_tmp_root;`.\n\nTwo separate compilations of the same source — no cross-crate\nlinking. Constraint maintained: the file is dependency-free\n(no `crate::*` imports), so the integration-test crate (whose\ncrate root is `tests/cli_init.rs`, not `src/main.rs`) can compile\nit. When the priority chain extends to config files, the\nconfig-loading should pass its result *in*, preserving this\nconstraint.\n\nThe user framed this as a precedent: **strive to share code, not\nmandatory but a \"good to have\"**. The duplication threshold for\napplying `#[path]` is judgment-driven; here, ~10 lines × 2 sites\n+ expected growth made it worthwhile.\n\n**README.md `## Testing` section:**\n\nNew top-level section before `## Contributing`. Documents:\n\n- The two flavors (in-process vs CLI subprocess) and how to run\n  each (`cargo test`, `--lib`, `--test cli_init`).\n- The `$VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR` env var with example usage and the\n  cleanup `find` recipe for SIGKILL leaks.\n- Pointer to the todo for the future config-file extension.\n\n**Test counts:**\n\n- 327 in-process tests (unit + library), unchanged.\n- 2 `cli_smoke` integration tests (new).\n- 2 `cli_init` integration tests (new).\n- Total: 331, all green.\n\n**WIP ladder:**\n\nSingle commit; harness + smoke + init tests + tempdir-root sharing\n+ README docs + todo entry all ride together. The harness alone\nisn't useful without tests; the env-var support isn't useful\nwithout doc; sharing the resolver with the in-process side is the\nnew precedent the user wanted to establish from day one.\n\n### Decisions made during design"
}
```

> AGENT

That's correct behavior, not a bug. RAII `Drop` removes the tempdir when the fixture goes out of scope at test end:

- `tests/common/mod.rs:120-122` — `impl Drop for CliFixture` runs `remove_dir_all(&self.base)`.
- `src/test_helpers.rs` — `impl Drop for Fixture` and `impl Drop for FixturePor` do the same.

A successful test leaves no residue in `$TMPDIR` by design — that's why the README's cleanup recipe targets `-mtime +1` (only stale leaks from SIGKILLed runs survive).

Quick proof they exist *while* the test is running:

> AGENT

Captured: the tempdir `/tmp/vc-x1-cli-test-init-dual-layout-…` exists during the test (caught around iteration 4 ≈ 200ms in), and is gone after the test exits. So Drop is working as designed.

If you want to inspect tempdirs after a test for debugging, options:
1. **Quickest hack** — comment out the `let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(...)` line in the relevant `Drop` impl temporarily.
2. **Cleaner knob (small future enhancement)** — wire a `$VC_X1_TEST_KEEP` env var that suppresses `remove_dir_all` when set. Would land in both `Drop` impls; ~6 lines. Could fit in the same future cycle as the config-extension todo, or as its own one-liner.
3. **Failed test** — failing assertions panic before `Drop`'s ordinary path? No, actually `Drop` still runs on panic during stack unwind, so failed tests *also* clean up. To preserve a failing test's tempdir for inspection, you'd need option 1 or 2.

Want me to add `$VC_X1_TEST_KEEP` support now (small, useful for the iteration phase you mentioned), or capture it as a todo for later?

  "old_string": "/// Resolve the parent directory for test tempdirs.\n///\n/// Priority: `$VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR` (if set and non-empty) →\n/// `std::env::temp_dir()` (= `$TMPDIR` on Unix, else `/tmp`).\npub fn resolve_tmp_root() -> PathBuf {\n    if let Ok(p) = std::env::var(\"VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR\")\n        && !p.is_empty()\n    {\n        return PathBuf::from(p);\n    }\n    std::env::temp_dir()\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Resolve the parent directory for test tempdirs.\n///\n/// Priority: `$VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR` (if set and non-empty) →\n/// `std::env::temp_dir()` (= `$TMPDIR` on Unix, else `/tmp`).\npub fn resolve_tmp_root() -> PathBuf {\n    if let Ok(p) = std::env::var(\"VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR\")\n        && !p.is_empty()\n    {\n        return PathBuf::from(p);\n    }\n    std::env::temp_dir()\n}\n\n/// Whether to preserve test tempdirs across `Drop`.\n///\n/// Returns `true` if `$VC_X1_TEST_KEEP` is set and non-empty.\n/// Consulted by RAII `Drop` impls (`Fixture`, `FixturePor`,\n/// `CliFixture`) to suppress tempdir cleanup for debugging:\n///\n/// ```bash\n/// VC_X1_TEST_KEEP=1 cargo test -- --nocapture\n/// ```\n///\n/// Pairs with `--nocapture` so the per-fixture stderr line\n/// announcing the preserved path is visible.\npub fn should_keep_tempdir() -> bool {\n    std::env::var(\"VC_X1_TEST_KEEP\")\n        .map(|v| !v.is_empty())\n        .unwrap_or(false)\n}"

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/test_tmp_root.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Whether to preserve test tempdirs across `Drop`.\n///\n/// Returns `true` if `$VC_X1_TEST_KEEP` is set and non-empty.\n/// Consulted by RAII `Drop` impls (`Fixture`, `FixturePor`,\n/// `CliFixture`) to suppress tempdir cleanup for debugging:\n///\n/// ```bash\n/// VC_X1_TEST_KEEP=1 cargo test -- --nocapture\n/// ```\n///\n/// Pairs with `--nocapture` so the per-fixture stderr line\n/// announcing the preserved path is visible.\npub fn should_keep_tempdir() -> bool {\n    std::env::var(\"VC_X1_TEST_KEEP\")\n        .map(|v| !v.is_empty())\n        .unwrap_or(false)\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Whether to preserve test tempdirs across `Drop`.\n///\n/// Returns `true` if `$VC_X1_TEST_KEEP` is set and non-empty.\n/// Consulted by RAII `Drop` impls (`Fixture`, `FixturePor`,\n/// `CliFixture`) to suppress tempdir cleanup for debugging:\n///\n/// ```bash\n/// VC_X1_TEST_KEEP=1 cargo test -- --nocapture\n/// ```\n///\n/// Pairs with `--nocapture` so the per-fixture stderr line\n/// announcing the preserved path is visible.\npub fn should_keep_tempdir() -> bool {\n    keep_decision(std::env::var(\"VC_X1_TEST_KEEP\").ok().as_deref())\n}\n\n/// Pure-policy form of `should_keep_tempdir`. Takes the env-var\n/// value (`None` if unset, `Some(\"\")` if set-but-empty) and\n/// returns whether to preserve. Factored out so the decision is\n/// unit-testable without manipulating global env state — env\n/// mutation is not thread-safe and would race with other tests\n/// reading `VC_X1_TEST_KEEP` from `Drop`.\nfn keep_decision(env_value: Option<&str>) -> bool {\n    env_value.map(|v| !v.is_empty()).unwrap_or(false)\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    /// `$VC_X1_TEST_KEEP` unset → no preserve.\n    #[test]\n    fn keep_decision_unset_is_false() {\n        assert!(!keep_decision(None));\n    }\n\n    /// `$VC_X1_TEST_KEEP=\"\"` → no preserve. Empty-string is the\n    /// \"set but explicitly disabled\" form (matches how env vars\n    /// are unset on some shells via `VAR=`).\n    #[test]\n    fn keep_decision_empty_is_false() {\n        assert!(!keep_decision(Some(\"\")));\n    }\n\n    /// Any non-empty value → preserve. Aligns with the conventional\n    /// env-var-as-flag pattern (`VAR=1`, `VAR=yes`, `VAR=true` —\n    /// or even `VAR=0`, since the policy treats *any* non-empty\n    /// value as \"set\").\n    #[test]\n    fn keep_decision_nonempty_is_true() {\n        assert!(keep_decision(Some(\"1\")));\n        assert!(keep_decision(Some(\"yes\")));\n        assert!(keep_decision(Some(\"true\")));\n        assert!(keep_decision(Some(\"0\")));\n    }\n}"
}
```

> AGENT

Two things going on, both shell-mechanical:

1. **`VC_X1_TEST_KEEP` wasn't set in your environment.** The `eprintln!` only fires when the env var is non-empty. Without it, `Drop` just deletes as before — silent.

2. **`eprintln!` goes to stderr, but `|` only pipes stdout.** Even if the env var were set, `rg` would never see the message because the pipe drops stderr.

The full incantation:

```bash
VC_X1_TEST_KEEP=1 cargo test -- --nocapture 2>&1 | rg TEST_KEEP
```

A note worth flagging: leaving `VC_X1_TEST_KEEP=1` exported across many test runs accumulates leaked dirs fast (every fixture × every test = a new dir, none cleaned). The README cleanup recipe handles it — just be deliberate about turning the flag on/off.

- **`keep_decision` unit tests (in `src/test_tmp_root.rs`)** — pure-policy tests. They call `keep_decision(Some("1"))` etc. with explicit `Option` values. They don't read or set `VC_X1_TEST_KEEP`, so they never trigger the `eprintln!` (which lives in `Drop`, behind `should_keep_tempdir()`).

- **CLI keep-test** — the one that *would* set `VC_X1_TEST_KEEP=1`, build a `CliFixture`, drop it, and assert the path survives. **I haven't written that yet.** Stopped after Drop wiring, intended to add it next.

So `cargo test` without the env var produces no `TEST_KEEP` output by design — there's literally no code path in the current test suite that sets the env var. Your manual run (with `VC_X1_TEST_KEEP=1` exported) is the only thing that exercises the keep branch right now.

Adding `tests/cli_keep.rs` next will close that gap — it'll set the env var inside a test and verify the preservation programmatically, so every `cargo test` exercises the keep code path.

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/tests/cli_keep.rs",
  "content": "//! CLI subprocess test: `$VC_X1_TEST_KEEP` preserves the fixture\n//! across `Drop`.\n//!\n//! Single-test file by design — env-var writes are not thread-safe\n//! (`std::env::set_var` is `unsafe` since Rust 1.83) and would race\n//! with sibling tests reading `$VC_X1_TEST_KEEP` from their own\n//! `Drop` impls. Cargo compiles each `tests/*.rs` as its own\n//! binary, so this process is isolated from `cli_init` /\n//! `cli_smoke`.\n\nmod common;\n\nuse common::CliFixture;\n\n/// `$VC_X1_TEST_KEEP=1` makes `CliFixture::drop` skip\n/// `remove_dir_all`. After the fixture goes out of scope, the\n/// tempdir must still exist on disk; the test removes it manually.\n#[test]\nfn keep_env_preserves_fixture_across_drop() {\n    // SAFETY: `set_var` is unsafe due to non-thread-safe libc\n    // implementations. This is the only test in this binary, so no\n    // other thread in this process is reading `$VC_X1_TEST_KEEP`\n    // concurrently. Other test binaries run as separate processes\n    // and don't share env state.\n    unsafe {\n        std::env::set_var(\"VC_X1_TEST_KEEP\", \"1\");\n    }\n\n    let preserved_path = {\n        let fx = CliFixture::new(\"keep-env\");\n        assert!(\n            fx.base.exists() && fx.base.is_dir(),\n            \"fixture base must exist mid-test\"\n        );\n        fx.base.clone()\n        // fx drops here; should NOT remove the tree because\n        // `should_keep_tempdir()` returns true.\n    };\n\n    assert!(\n        preserved_path.exists(),\n        \"VC_X1_TEST_KEEP=1 should have preserved {}\",\n        preserved_path.display()\n    );\n    assert!(\n        preserved_path.join(\"home\").exists(),\n        \"preserved tree should include the home subdir created by CliFixture::new\"\n    );\n\n    // Clean up manually since Drop didn't.\n    std::fs::remove_dir_all(&preserved_path).expect(\"cleanup preserved fixture\");\n\n    // Restore env state. SAFETY: same as above — single-test binary.\n    unsafe {\n        std::env::remove_var(\"VC_X1_TEST_KEEP\");\n    }\n}\n"
}
```

```
720	## Testing
721	
722	The repo ships two flavors of tests:
723	
724	- **In-process tests** — `#[cfg(test)] mod tests { … }` blocks inside
725	  `src/*.rs`. These call library code directly (no subprocess spawn)
726	  and run fastest. The dual / POR fixture tests under
727	  `src/init.rs::tests` build throwaway workspaces by invoking
728	  `init::init_with_symlink` as a function.
729	- **CLI subprocess integration tests** — files under `tests/`. These
730	  spawn the `vc-x1` binary that Cargo built (via
731	  `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1")`) so the full argument-parsing,
732	  exit-code, and stdout/stderr surface is exercised. Each subprocess
733	  gets a `HOME` override so the user's real `~/.config/vc-x1/` can't
734	  leak in or get clobbered.
735	
736	Run everything with:
737	
738	```bash
739	cargo test                 # unit + integration
740	cargo test --lib           # in-process only
741	cargo test --test cli_init # one integration test crate
742	```
743	
744	### Test tempdir location
745	
746	Both test layers create throwaway fixtures under a tempdir. The
747	parent directory resolves in priority order:
748	
749	1. `$VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR` — explicit env override.
750	2. `std::env::temp_dir()` — standard fallback (`$TMPDIR` on Unix,
751	   else `/tmp`).
752	
753	Useful when you want tests on a tmpfs / SSD / project-local path
754	without exporting `TMPDIR` globally:
755	
756	```bash
757	VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR=/dev/shm/vc-x1 cargo test
758	```
759	
760	Fixture directories are named `vc-x1-test-<tag>-<ts>-<n>` (in-process)
761	and `vc-x1-cli-test-<tag>-<ts>-<n>` (CLI). RAII drop removes them on
762	test exit; SIGKILLs / panics in `Drop` can leak — search and clean
763	manually:
764	
765	```bash
766	find "${VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR:-${TMPDIR:-/tmp}}" -maxdepth 1 \
767	     -name 'vc-x1-*test*' -mtime +1 -exec rm -rf {} +
768	```
769	
770	A future enhancement (tracked in `notes/todo.md`) extends the
771	priority chain through `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml` and project-local
772	`.vc-config.toml`.
773	
774	## Contributing
```

  "new_string": "A future enhancement (tracked in `notes/todo.md`) extends the\npriority chain through `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml` and project-local\n`.vc-config.toml`.\n\n### Preserving fixtures for debugging\n\nWhen a test fails and you want to inspect the workspace it built,\nset `$VC_X1_TEST_KEEP`. The RAII `Drop` impls (`Fixture`,\n`FixturePor`, `CliFixture`) skip `remove_dir_all` and print the\npreserved path on stderr:\n\n```bash\nVC_X1_TEST_KEEP=1 cargo test -- --nocapture 2>&1 | grep TEST_KEEP\n```\n\nTwo shell gotchas worth remembering:\n\n- The preservation message goes to **stderr**, so a plain\n  `cargo test | grep ...` won't see it — use `2>&1 |` or write\n  the full output to a file.\n- `--nocapture` is needed to bypass libtest's stdout/stderr\n  capture; without it, the messages get swallowed by the test\n  runner's pretty-printer.\n\n`VC_X1_TEST_KEEP` is a debugging knob — every fixture-creating\ntest in the run leaks its tempdir while it's set. Clean up with\nthe `find` recipe above, or just `rm -rf` the announced paths.\n\n## Contributing"
}

So those 23 dirs are from **in-process** `Fixture` / `FixturePor` tests (used in `src/init.rs::tests`, `src/sync.rs::tests`, `src/push.rs::tests`). The CLI keep-demo from earlier (`VC_X1_TEST_KEEP=1 cargo test --test cli_smoke ...`) only ran 2 CLI tests, which created `vc-x1-cli-test-smoke-*` paths. We cleaned those up after.

> AGENT

Confirmed — fresh `cargo test` from a clean `/tmp` produces **zero leaks**. The 23 dirs you had were stale residue from earlier in the session, not a behavior of the current code. Cleanup looks like:

```
before: 0
after fresh cargo test: 0
```

Most likely cause of the original 23: at some point during this session a `cargo test` ran while `VC_X1_TEST_KEEP` was active (perhaps from a prior shell invocation or a test that set then panicked before unsetting). It's not reproducible now, and the keep-test you just verified explicitly unsets the var on success.

The README's `find … -mtime +1` recipe is conservative — it only catches dirs older than a day. For accumulated debug-session leaks, a more aggressive cleanup is fine:

```bash
rm -rf /tmp/vc-x1-test-* /tmp/vc-x1-cli-test-*
```

(Don't run that while a test is mid-flight, obviously.)

> AGENT

Down to just `/tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log` (a non-fixture log). Decision point:

**The `cfg_tempdir` leak is unrelated to -6.4 in scope**, but it's directly relevant in spirit — we just made tempdir handling a first-class concern with `resolve_tmp_root()` + `should_keep_tempdir()` + RAII `Drop`. Letting one module leak is inconsistent with the precedent.

**Options:**

1. **Fix in -6.4** — replace `cfg_tempdir() -> PathBuf` with a small RAII `CfgTempDir(PathBuf)` struct that:
   - Builds via `resolve_tmp_root().join(format!("vc-x1-cfg-{tag}-{ts}-{n}"))` (now matches the in-process / CLI naming pattern: `vc-x1-cfg-<tag>-<ts>-<n>`)
   - Honors `should_keep_tempdir()` in `Drop`
   - `Deref<Target = Path>` so existing call sites still work
   
   ~20 lines, all in src/config.rs::tests. Brings config tests under the shared infra. Adds 1 unit test asserting Drop cleans up.

2. **Defer** — capture as a todo, keep -6.4 focused on the harness + initial CLI tests it shipped.

My lean: option 1, since (a) it's small, (b) it generalizes the precedent the user explicitly named ("strive to share code"), and (c) catching this leak right after building the `should_keep_tempdir` knob is the natural moment.

Which way?

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/config.rs",
  "old_string": "#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use std::fs;\n\n    fn cfg_tempdir(tag: &str) -> PathBuf {\n        let ts = std::time::SystemTime::now()\n            .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)\n            .map(|d| d.as_nanos())\n            .unwrap_or(0);\n        let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(\"vc-x1-cfg-{tag}-{ts}\"));\n        fs::create_dir_all(&dir).expect(\"mkdir tempdir\");\n        dir\n    }\n\n    fn write_cfg(tag: &str, contents: &str) -> PathBuf {\n        let dir = cfg_tempdir(tag);\n        let path = dir.join(\"config.toml\");\n        fs::write(&path, contents).unwrap();\n        path\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn missing_file_returns_default() {\n        let dir = cfg_tempdir(\"missing\");\n        let path = dir.join(\"nonexistent.toml\");\n        let cfg = load_from(&path).unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(cfg, UserConfig::default());\n    }",
  "new_string": "#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use std::path::Path;\n    use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU64, Ordering};\n    use std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};\n    use std::{fs, path::PathBuf};\n\n    use crate::test_tmp_root::{resolve_tmp_root, should_keep_tempdir};\n\n    /// Per-process counter so same-nanosecond tempdir collisions\n    /// yield distinct paths when tests run in parallel.\n    static COUNTER: AtomicU64 = AtomicU64::new(0);\n\n    /// RAII tempdir for `config` tests — sibling of `Fixture` /\n    /// `FixturePor` / `CliFixture`, scaled to the much smaller\n    /// \"write a config.toml and parse it\" use case. Replaces the\n    /// pre-0.41.1-6.4 `cfg_tempdir(tag) -> PathBuf` helper, which\n    /// had no `Drop` and leaked one dir per test invocation.\n    ///\n    /// Uses the shared `resolve_tmp_root` (`$VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR` →\n    /// `std::env::temp_dir()`) and honors `should_keep_tempdir()`\n    /// (`$VC_X1_TEST_KEEP`) so the same env-var knobs control all\n    /// fixture types uniformly.\n    struct CfgTempDir {\n        dir: PathBuf,\n    }\n\n    impl CfgTempDir {\n        fn new(tag: &str) -> Self {\n            let ts = SystemTime::now()\n                .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)\n                .map(|d| d.as_nanos())\n                .unwrap_or(0); // OK: clock error → 0 is harmless for unique tempdir naming\n            let n = COUNTER.fetch_add(1, Ordering::SeqCst);\n            let dir = resolve_tmp_root().join(format!(\"vc-x1-cfg-{tag}-{ts}-{n}\"));\n            fs::create_dir_all(&dir).expect(\"mkdir cfg tempdir\");\n            Self { dir }\n        }\n\n        fn dir(&self) -> &Path {\n            &self.dir\n        }\n    }\n\n    impl Drop for CfgTempDir {\n        fn drop(&mut self) {\n            if should_keep_tempdir() {\n                eprintln!(\"VC_X1_TEST_KEEP set; preserving {}\", self.dir.display());\n            } else {\n                let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(&self.dir);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Write `contents` to `<dir>/config.toml`. Returns the\n    /// `CfgTempDir` guard alongside the file path so callers can\n    /// keep the guard alive for the test's scope.\n    fn write_cfg(tag: &str, contents: &str) -> (CfgTempDir, PathBuf) {\n        let cfg = CfgTempDir::new(tag);\n        let path = cfg.dir().join(\"config.toml\");\n        fs::write(&path, contents).unwrap();\n        (cfg, path)\n    }\n\n    /// `Drop` removes the tempdir on success: keep the path\n    /// outside the inner scope, drop the guard, and verify the\n    /// directory is gone. Pins the leak fix that landed in\n    /// 0.41.1-6.4.\n    #[test]\n    fn cfg_tempdir_drop_cleans_up() {\n        let path: PathBuf;\n        {\n            let cfg = CfgTempDir::new(\"drop-cleanup\");\n            path = cfg.dir().to_path_buf();\n            assert!(path.exists(), \"tempdir must exist while guard is alive\");\n        }\n        assert!(\n            !path.exists(),\n            \"tempdir must be removed after CfgTempDir drops, got: {}\",\n            path.display()\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn missing_file_returns_default() {\n        let cfg = CfgTempDir::new(\"missing\");\n        let path = cfg.dir().join(\"nonexistent.toml\");\n        let parsed = load_from(&path).unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(parsed, UserConfig::default());\n    }"
}

Same pattern in\n  all three: skip removal + `eprintln!` when keep, otherwise\n  best-effort `remove_dir_all`.\n- New `tests/cli_keep.rs` (single-test binary) exercises the\n  env-var path end-to-end: sets `VC_X1_TEST_KEEP=1` (`unsafe`\n  per Rust 1.83+), creates a `CliFixture`, drops it, asserts\n  the tempdir survives, manually removes, restores env. Lives\n  in its own test crate so the env-write doesn't race with\n  parallel reads in `cli_init` / `cli_smoke` (separate\n  processes, no shared env).\n\n**`cfg_tempdir` leak fix (`src/config.rs::tests`):**\n\n`config.rs`'s test module had a long-standing leak: a private\n`cfg_tempdir(tag) -> PathBuf` helper created\n`/tmp/vc-x1-cfg-<tag>-<ts>/` and never cleaned up — every test\ninvocation that called it (8 tests) leaked one dir. After many\nlocal `cargo test` runs, the user's `/tmp` had ~217 stale\n`vc-x1-cfg-*` dirs.\n\nFixed by replacing the bare `PathBuf` helper with a `CfgTempDir`\nRAII struct:\n\n- Same shape as the other fixtures (counter + `resolve_tmp_root`\n  + `should_keep_tempdir` in `Drop`).\n- New naming convention `vc-x1-cfg-<tag>-<ts>-<n>` aligns with\n  `vc-x1-test-*` and `vc-x1-cli-test-*`.\n- `write_cfg(tag, contents)` now returns `(CfgTempDir, PathBuf)`\n  — caller binds the guard to a `_cfg` slot to keep it alive\n  for the test scope; existing 6 call sites updated 1:1.\n- New unit test `cfg_tempdir_drop_cleans_up` pins the cleanup\n  invariant: capture path, drop guard, assert path is gone.\n\nThis isn't strictly part of the -6.4 brief, but the cycle is\nspecifically about tempdir hygiene and the leak surfaced while\nverifying `should_keep_tempdir` worked. Bringing config tests\nunder the shared infra (rather than letting one module quietly\nleak) keeps the new precedent honest.\n\n**README.md `## Testing` section:**\n\nNew top-level section before `## Contributing`. Documents:\n\n- The two flavors (in-process vs CLI subprocess) and how to run\n  each (`cargo test`, `--bins`, `--test cli_init`).\n- The `$VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR` env var with example usage and the\n  cleanup `find` recipe for SIGKILL leaks.\n- The `$VC_X1_TEST_KEEP` env var with the full\n  `2>&1 | grep`/`--nocapture` incantation and the two shell\n  gotchas (stderr vs stdout, libtest capture).\n- Pointer to the todo for the future config-file extension.\n\n**Test counts:**\n\n- 331 binary unit tests (327 prior + 3 `keep_decision` +\n  1 `cfg_tempdir_drop_cleans_up`).\n- 5 `cli_init` (2 init tests + 3 `keep_decision` via\n  `#[path]`).\n- 4 `cli_keep` (1 keep-fixture test + 3 `keep_decision` via\n  `#[path]`).\n- 5 `cli_smoke` (2 smoke tests + 3 `keep_decision` via\n  `#[path]`).\n- Total: 345, all green. `keep_decision` runs in 4 contexts\n  due to the `#[path]` include; each run is microseconds and\n  also confirms the shared file compiles cleanly in every\n  test crate.\n\n**WIP ladder:**\n\nSingle commit. > DEVELOPER

explain env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1") seems an odd env name

> AGENT

It's a Cargo-specific env var, set automatically by Cargo at **compile time** for integration tests under `tests/`. Quick anatomy:

**Naming:** `CARGO_BIN_EXE_<binary-name>` — the suffix is the literal name of a binary target from `Cargo.toml`. For us that's `vc-x1`, so the var is `CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1` (yes, with the hyphen — Cargo doesn't sanitize).

**When it's set:** only when compiling files under `tests/` (Cargo's "integration test" target type). Not in `src/`, not in `examples/`, not in build scripts. Each binary in the package gets its own `CARGO_BIN_EXE_*` var pointing at where Cargo built that binary for the current test profile.

**Value:** an absolute path to the compiled binary, like `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/target/debug/vc-x1`. Cargo guarantees the binary is built before the test runs, so the path is always valid by the time `env!` evaluates.

**Why `env!` and not `std::env::var`:**
- `env!("FOO")` is a macro that reads `FOO` at **compile time** — the value is baked into the test binary as a string literal. Compile fails if `FOO` is unset.
- `std::env::var("FOO")` is a runtime function — reads whatever's in the process env when called.

For locating the binary, compile-time is correct: the path won't change between compile and run, and we want a hard error if the test target accidentally moves out of the right context (e.g., someone tries to run the harness from `src/`).

**The "odd" feel:** Cargo invents a few env vars like this (`CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR`, `CARGO_PKG_VERSION`, `CARGO_TARGET_TMPDIR`, etc.). They look like environment variables but in practice they're a *protocol* between Cargo and your code, set only when Cargo invokes your build/test. The integration-test pattern of `Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1"))` is the documented Rust idiom — see [The Cargo Book → Environment variables Cargo sets for crates](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/environment-variables.html#environment-variables-cargo-sets-for-crates).

Tradeoff vs alternatives:
- Hard-coding `target/debug/vc-x1` would break in `--release` and on cross-compile.
- Using `which("vc-x1")` would find the system-installed copy (potentially stale), not the freshly-built one.
- `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1")` is the only one that says "the version Cargo just built."

> AGENT

Good catch — the test body doesn't show `env!` directly because the indirection is one layer up. Trace:

```
cli_version_runs (tests/cli_smoke.rs)
  └─ fx.cmd()                    ← CliFixture::cmd
       └─ vc_x1()                ← common::vc_x1 (tests/common/mod.rs:47)
            └─ Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1"))
```

So the test calls `fx.cmd()` → `cmd()` adds the HOME env override and returns the `Command` already pre-loaded with the binary path from `vc_x1()`, which is the only place `env!` is invoked.

**The doc comment is technically correct but reads as a non-sequitur** — a reader looking at just `cli_version_runs` sees `fx.cmd()` and `--version`, no `env!` anywhere. The link isn't visible without chasing two helpers up the call stack.

Two ways to fix:

**(a) Restate the test's actual surface, drop the `env!` reference:**

```rust
/// `vc-x1 --version` exits 0 and prints a line containing the
/// crate name. Smoke check that the harness can spawn the binary
/// Cargo built and the binary runs to completion.
```

**(b) Make the indirection explicit:**

```rust
/// `vc-x1 --version` exits 0 and prints a line containing the
/// crate name. Smoke check that `CliFixture::cmd()` →
/// `common::vc_x1()` → `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1")` resolves
/// to a runnable binary.
```

(a) is shorter and matches the typical "doc comments describe what the test asserts, not the implementation chain." (b) preserves the `env!` mention but only by also naming the helpers it flows through. Lean toward (a) — the harness's `env!` use is documented at `vc_x1()`'s definition; tests don't need to repeat it.

Want me to update with (a), (b), or leave?

Updated. Want me to also re-flow the split doc/attribute (`#![allow(dead_code)]` between the two `//!` blocks at the top) into a contiguous block, or leave that for a separate cleanup?

> DEVELOPER

I tweaked the "dead_code" and why do we have to add the allow(dead_code)?

> AGENT

Each `tests/*.rs` is compiled as its **own separate test crate**, and each one declares `mod common;` which pulls in **everything** from `tests/common/mod.rs`. But individual test crates only use a *subset* of the helpers.

Concrete example with current code:

| Helper | `cli_smoke` | `cli_init` | `cli_keep` |
|---|---|---|---|
| `CliFixture::new` | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| `CliFixture::cmd` | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| `CliFixture::path` | — | ✓ | — |
| `run_ok` | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| `run_err` | — | — | — |

`run_err` is dead in **all three** test crates today. `cmd` is dead in `cli_keep`. `path` is dead in `cli_smoke` and `cli_keep`. And so on.

Without `#![allow(dead_code)]`, each test crate's compilation would emit warnings for the helpers it doesn't reach — three different warning sets, depending on which crate is compiling. The warnings would be noise, not signal: the helpers exist for *future* tests in *other* test crates that haven't been written yet (`run_err` is the obvious one — first error-path test that needs it will use it).

**Why the standard fix is crate-level allow on `common/mod.rs`:**
- Per-item `#[allow(dead_code)]` annotations would have to live on every helper that's dead in any crate — and the set changes as tests get added/removed.
- You can't conditionally allow based on which test crate is compiling — there's no cfg flag for "I'm being included by `cli_smoke`."
- Refactoring to "only include what each crate uses" defeats the purpose of having a shared module.

So the crate-level `#![allow(dead_code)]` at the top of the shared module is the documented Rust idiom for `tests/common/`. It says "this module's contents are intentionally a superset of any one crate's needs; don't warn about that."

The trade-off you accept: the allow also suppresses warnings about *genuinely* dead helpers (ones no test crate will ever use). For a small shared module this is fine — periodic manual review catches accumulated cruft. If the module grows large enough that this becomes risky, the standard escalation is to split helpers into multiple smaller `tests/common/<topic>.rs` files, each gated to where it's actually needed.

> DEVELOPER

Having it in the middle of the doc! comment seems weird, how about putting it above the first use:: statement

> AGENT

Yes, that works — both `//!` and `#![…]` are "inner" pre-item things, so the allow can sit right above the first item (the `use` statements) as long as no items come before it. The two `//!` blocks merge into one contiguous comment, then the attribute, then `use`.

720	## Testing
721	
722	The repo ships two flavors of tests:
723	
724	- **In-process tests** — `#[cfg(test)] mod tests { … }` blocks inside
725	  `src/*.rs`. These call library code directly (no subprocess spawn)
726	  and run fastest. The dual / POR fixture tests under
727	  `src/init.rs::tests` build throwaway workspaces by invoking
728	  `init::init_with_symlink` as a function.
729	- **CLI subprocess integration tests** — files under `tests/`. These
730	  spawn the `vc-x1` binary that Cargo built (via
731	  `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1")`) so the full argument-parsing,
732	  exit-code, and stdout/stderr surface is exercised. Each subprocess
733	  gets a `HOME` override so the user's real `~/.config/vc-x1/` can't
734	  leak in or get clobbered.
735	
736	Run everything with:
737	
738	```bash
739	cargo test                 # unit + integration
740	cargo test --bins          # binary unit tests only (in-process)
741	cargo test --test cli_init # one integration test crate
742	```
743	
744	### Test tempdir location
745	
746	Both test layers create throwaway fixtures under a tempdir. The
747	parent directory resolves in priority order:
748	
749	1. `$VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR` — explicit env override.
750	2. `std::env::temp_dir()` — standard fallback (`$TMPDIR` on Unix,
751	   else `/tmp`).
752	
753	Useful when you want tests on a tmpfs / SSD / project-local path
754	without exporting `TMPDIR` globally:
755	
756	```bash
757	VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR=/dev/shm/vc-x1 cargo test
758	```
759	
760	Fixture directories are named `vc-x1-test-<tag>-<ts>-<n>` (in-process)
761	and `vc-x1-cli-test-<tag>-<ts>-<n>` (CLI). RAII drop removes them on
762	test exit; SIGKILLs / panics in `Drop` can leak — search and clean
763	manually:
764	
765	```bash
766	find "${VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR:-${TMPDIR:-/tmp}}" -maxdepth 1 \
767	     -name 'vc-x1-*test*' -mtime +1 -exec rm -rf {} +
768	```
769	
770	A future enhancement (tracked in `notes/todo.md`) extends the
771	priority chain through `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml` and project-local
772	`.vc-config.toml`.
773	
774	### Preserving fixtures for debugging
775	
776	When a test fails and you want to inspect the workspace it built,
777	set `$VC_X1_TEST_KEEP`. The RAII `Drop` impls (`Fixture`,
778	`FixturePor`, `CliFixture`) skip `remove_dir_all` and print the
779	preserved path on stderr:
780	
781	```bash
782	VC_X1_TEST_KEEP=1 cargo test -- --nocapture 2>&1 | grep TEST_KEEP
783	```
784	
785	Two shell gotchas worth remembering:
786	
787	- The preservation message goes to **stderr**, so a plain
788	  `cargo test | grep ...` won't see it — use `2>&1 |` or write
789	  the full output to a file.
790	- `--nocapture` is needed to bypass libtest's stdout/stderr
791	  capture; without it, the messages get swallowed by the test
792	  runner's pretty-printer.
793	
794	`VC_X1_TEST_KEEP` is a debugging knob — every fixture-creating
795	test in the run leaks its tempdir while it's set. Clean up with
796	the `find` recipe above, or just `rm -rf` the announced paths.
797	
798	## Contributing
799	
800	Bot-following workflow, commit conventions, and code style are
801	canonical in [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md):
802	
803	- [Versioning during development](CLAUDE.md#versioning) — `-N`
804	  pre-release suffix convention (single-step vs multi-step).
805	- [Commit message style](CLAUDE.md#commit-message-style).
806	- [Commit-Push-Finalize Flow](CLAUDE.md#commit-push-finalize-flow) —
807	  two-checkpoint per-step discipline.
808	- [Code Conventions](CLAUDE.md#code-conventions) — doc comments on
809	  every file / fn / method, `// OK: …` on `unwrap*` calls,
```

> AGENT

Read carefully. Two things:

**1. Typo (worth fixing):** `binaries location` → `binary's location` (possessive apostrophe). "binaries" without `'s` is a plural noun, which doesn't fit here.

**2. Optional tightening:** *"the value in `env!(...)`"* — `env!` is a macro, not a container, so "in" reads slightly off. More natural: *"the value of `env!(...)`"* or *"the path that `env!(...)` returns"*.

Tightened version that keeps your intent (explicit, non-parenthetical):

```markdown
- **CLI subprocess integration tests** — files under `tests/`. These
  spawn the `vc-x1` binary that Cargo built; the binary's location
  is the value of `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1")`. The full
  argument-parsing, exit-code, and stdout/stderr surface is
  exercised. Each subprocess gets a `HOME` override so the user's
  real `~/.config/vc-x1/` can't leak in or get clobbered.
```

Two small structural changes vs your edit:
- `built and the binaries location is` → `built; the binary's location is` — semicolon breaks the run-on sentence cleanly.
- `env!(...) so the full ...` → `env!(...). The full ...` — splits into two sentences so the grammar of the second one stands alone (and lets you read it without backtracking).

Apart from the typo, the original is clear enough — only apply the structural changes if you like them.

> DEVELOPER

It's "in" the environment variable returned by a macro it's didn't fabricate it, some macro's create things so being explicit the source of truth is the environment variable, correct?

> AGENT

You're right and I was being too rigid about "in" — semantically the source of truth IS the environment variable; the macro is just the read mechanism. Your "in" carries that meaning intentionally.

The reason "in `env!(...)`" reads slightly off is purely shape: `env!(...)` is *call notation* (a macro invocation), not the env var itself, so "in" applied to a function-call-looking thing feels off. But the *meaning* is right.

Two ways to keep your intent and dodge the shape issue:

**(a) Name the env var directly, mention `env!` as the access mechanism:**
```markdown
the binary's location is the value of the `CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1`
env var, which `env!()` reads at compile time
```

**(b) Lean into "in":**
```markdown
the binary's location is in the env var read by
`env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1")`
```

(a) makes the env-var-as-source-of-truth point loudest. (b) is closer to your phrasing.

If you do want to keep your version more or less as-is, the only must-fix is the typo:
> `binaries location` → `binary's location`

The "in" debate is a stylistic call, not a correctness one. You picked it deliberately to convey "env var is the real source," which is true.

> DEVELOPER

It reads it at runtime or compile time, I would guess it variable is read at runtime passing the contents as the executable to run?

> AGENT

Compile time, not runtime. This is the core distinction between the two `env`-named things in Rust:

| | When it reads | If unset |
|---|---|---|
| `env!("FOO")` (macro) | At **compile time** | Compile error |
| `std::env::var("FOO")` (function) | At **runtime** | Returns `Err` |

For `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1")`:

1. When Cargo builds the test binary (e.g. `tests/cli_smoke.rs`), Cargo sets `CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1=/path/to/target/debug/vc-x1` in the *compiler's* environment.
2. `rustc` expands the `env!` macro, reads that env var, and **substitutes the value as a string literal** into the generated code.
3. The compiled test binary has the literal path baked in as a `&'static str`. There's no env var lookup at runtime — the path is just sitting in the data segment.
4. When `cargo test` runs the binary, `Command::new("/path/to/target/debug/vc-x1")` uses the already-embedded string.

**Why `env!` instead of `std::env::var`** for this use case:
- **Compile-time guarantee** — if the build context isn't right (e.g., someone tries to build the test outside Cargo), it fails at compile time with a clear error, rather than at runtime with a missing-env-var panic.
- **No runtime cost** — no env lookup, no `String` allocation; just a constant `&str`.
- **Standard idiom** — Cargo documents this as the canonical pattern for integration tests locating a built binary.

If you want to verify, you can disassemble or `strings` the test binary — the path will be visible as a literal:

```bash
strings target/debug/deps/cli_smoke-* | grep target/debug/vc-x1
```

It's compiled in, not looked up. So when the README says "the binary's location is in the env var read by `env!(...)`," **the reading happens during compilation; the runtime value is the embedded result.**

> AGENT

There's the proof, in three layers:

**1. Direct embedding** (`cli_smoke`, `cli_init`):
```
/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/target/debug/vc-x1
```
Both test binaries that actually invoke `vc_x1()` carry the path verbatim in their `.rodata` segment. No env lookup happens at runtime — the literal is just sitting there.

**2. Cargo's env-dep marker** (`cli_keep` and all others):
```
# env-dep:CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1=/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/target/debug/vc-x1
```
Even when the function that uses `env!()` is dead-code-eliminated (cli_keep doesn't reach `vc_x1()` — it only constructs a `CliFixture` and never calls `cmd()`), Cargo still records the env var as an *incremental-build dependency* so that if the binary path changes, the crate gets rebuilt.

**3. Runtime check** — the embedded path actually points at a working binary:
```
$ /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/target/debug/vc-x1 --version
vc-x1 0.41.1-6.4
```

So: compile-time read into a `&'static str`, baked into the binary's data segment, plus Cargo's metadata for rebuild correctness. The runtime test process literally has the absolute path in memory before it ever calls `Command::new`.

Doubting Thomas: satisfied? :)

wink@3900x 26-04-30T00:51:21.420Z:~/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/target/debug/deps

> AGENT

Right — that's libtest's standard output, the same harness Rust uses for **all** tests (unit *and* integration). The "looks like a unit test" is half right, half a useful distinction:

**Same harness, different crate type.** Every Rust test binary — whether built from `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` inside `src/` or from `tests/*.rs` — links against `libtest` and produces that exact "running N tests" / "test result: ok." output. There's no separate "integration test runner" — it's libtest all the way down.

**What makes `cli_smoke-*` an integration test binary** is *which crate compiles it*: `tests/cli_smoke.rs` is its own crate, separate from the `vc-x1` binary crate. That's the only structural distinction. Privately-scoped items in `src/` are unreachable from integration test crates (you only see the public API + things explicitly included via `#[path]`).

**Why 5 tests in `cli_smoke-*`:**

```
common::test_tmp_root::tests::keep_decision_empty_is_false
common::test_tmp_root::tests::keep_decision_unset_is_false
common::test_tmp_root::tests::keep_decision_nonempty_is_true
cli_version_runs
cli_help_lists_init
```

- The 3 `keep_decision_*` tests come from `src/test_tmp_root.rs`'s `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` block. They appear in `cli_smoke-*` because `tests/common/mod.rs` does `#[path = "../../src/test_tmp_root.rs"] mod test_tmp_root;`. The included file's `mod tests` block is itself `#[cfg(test)]`, and from `cli_smoke`'s perspective `#[cfg(test)]` is true (it IS a test crate), so the inner tests get compiled in here too.
- The 2 `cli_*` tests are the actual subprocess tests defined in `tests/cli_smoke.rs`.

So those 3 `keep_decision` tests run **four times total** across the test suite — once in the binary's own `#[cfg(test)]`, plus once per integration test crate that includes the file (`cli_smoke`, `cli_init`, `cli_keep`). Cost is microseconds × 4; benefit is they keep the shared file honest in every crate context.

(If that duplication ever becomes annoying, the canonical fix is to move the `mod tests { … }` block to a sibling file `src/test_tmp_root_tests.rs` only included from `main.rs`. We discussed it earlier; deemed not worth the complexity for now.)

Direct invocation like you just did (`./cli_smoke-303b884c56cb0b09`) is exactly what `cargo test` does under the hood — it compiles the binary and then runs it. You can pass libtest flags directly:

```bash
./cli_smoke-303b884c56cb0b09 --list             # list tests, no run
./cli_smoke-303b884c56cb0b09 cli_version        # run only matching
./cli_smoke-303b884c56cb0b09 -- --nocapture     # don't capture stdout/err
./cli_smoke-303b884c56cb0b09 --test-threads=1   # serialize
```

Useful for fast iteration after `cargo test --no-run`.

> AGENT

Yes — that's the same path baked into `cli_smoke`'s rodata. Quick tour of `target/debug/`:

| Path | What it is |
|---|---|
| `target/debug/vc-x1` | **The binary.** Hardlinked from `target/debug/deps/vc_x1-<hash>`. This is the path `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1")` resolves to. |
| `target/debug/deps/` | Where Cargo actually puts every compile artifact: the binary, every `tests/*.rs` test binary, every dep's `.rlib`, etc. — each with a content-hash suffix. |
| `target/debug/build/` | Build-script output for crates that have a `build.rs`. |
| `target/debug/examples/` | Example binaries (we have none). |
| `target/debug/incremental/` | Compiler's incremental compilation cache — tracks what was rebuilt. |

You can confirm the hardlink:

  "description": "Show inode equality (hardlink)"
}
```

> TOOL

tool_result
id: toolu_01JbkxzWCAo5XJDjzyF8647f
```
198217036 -rwxr-xr-x 2 wink users 225078336 Apr 29 17:01 target/debug/deps/vc_x1-839187a6a0f6ed31
198217011 -rwxr-xr-x 1 wink users 183657568 Apr 29 17:02 target/debug/deps/vc_x1-a0f1842954589aed
198217036 -rwxr-xr-x 2 wink users 225078336 Apr 29 17:01 target/debug/vc-x1
```

> AGENT

Notice the inodes:

```
198217036  target/debug/deps/vc_x1-839187a6a0f6ed31    (link count 2)
198217036  target/debug/vc-x1                          (link count 2)  ← same inode
198217011  target/debug/deps/vc_x1-a0f1842954589aed    (link count 1)
```

**Two `vc_x1` artifacts in `deps/`** — that's not a mistake:

1. `vc_x1-839187a6a0f6ed31` — the **production binary** (no `#[cfg(test)]`). Cargo also exposes it at `target/debug/vc-x1` via hardlink (link count = 2 confirms the same file under both paths). This is what `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1")` points at.

2. `vc_x1-a0f1842954589aed` — the **unit-test binary** built from `src/main.rs` with `#[cfg(test)]` enabled. This is what runs when you do `cargo test --bins`; it has the 331 in-process unit tests baked into it. Standalone — not hardlinked anywhere outside `deps/`.

That's why earlier when you saw `Running unittests src/main.rs (target/debug/deps/vc_x1-a0f1842954589aed)` it pointed at the second hash, distinct from the binary you're now running with `./vc-x1`.

`cargo test` orchestrates running multiple binaries:
- 1 unit-test binary (`vc_x1-<hash>` — the `#[cfg(test)]` version)
- N integration test binaries (`cli_smoke-<hash>`, `cli_init-<hash>`, `cli_keep-<hash>`, …)
- Each integration test binary internally spawns the *production* `vc_x1-<hash>` (the hardlink target) for the subprocess tests.

Three different roles, all visible in `target/debug/deps/` if you list with `--all`.

wink@3900x 26-04-30T00:55:48.544Z:~/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/target/debug


correct

> AGENT

Yes — `./deps/cli_init-c41a3b94619ccd59` is the integration test binary for `tests/cli_init.rs`. You can run it directly the same way you ran cli_smoke.

The four files split into:

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `cli_init-c41a3b94619ccd59` | The actual executable (current build) |
| `cli_init-c41a3b94619ccd59.d` | Dependency manifest for that executable |
| `cli_init-3712a95c12610deb.d` | Stale dependency manifest from a prior compilation |
| `cli_init-72e5814b02c2e155.d` | Stale dependency manifest from another prior compilation |

**`.d` files** (`*.d`) are Makefile-format lists that tell Cargo which source files the artifact depends on, so incremental rebuilds know what to redo. Cargo writes one per artifact. Sometimes the matching executable gets cleaned up (or rewritten under a new hash) but the `.d` lingers — harmless, just stale metadata.

You can run the integration test directly:

```bash
./deps/cli_init-c41a3b94619ccd59             # all 5 tests
./deps/cli_init-c41a3b94619ccd59 cli_init    # only matching tests
./deps/cli_init-c41a3b94619ccd59 --list      # list, no run
```

It'll spawn `target/debug/vc-x1` (the production binary) for each subprocess test — exactly what `cargo test` does, just without Cargo's wrapper around it.

The literal IS there — `strings` is just gluing it to its neighbors because Rust's `&'static str`s are stored back-to-back in rodata with no terminators (length-prefixed, not null-terminated). Look closely at this line:

```
mkdir cli fixture home/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/target/debug/vc-x1spawn vc-x1
└──────── A ───────┘└─────────────────── B ───────────────────────┘└─── C ───┘
```

Three separate Rust string literals concatenated by `strings` because they were placed adjacently in the binary:

| Slice | Source |
|---|---|
| **A** `"mkdir cli fixture home"` | `tests/common/mod.rs:106` — `.expect("mkdir cli fixture home")` |
| **B** `"/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/target/debug/vc-x1"` | `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1")` — exactly the literal we baked in |
| **C** `"spawn vc-x1"` | `tests/common/mod.rs:65` — `.expect("spawn vc-x1")` |

Boundary clue: **A** ends in "home", and **B** begins with `/home/...` — so visually it reads as one path "home/home/...", but the `home` belongs to A's "fixture home" message and the `/home/...` is B starting its absolute path.

--
 1b040 70726773 2f727573 742f7663 2d78312f  prgs/rust/vc-x1/
 1b050 74617267 65742f64 65627567 2f76632d  target/debug/vc-

> AGENT

There's the smoking gun — raw bytes in `.rodata`:

```
1b040 prgs/rust/vc-x1/
1b050 target/debug/vc-
1b060 x1spawn vc-x1\0\0\0
```

You can see:
- The path runs from `prgs/...` (start of B continuing from a previous chunk) to `vc-x1` ending at byte position 49 of 0x1b060.
- **No separator** between the path's `vc-x1` and the next string `spawn vc-x1` — they're packed tight.
- **Three null bytes** appear AFTER `spawn vc-x1` — that's just alignment padding before the next data, not a string terminator.

So the rules:

| Tool | What it shows | Good for |
|---|---|---|
| `strings binary` | Contiguous printable runs | Quick eyeball but can over-glue |
| `grep -aoE 'PATTERN' binary` | Anchored byte match | Verifying a specific string is present |
| `objdump -s -j .rodata` | Hex+ASCII dump of read-only data | Forensic — see exactly where bytes live |

You were right that `strings` was misleading; it didn't lie, but its output looks like one giant string when the underlying bytes are three separate literals. `objdump` removes the ambiguity.

So: **`env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1")` is in the binary**, exactly where we said it is. Doubting Thomas: even more satisfied? :)

> DEVELOPER

it looks to me like the string is: "/home/.../vc-x1spawn"

> AGENT

That's a great observation — and it raises the deeper question: **how does Rust know where the string ends if there's no separator?**

The answer: in Rust, `&'static str` is a **fat pointer** — two `usize`s, *not* a C-style nul-terminated string:

```
&'static str  =  (data_ptr, length)
                  ↑          ↑
                  where      how many bytes
                  it starts  to read
```

The string's **length is part of the reference**, not part of the bytes in memory. When code says `Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1"))`, the compiler emits something like:
- `data_ptr = 0x...1b03X`  (start of `/home/...`)
- `length = 50`  (exactly the length of the path, not including `spawn`)

`Command::new` reads exactly 50 bytes from that pointer. The "spawn vc-x1" bytes are *physically adjacent* in rodata, but they're a **different string** with its **own** `(pointer, length)` pair pointing at the `s` of `spawn`.

So the rodata layout is:

```
... data_for_string_A | data_for_string_B | data_for_string_C ...
    "mkdir cli ..."     "/home/.../vc-x1"   "spawn vc-x1"
    ↑ (ptrA, lenA)      ↑ (ptrB, lenB)      ↑ (ptrC, lenC)
```

The pointers and lengths are stored elsewhere (in the code that uses these strings, or in vtables / consts). `strings` and `objdump -s -j .rodata` only show the raw bytes — they can't reconstruct the (pointer, length) pairs that delimit the logical strings.

This packing is a deliberate optimization. The compiler sometimes goes even further — if string `"foobar"` and `"bar"` both exist, it can use the same bytes in memory and just give `"bar"` a pointer 3 bytes into `"foobar"` with length 3. Saves space.

**Empirical proof** — write a tiny program that prints the literal:

> AGENT

So:

- The test passes (1 passed; 0 failed) → `Command::new` was given a working path.
- `vc-x1spawn` does **not** exist on disk — if the embedded string were that long, every spawn would fail with "No such file or directory".

Therefore the runtime literal must terminate at byte 50, exactly where `vc-x1` ends. The bytes after (`spawn vc-x1`) are a **separate string literal** that simply happens to share rodata adjacency.

**Takeaway:** in compiled binaries, "where a string ends" is metadata in the calling code, not a property of the bytes themselves. C and Rust differ here:

| Language | Termination |
|---|---|
| C `const char *` | Reader scans for `\0` byte |
| Rust `&str` | Reader uses the `length` field of the fat pointer |

Both pieces of code can correctly extract their "own" string from the same rodata bytes; only the (ptr, length) pair distinguishes them.

> DEVELOPER

Actually, I'm actually contemplating that we change vc-config.toml to be `code = "/"` + `bot = "/.claude"` instead of "relative that why both vc-config.toml are identical, thoughts?

> AGENT

Interesting idea — symmetric configs are appealing. Let me lay out the implications:

**The idea:** instead of each repo's config being self-relative, both sides ship the *same* file:

```toml
# both repos: identical
[workspace]
code = "/"
bot  = "/.claude"
```

vs. current:

```toml
# code:                              # session:
[workspace]                          [workspace]
path = "/"                           path = "/.claude"
other-repo = ".claude"               other-repo = ".."
```

**Benefits:**
- Files are byte-identical → no drift, no per-side rewrites at init time, no diff noise.
- Symmetric naming — neither side is "self" / "other"; both are first-class roles.
- Extensible to a third role (e.g. `docs = "/.docs"`) by adding one key, no config edits to existing repos.
- ochid path resolution stays the same — trailers are still `/<chid>` (code) and `/.claude/<chid>` (session), and both configs map those prefixes to the same repos.

**Costs:**
- "Which one am I?" — current design self-anchors via `path = "/"` or `path = "/.claude"`. Symmetric configs need a different mechanism. Options:
  1. Walk up the directory tree from `cwd` to find the workspace root, then derive role from relative position.
  2. A separate top-level workspace marker (e.g. `.vc-x1/workspace.toml` at code repo root) that lists the repos; per-repo `.vc-config.toml` shrinks or disappears.
  3. Check `pwd` against the configured paths at runtime.
- **Migration story** — every existing workspace has the old shape. Need either a one-shot migration tool or runtime back-compat that accepts both forms.
- **Touches more than -6.5** — would change `init`'s config writes, `validate-desc` / `fix-desc` / `sync` / `push`'s config reads, and the role-detection logic in `chid` / `desc` / `list`. That's most of the codebase.

**Recommendation:**

The change has merit but is a 0.42.0-cycle item, not a -6.5 sub-step. 0.42.0's todo already includes the sum-type refactor and scope-flag work, both of which touch the same surfaces. Bundling the config-schema change there means **one migration**, not two.

For -6.5, I'd:

1. **Stay with the original plan** — `cross_ref_ochids` extraction + eliminate `init_one` + final `create_dual` collapse.
2. **Capture the symmetric-config idea as a todo** under the 0.42.0 cluster, with the design tradeoffs noted (especially "which one am I?" → workspace marker option).

That keeps -6.5 small and focused (which is its whole point), and lines up the schema change with 0.42.0's natural breakage point.

Sound okay, or do you want to fold this into -6.5 / open a parallel cycle for it?

Confirmed — `src/common.rs:625` has `find_workspace_root_from` that walks up looking for `[workspace] path = "/"`. Other helpers (`default_scope`, `other_repo_from_config`, `ochid_prefix_from_config`) read the same config. So the walk-up infrastructure exists; the schema change is a swap of *what* it reads, not adding new mechanics.

That makes dual-format support trivial — one normalizer at the loader:

```rust
fn parse_workspace_config(map: &TomlMap) -> WorkspaceConfig {
    if let Some("/") = toml_get(map, "workspace.code").as_deref() {
        // New symmetric schema
        return WorkspaceConfig::from_symmetric(map);
    }
    if let Some("/") = toml_get(map, "workspace.path").as_deref() {
        // Old self-relative schema
        return WorkspaceConfig::from_legacy(map);
    }
    // ...
}
```

All call sites then use the unified `WorkspaceConfig` struct. Migration cost is bounded to the loader.

**Three placement options:**

**A. Fold into -6.5** — bundle schema change with `cross_ref_ochids` extraction. Synergy: both touch the cross-ref/config surface.
- Pros: one commit covers the full "config + cross-ref" overhaul.
- Cons: -6.5 was meant to be small; expanding it loses the focus that's worked for -6.0 through -6.4.

**B. New step in the -6 ladder, before `detect_state`** — insert as -6.6 (or rename), shift others up:
- `-6.5` cross_ref_ochids + eliminate init_one (current)
- `-6.6` **symmetric `.vc-config.toml` schema + dual-format reader** (new)
- `-6.7` detect_state + InitTargetState (was -6.6)
- ... rest shift +1
- Pros: keeps -6.5 small; `detect_state` (which reads config heavily) gets to use the new schema natively, no dual-format leakage into upgrade-path code.
- Cons: one more step in the ladder.

**C. Defer to 0.42.0** — bundle with sum-type rewrite and scope-flag work.
- Pros: one migration paired with another large change.
- Cons: -6.6 onward (detect_state, upgrade paths) would have to work with the old schema and live with the legacy format until 0.42.0.

**My lean: B.** The walk-up confirmation makes it light enough to do *before* `detect_state` lands, which means the upgrade-path logic in -6.7 onward gets to read the new schema directly (one less thing to dual-format). And it preserves -6.5's tight scope.

> AGENT

Strong direction. The two-line summary captures the destination cleanly. A few thoughts on layering:

**Your interim works, with a small refinement:** if `create_dual = create_repo + create_repo + tweak_config`, then `create_repo` shouldn't be writing config at all — otherwise dual writes role-specific config inside `create_repo` and then immediately overwrites it in `tweak_config`. The clean version is:

```
create_repo  ← pure: mkdir, git init, jj init, optional template, initial commit
                no .vc-config.toml, no .gitignore
write_config_for(role, dir)  ← role-specific config + .gitignore writer
```

Then:

```rust
// Interim (current schema, asymmetric configs)
fn create_por(...)  { create_repo(...);  write_config_for(Por, ...);   /* push_repo */ }
fn create_dual(...) {
    create_repo(/* code  */);
    create_repo(/* bot   */);
    write_config_for(Code, code_dir);
    write_config_for(Bot,  bot_dir);
    cross_ref_ochids(...);
    /* push_repo × 2 */
}
```

```rust
// End-state (symmetric schema, both files identical)
fn create_por(...)  { create_repo(...);  write_workspace_config(...);   /* push_repo */ }
fn create_dual(...) {
    create_repo(/* code */);
    create_repo(/* bot  */);
    write_workspace_config(code_dir);  // same file
    write_workspace_config(bot_dir);   // ditto — could even hardlink
    cross_ref_ochids(...);
    /* push_repo × 2 */
}
```

The migration is then just *what `write_workspace_config` writes* (same content vs different content), not the call structure.

**Why pull config out of `create_repo`:** it makes `create_repo` honest about being role-agnostic. After -6.4 the function takes `info_label: &str` purely for narration *and* `config / gitignore: Option<&str>` to decide what to write — those two params smell like role coupling crept back in. Extracting them clears that smell.

**For -6.5 timing**, two options:

1. **Stay narrow (original -6.5):** just `cross_ref_ochids` + eliminate `init_one` + collapse `create_dual`. Defer the config-extraction to -6.6 (alongside or before symmetric schema).
2. **Expand -6.5 a little:** add the config-extraction now. Then -6.6 (symmetric schema) just changes config *content*, not layering. Smaller two-step lifting overall.

I lean **(2)** — the refactors are mutually reinforcing (`create_dual`'s collapse is even cleaner once config-writing is out), and the config-extraction is small (~30 lines: drop two params from `create_local_repo`, introduce two `write_config_for_*` helpers, update 3 call sites).

Sound right? If yes I'll re-draft the -6.5 plan with the expanded scope and we can review before any code lands.

> AGENT

Got it — `create_local_repo`, my bad on the shorthand. So your design keeps the `config: Option<&str>` parameter and just has POR pass `None`. That's a valid alternative to my "drop the param entirely" suggestion. Either works at the call-site level.

But it raises a question about POR semantics that I want to confirm:

**Current state:** POR writes `VC_CONFIG_APP_ONLY` containing `[workspace] path = "/"`. That's the marker `find_workspace_root_from` looks for to identify the workspace root.

**Under your design (`create_por` calls `create_local_repo(... None ...)`):** POR has no `.vc-config.toml` at all. Implications:

- `find_workspace_root_from` returns `None` for any path inside a POR — POR becomes "not a vc-x1 workspace" from the walk-up's perspective.
- `default_scope(None)` already returns `Scope([Code])`, so dispatch still works.
- Anything that loads `<por>/.vc-config.toml` directly (currently nothing critical, but `validate-desc` / `fix-desc` paths might) would need to handle the absent-file case.
- POR becomes indistinguishable from a plain git repo to vc-x1's workspace-root logic — which is *conceptually* correct (POR *is* a plain repo with no peer to coordinate with).

This is a real semantics change but probably the right one — the workspace marker only earns its keep when there's a workspace (i.e., dual). For a single repo, the marker just adds a file to manage with no readers that benefit from it.

**Two interpretations of what you're saying:**

(a) **You've thought this through and want POR with no config** — POR becomes "plain git repo, vc-x1 just happens to have created it", workspace concept becomes dual-only. I'm on board if so.

(b) **You meant only "create_por doesn't pass a config TO `create_local_repo`"** — the config still gets written, just by a separate step (e.g., `create_por` itself, after `create_local_repo` returns). Like my proposed factoring but with the param retained on `create_local_repo`.

Which one? If (a), the implication is one less file POR has to carry. If (b), the call shape is `create_por = create_local_repo(None) + write_por_config(dir)`, which is functionally equivalent to my original suggestion just with a different boundary.

Either direction the dual side is the same: `create_dual = create_local_repo(None) + create_local_repo(None) + tweak_config(both, role-specific) + cross_ref_ochids + push_repo + push_repo`, collapsing to `+ write_workspace_config(both, identical)` in the end-state.

> DEVELOPER

I'd propose the user decides `vc-x1 init --scope=por --config=<path-to-a-config>` and the default for --config=None so user decide, thoughts

> AGENT

User-decides is good — turns config into a deliberate choice rather than a baked-in assumption. A few things to think through:

**1. Does `--config` apply to dual?**

POR is the easy case: zero or one config file. `--config=<path>` is straightforward.

Dual is messier with the *current* schema — two role-specific configs that have to agree on the cross-ref. So today, `--config=<path>` for dual would either:
- Apply to both sides (only sensible with **symmetric schema** — both files identical)
- Force two flags (`--config-code=<path>` `--config-bot=<path>`) — awkward CLI
- Be rejected for dual (`--config` is `--scope=por` only) — clean separation, but users with the *symmetric* schema would expect it to work

I'd argue: **make `--config` POR-only until symmetric schema lands**, then the dual case becomes "both sides write the same file" and `--config` extends naturally.

**2. Is a built-in template needed, or do users always BYOC?**

If `--config` defaults to `None` and POR has no config, then **most users won't ever write one** — they don't know what should go in it, and the typical case (single repo, just track changes) doesn't need anything. That's fine if "no config" is a fully-supported state.

If we *do* want to make it easy for users who want the workspace marker (e.g., for tools that key off `find_workspace_root_from`), a `--config=default` short form (or some shipped template at `~/.config/vc-x1/templates/por.toml`) would help. Otherwise users have to write a TOML file from scratch to get the same thing today's POR gives them automatically.

Three policy choices for "how easy is it to get a config":
- **(A) BYOC only** — `--config=<path>` always means a user-supplied file. Users without one get nothing. Simplest CLI; more friction for users who want the marker.
- **(B) `--config=default`** — a sentinel that means "use vc-x1's shipped POR template". Works without a file on disk.
- **(C) `--with-config`** — boolean flag, no value, writes the shipped template. `--config=<path>` for custom.

I lean **(C)** — two clearly distinct flags, neither requires the user to know the template content.

**3. Sequencing relative to -6.5**

`--config` is a new CLI feature, not a refactor. I'd keep -6.5 a pure refactor (cross_ref_ochids + eliminate init_one + extract config-writing from `create_local_repo`) and put `--config` in a separate step. Two clean directions:

- **(α) `--config` lands in the same -6.6 as symmetric schema** — they're the right pair (the schema makes the dual case clean, and the flag becomes uniformly applicable).
- **(β) `--config` as -6.5.5 (POR-only), symmetric schema as -6.6, expand to dual in -6.7** — three small steps instead of one bigger one.

I'd lean (α) for the same reason as bundling symmetric schema with `--config` makes them naturally cohesive.

**Updated proposed -6 ladder:**

```
-6.5  cross_ref_ochids extract + eliminate init_one + extract
      config-writing from create_local_repo (current)
-6.6  symmetric .vc-config.toml schema (dual-format reader) +
      --config flag + --with-config flag (NEW)
-6.7  detect_state + InitTargetState (was -6.6)
... rest shifted
```

Sound right? If so I'll re-draft the ladder in `notes/todo.md` with the expanded scope, then proceed with -6.5's refactor work.

> AGENT

Agreed:

**`--config` design (POR-only, lands in -6.6):**

```
vc-x1 init --scope=por                          # no config (default)
vc-x1 init --scope=por --config=none            # explicit "no config" (same as default)
vc-x1 init --scope=por --config=<path>          # copy <path> verbatim into the new repo
```

- `--config=none` is the default; `none` is a literal sentinel (treated case-insensitively or strictly — your call).
- `--config=<path>` copies the file at `<path>` to `<work>/.vc-config.toml`. Errors fatally if the path doesn't exist, isn't a regular file, or contains invalid TOML (cheap to validate at parse time).
- BYOC only — no shipped templates, no `--with-config` boolean.
- Rejected for `--scope=code,bot` (`--config is meaningful only with --scope=por`).

**Updated -6 ladder:**

| Step | Scope | Status |
|---|---|---|
| -6.5 | cross_ref_ochids extract + eliminate init_one + **extract config-writing from `create_local_repo`** + final create_dual collapse | current |
| -6.6 | **`--config=none\|<path>` flag (POR)** + **symmetric `.vc-config.toml` schema (dual)** + dual-format reader for back-compat | new |
| -6.7 | detect_state + InitTargetState enum + unit tests | was -6.6 |
| -6.8 | wire detect_state into dispatch | was -6.7 |
| -6.9 | PorWithPeerPor upgrade | was -6.8 |

**Split rationale:**
- -6.5 stays a pure refactor — no behavior change visible to users. Config-writing gets pulled out of `create_local_repo` (so `create_local_repo`'s `config`/`gitignore` params disappear, callers do their own writes), but POR still writes `VC_CONFIG_APP_ONLY` and dual still writes the asymmetric pair. Tests should pass identically.
- -6.6 is the user-visible change: `--config` flag, POR's default goes from "writes APP_ONLY" to "writes nothing", dual moves to symmetric schema. Loader gains a dual-format read so existing workspaces keep working until they're re-init'd or migrated.

Sound? If so I'll update `notes/todo.md` with this ladder, then start the -6.5 work.

28	/// Create a new local repo at `target`. Returns the chid of the
29	/// new initial commit (`jj @-`).
30	///
31	/// Performs:
32	/// - Creates `target` (and its parent if needed).
33	/// - `git init` + `jj git init --colocate`.
34	/// - Optionally writes `.vc-config.toml` and/or `.gitignore`.
35	/// - Optionally copies a template tree, rewriting any
36	///   `README.md`'s first line to `# <name>`.
37	/// - Commits `Initial commit` (with an `ochid: /none` trailer
38	///   when `ochid_strategy` is `Placeholder`).
39	///
40	/// Parameters:
41	/// - `target` — destination directory for the new repo. Created
42	///   (along with its parent if needed); must not already exist
43	///   as a populated repo.
44	/// - `info_label` — narration tag (`"code"`, `"bot"`, `"scratch"`,
45	///   etc.); appears in `info!()` lines.
46	/// - `config` — optional `.vc-config.toml` contents. `Some(s)`
47	///   writes the file; `None` skips. Useful for upgrade paths
48	///   that leave config in place, or for scratch repos that want
49	///   a bare git+jj init without project-specific files.
50	/// - `gitignore` — optional `.gitignore` contents; same
51	///   `Some`/`None` semantics as `config`.
52	/// - `template` — optional source dir. When present, copied
53	///   recursively (non-hidden only) and any `README.md`'s first
54	///   line is rewritten to `# <name>`.
55	/// - `name` — repo name used by the README rewrite.
56	/// - `ochid_strategy` — initial-commit message policy.
57	#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
58	pub fn create_local_repo(
59	    target: &Path,
60	    info_label: &str,
61	    config: Option<&str>,
62	    gitignore: Option<&str>,
63	    template: Option<&Path>,
64	    name: &str,
65	    ochid_strategy: OchidStrategy,
66	) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
67	    info!(
68	        "Creating local repo {info_label} directory at {}",
69	        target.display()
70	    );
71	
72	    if let Some(parent) = target.parent() {
73	        mkdir_p(parent)?;
74	    }
75	    mkdir_p(target)?;
76	
77	    info!("Initializing {info_label} repo (git + jj)...");
78	    run("git", &["init"], target)?;
79	    info!("colocate jj atop the {info_label} git repo");
80	    run("jj", &["git", "init", "--colocate"], target)?;
81	
82	    if config.is_some() || gitignore.is_some() {
83	        info!("Writing {info_label} config files...");
84	        if let Some(c) = config {
85	            write_file(&target.join(".vc-config.toml"), c)?;
86	        }
87	        if let Some(g) = gitignore {
88	            write_file(&target.join(".gitignore"), g)?;
89	        }
90	    }
91	
92	    if let Some(t) = template {
93	        info!(
94	            "Copying {info_label} template: {} -> {}",
95	            t.display(),
96	            target.display()
97	        );
98	        copy_template_recursive(t, target)?;
99	        rewrite_readme_first_line(target, name)?;
100	    }
101	
102	    let msg = match ochid_strategy {
103	        OchidStrategy::None => "Initial commit",
104	        OchidStrategy::Placeholder => "Initial commit\n\nochid: /none",
105	    };
106	    info!("Committing {info_label}...");
107	    run("jj", &["commit", "-m", msg], target)?;
108	
109	    let chid = jj_chid("@-", target)?;
110	
111	    info!(
112	        "Created local repo {info_label} directory at {} chid = {chid}",
113	        target.display()
114	    );
115	    Ok(chid)
116	}

{info_label} repo (git + jj)...\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"init\"], target)?;\n    info!(\"colocate jj atop the {info_label} git repo\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"], target)?;\n\n    if config.is_some() || gitignore.is_some() {\n        info!(\"Writing {info_label} config files...\");\n        if let Some(c) = config {\n            write_file(&target.join(\".vc-config.toml\"), c)?;\n        }\n        if let Some(g) = gitignore {\n            write_file(&target.join(\".gitignore\"), g)?;\n        }\n    }\n\n    if let Some(t) = template {\n        info!(\n            \"Copying {info_label} template: {} -> {}\",\n            t.display(),\n            target.display()\n        );\n        copy_template_recursive(t, target)?;\n        rewrite_readme_first_line(target, name)?;\n    }\n\n    let msg = match ochid_strategy {\n        OchidStrategy::None => \"Initial commit\",\n        OchidStrategy::Placeholder => \"Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /none\",\n    };\n    info!(\"Committing {info_label}...\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"commit\", \"-m\", msg], target)?;\n\n    let chid = jj_chid(\"@-\", target)?;\n\n    info!(\n        \"Created local repo {info_label} directory at {} chid = {chid}\",\n        target.display()\n    );\n    Ok(chid)\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Create a new local repo at `target`. Returns the chid of the\n/// new initial commit (`jj @-`).\n///\n/// Performs:\n/// - Creates `target` (and its parent if needed).\n/// - `git init` + `jj git init --colocate`.\n/// - Optionally copies a template tree, rewriting any\n///   `README.md`'s first line to `# <name>`.\n/// - Commits `Initial commit` (with an `ochid: /none` trailer\n///   when `ochid_strategy` is `Placeholder`).\n///\n/// Role-agnostic — does not write `.vc-config.toml` or\n/// `.gitignore`. Callers that need workspace metadata write it\n/// after this returns (e.g. `init`'s POR/dual orchestrators\n/// add their schema-specific configs separately).\n///\n/// Parameters:\n/// - `target` — destination directory for the new repo. Created\n///   (along with its parent if needed); must not already exist\n///   as a populated repo.\n/// - `info_label` — narration tag (`\"code\"`, `\"bot\"`, `\"scratch\"`,\n///   etc.); appears in `info!()` lines.\n/// - `template` — optional source dir. When present, copied\n///   recursively (non-hidden only) and any `README.md`'s first\n///   line is rewritten to `# <name>`.\n/// - `name` — repo name used by the README rewrite.\n/// - `ochid_strategy` — initial-commit message policy.\npub fn create_local_repo(\n    target: &Path,\n    info_label: &str,\n    template: Option<&Path>,\n    name: &str,\n    ochid_strategy: OchidStrategy,\n) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    info!(\n        \"Creating local repo {info_label} directory at {}\",\n        target.display()\n    );\n\n    if let Some(parent) = target.parent() {\n        mkdir_p(parent)?;\n    }\n    mkdir_p(target)?;\n\n    info!(\"Initializing {info_label} repo (git + jj)...\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"init\"], target)?;\n    info!(\"colocate jj atop the {info_label} git repo\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"], target)?;\n\n    if let Some(t) = template {\n        info!(\n            \"Copying {info_label} template: {} -> {}\",\n            t.display(),\n            target.display()\n        );\n        copy_template_recursive(t, target)?;\n        rewrite_readme_first_line(target, name)?;\n    }\n\n    let msg = match ochid_strategy {\n        OchidStrategy::None => \"Initial commit\",\n        OchidStrategy::Placeholder => \"Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /none\",\n    };\n    info!(\"Committing {info_label}...\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"commit\", \"-m\", msg], target)?;\n\n    let chid = jj_chid(\"@-\", target)?;\n\n    info!(\n        \"Created local repo {info_label} directory at {} chid = {chid}\",\n        target.display()\n    );\n    Ok(chid)\n}"
}

1157	///
1158	/// Composes:

1219	/// Create-from-empty orchestrator for `--scope=code,bot` (dual).
1220	///
1221	/// Composes:
1222	/// - `create_local_repo` for code side (`OchidStrategy::Placeholder`).
1223	/// - `create_local_repo` for session side (`OchidStrategy::Placeholder`).
1224	/// - Step 6: cross-reference ochids using both returned chids.
1225	/// - `push_repo` for session side (steps 7, 8, 10).
1226	/// - `push_repo` for code side (steps 7, 9, 10) with
1227	///   `clean_exclude = Some(".claude")` so the nested session
1228	///   repo survives the code-side clean.
1229	/// - Step 11: install symlink (when `create_symlink`).
1230	fn create_dual(
1231	    args: &InitArgs,
1232	    plan: &InitPlan,
1233	    templates: Option<(PathBuf, Option<PathBuf>)>,
1234	    visibility: &str,
1235	    create_symlink: bool,
1236	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
1237	    #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
1238	    let session_dir = plan.session_dir.as_ref().unwrap(); // OK: scope=code,bot ⇒ session_dir set
1239	    #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]

1243	
1244	    let (code_template, bot_template) = match templates.as_ref() {
1245	        Some((c, b)) => (Some(c.as_path()), b.as_deref()),
1246	        None => (None, None),
1247	    };
1248	
1249	    // Steps 1-5 per side: code first, then bot.
1250	    let code_chid = create_local_repo(

1253	        Some(VC_CONFIG_CODE),
1254	        Some(GITIGNORE_CODE),
1255	        code_template,
