1264	        bot_template,
1265	        session_name,
1266	        OchidStrategy::Placeholder,
1267	    )?;
1268	
1269	    // Step 6: ochid cross-references (rewrites both placeholders)

After the rewrite, each commit's\n/// ochid trailer is a workspace-root-relative path: the code side\n/// points at `/.claude/<session_chid>`; the session side points at\n/// `/<code_chid>`.\n///\n/// Parameters:\n/// - `code_dir` — code repo on disk; receives `/.claude/<chid>`.\n/// - `code_chid` — code-side initial-commit chid; embedded into\n///   the session-side trailer.\n/// - `session_dir` — session repo on disk; receives `/<chid>`.\n/// - `session_chid` — session-side initial-commit chid; embedded\n///   into the code-side trailer.\npub fn cross_ref_ochids(\n    code_dir: &Path,\n    code_chid: &str,\n    session_dir: &Path,\n    session_chid: &str,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    info!(\"Setting ochid cross-references...\");\n    let code_desc = format!(\"Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /.claude/{session_chid}\");\n    let session_desc = format!(\"Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /{code_chid}\");\n\n    debug!(\"code side: rewrite initial commit's ochid to point at session chid\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"describe\", \"@-\", \"-m\", &code_desc], code_dir)?;\n    debug!(\"session side: rewrite initial commit's ochid to point at code chid\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"describe\", \"@-\", \"-m\", &session_desc], session_dir)?;\n\n    debug!(\"surface post-describe git hashes for the debug log\");\n    let hash = run(\"git\", &[\"rev-parse\", \"HEAD\"], code_dir)?;\n    debug!(\"code repo: chid={code_chid} hash={hash}\");\n    let hash = run(\"git\", &[\"rev-parse\", \"HEAD\"], session_dir)?;\n    debug!(\".claude:   chid={session_chid} hash={hash}\");\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {"
}

> AGENT

Thoughts on the mechanics:

- `jj new -R .` finalizes the current `@` (containing sub-units 1+2 + the todo.md ladder edits) as `@-`, creates a fresh empty `@` on top. Sub-units (3) and (4) land in the new `@`.
- At close-out: `jj squash -R .` folds `@` into `@-` (one consolidated commit), then `vc-x1 push` for the final commit message + remote.
- Description handling: `@-` will be empty-titled after `jj new`. Simplest is to leave it empty during the substep phase — at close-out `jj squash` will combine empty-into-`@`'s text (or open `$EDITOR` if both have content). The final commit message comes from `vc-x1 push` anyway, so intermediate descriptions are throwaway.
- Caveat: only the app repo gets the new `@`. The `.claude` repo keeps accumulating session data on its current `@` — push's `finalize-claude` stage handles that on close-out as usual.
- Reviewability win: when you `jj diff -R .` after I finish (3), it shows only (3)'s changes (against `@-` which now holds 1+2). Same for (4). Much cleaner than the cumulative diff you have now.

If that all looks right, I'll run `jj new -R .` and then start sub-unit (3).

> DEVELOPER

I suggest:
`jj new -R .` or just `jj new` do ss3
`jj new ` do ss4
then squash @ & @- onto @-- as part of the close out

thoughts

> AGENT

That works. The close-out "squash @ & @- onto @--" is just two default `jj squash` calls — first folds ss4 (`@`) into ss3 (`@-`), second folds ss3+ss4 (`@-` after step 1) into ss1+2 (`@--` originally). Each `jj squash` with no args defaults to `@` → `@-`, so:

```
jj squash -R .        # ss4 → ss3
jj squash -R .        # (ss3+ss4) → (ss1+2)
```

Result: one consolidated commit with all four sub-units, then `vc-x1 push` for the final message + remote.

One small note: descriptions on the intermediate `@` commits will be empty by default — that's fine, since `jj squash` only opens `$EDITOR` when both sides have non-empty descriptions, and `vc-x1 push` collects the real message at the end.

> DEVELOPER

I want to get rid of the "Step X" nomenclature it's a nice short reference name, but order can change over time so the it isn't consistent. Maybe we have a one or two word "title" for each so it's easier to reference, that title doesn't need to change unless the "body" changes. Thoughts, no changes!

> DEVELOPER

I want to get rid of the "Step X" nomenclature it's a nice short reference name, but order can change over time so it isn't consistent. Maybe we have a one or two word "title" the ":" followed by a "body", that title doesn't need to change unless the "body" changes. Thoughts, no changes!

> AGENT

Strong agreement. The number is the brittle part — when we add/skip steps the numbers slide and old log lines drift out of sync. The title is the durable thing. Some thoughts:

- **Title candidates** map cleanly to jj/git mental model: `commit`, `bookmark`, `clean`, `cross-ref`, `provision`, `colocate`, `symlink`. They describe *what* is happening, not *where* in a list.
- **Skipped-step lines lose their purpose** without numbers. `info!("Step 6: (skipped — no cross-reference in single-repo)")` mostly exists to call out which numbered step is gone. With titles we'd either drop them entirely (cleanest — silence is informative; if `cross-ref` line never appears, there's no cross-ref) or keep them as `cross-ref: (skipped — single-repo)` if the user-visible "POR has fewer steps" signal still matters.
- **`push_repo`'s `step_label_provision: &str` param** becomes vestigial. The only call sites pass `"Step 8"` and `"Step 9"` for the bot vs code provisioning. With titles, both sides would use `"provision"` — at which point the parameter doesn't carry information and could be hardcoded inside `push_repo`.
- **Dry-run preview is different.** `info!("  1. ...", "  2. ...", ...)` is presenting an ordered checklist to the user — numbers earn their keep there. The shift is for execution-time `info!()` lines, not the preview.
- **Tradeoff to flag:** numbers gave a coarse "where am I in the run" signal during long-running output. Titles convey more detail per line but no progress sense. Probably fine — `info!()` lines are sparse enough that progress is implicit.

Worth slotting as -6.7 (or a small standalone after -6.6) since it touches multiple files and benefits from being its own commit.

> DEVELOPER

SG, if numbering does matter in the future we can generate the numbers, but for now I don't think it's worth it. I suggest a title convention is strive for one word if more than one use "-" seperator so it's always "single word" label? Maybe "label" is a better nomenclature than "title", thoughts

> AGENT

All three feel right:

- **"label" over "title"** — label connotes a short tag/key; title implies a sentence-fragment. Logger libs tend to call this kind of thing a tag/label. "Label" reads better.
- **Single-word, hyphen-joined** — works naturally for the candidates I listed: `cross-ref`, `bookmark`, `provision`, `colocate`, `symlink`, `clean`, `commit`. The constraint forces brevity which is the whole point.
- **Numbers on demand** — if a future need surfaces (progress UI, manual reference), a one-pass transform over the label sequence is trivial. No reason to pay per-call-site cost now.

Two small things to flag for whenever -6.7 (or whatever it becomes) actually lands:

1. **Repeated labels.** Step 5 (`commit`) and step 10 (`colocate`) each happen on both code and bot sides; step 10 is also a *re-*colocate after step 7's `git clean -xdf`. Probably best handled by composing label + side into the message body (e.g. `colocate: bot side` or `re-colocate: code side`), not by inventing distinct labels. Decide at implementation.

2. **`info_label`'s relationship to the new label.** `info_label` ("code" / "bot") is already a side-tag. With action-labels, log lines become two-tagged. Cleanest is probably `<action-label>: <side> ...` (`bookmark: code, jj cleanup`) — keeps the durable tag at the start of the line where logs are easiest to grep.

> DEVELOPER

currently the model is all steps for one then the other, if so there could be a "side-title (code|bot)" line and the "labels" could be indented two spaces?

> AGENT

Good idea. A few observations on the actual flow:

**Dual mode is more interleaved than "all of one, then the other".** Reviewing `create_dual`:

```
code:    create_local_repo (mkdir, git init, jj init, template, commit)
bot:     create_local_repo (...)
cross-ref                              ← spans both, no side
bot:     push_repo (bookmark, clean, provision, colocate)
code:    push_repo (...)
symlink                                ← spans both
```

So the "side block" is a real grouping unit (each `create_local_repo` and each `push_repo` invocation), but they don't pair up neatly side-by-side — `cross-ref` separates create from push. That shape is fine for indented output:

```
code:
  commit: ... ochid /none
bot:
  commit: ... ochid /none
cross-ref: setting cross-references
bot:
  bookmark: setting main + jj cleanup
  clean: git clean -xdf
  provision: gh repo create + push
  colocate: jj git init --colocate
code:
  bookmark: ...
  ...
symlink: ~/.claude/projects/<path>/.claude
```

**A few open questions for whenever this lands:**

- **POR:** the side header is redundant when there's only one side. Probably cleanest is to omit headers in POR and emit flat labels — but consistency has a case too (always show `code:`).
- **Helper vs convention:** could enforce shape with a tiny helper like `log_action("code", "bookmark", "set main + jj cleanup")` that owns the indentation, or leave it as a string convention at call sites. Helper is more typing per call but the shape can't drift; convention is lighter but anyone can break it.
- **Where to draw the verbose/quiet line.** Indented blocks add lines. If the default verbosity becomes too chatty, some current `info!` calls might want to drop to `debug!`. Worth assessing once we see the rendered output.

> DEVELOPER

> AGENT

**Real regression introduced in sub-unit (1).** Diagnosis:

Before my refactor, `create_local_repo` wrote `.vc-config.toml` and `.gitignore` *between* jj-init and jj-commit, so those files landed in the initial commit. After my refactor, the helpers (`write_por_config`, etc.) run *after* `create_local_repo` returns — i.e., after the `jj commit` finalizes. Result: config files sit in the new empty `@` as uncommitted changes.

That breaks `push_happy_claude_clean`: the test creates a fixture, asserts `.claude main` doesn't move when there are no pending session changes — but with my refactor, `.claude` *does* have pending changes (the un-committed `.vc-config.toml` and `.gitignore` left behind by `write_session_config`), so push commits them and `.claude main` advances. (Also: a later `git clean -xdf` in `push_repo` would wipe those untracked files entirely on the code side.)

I should also have run `cargo test --bins` after each sub-unit, not just at sub-unit (4). That would have caught it at (1).

**Two design options to fix.** I have a preference but want your call:

1. **Closure hook on `create_local_repo`** — add an `after_prepare: impl FnOnce(&Path) -> Result<…>` parameter. Caller passes `write_code_config` / `write_session_config` / `write_por_config` directly as the hook. One function, one call site per side. Tests pass `|_| Ok(())` for the no-config case.

2. **Split into two functions** — `prepare_local_repo(target, label, template, name)` does mkdir/init/template; `commit_initial(target, label, ochid_strategy)` does the commit + returns chid. Caller chains: prepare → write_config → commit. Three lines per side, but the seam is explicit.

My pref: option 1. The config-write is one specific moment in the lifecycle (after prepare, before commit) and a closure expresses that without extra functions. Either way, the helper extraction stays — the role-specific config logic lives in `init.rs`, not `repo_utils.rs`.

Which do you prefer?

> DEVELOPER

I lean towards (2.) with just 2 alternatives there is no gain to closure at this point, thoughts?

> AGENT

Agreed. Closure earns its keep when there's runtime variation per call — here the only variation is "which string to write," and the split-fns version expresses that with named helpers (`write_por_config` etc.), which is more readable than `|p| { … }` lambdas at the call site. Plus the closure form needs `<F>` + `where F: FnOnce(...)` plumbing in the signature — real syntactic cost to read.

The seam concern (caller forgets to `commit_initial`) is real but mild: the function names make it obvious, and a missing commit would surface immediately in any test that examines the resulting repo state.

**Proposed fix shape:**

- `src/repo_utils.rs` — split into:
  - `pub fn prepare_local_repo(target, info_label, template, name) -> Result<()>` — mkdir, git init, jj init, copy template + readme rewrite
  - `pub fn commit_initial(target, info_label, ochid_strategy) -> Result<String>` — jj commit + returns chid
  - Update tests to chain prepare → commit (no role-config in between since these tests don't exercise that)

- `src/init.rs` — update import + 3 call sites:
  - POR: `prepare_local_repo(...)?; write_por_config(&plan.project_dir)?; let code_chid = commit_initial(..., OchidStrategy::None)?;`
  - Dual code side: prepare → `write_code_config` → commit (Placeholder)
  - Dual bot side: prepare → `write_session_config` → commit (Placeholder)

**Where to land it:** I'll do this as a new `@` (sub-unit 5: "fix split-fns regression from (1)"). At close-out the squash folds all of (1)–(5) into one commit, so the broken intermediate state never appears in the final history — but per-substep diffs stay reviewable.

I'll also add `cargo test --bins` per substep going forward, so a regression like this surfaces at the substep that introduced it.

Permission to run `jj new -R .` and apply the fix?

> DEVELOPER

1	//! Repo lifecycle helpers — creation, finalization, cross-references.
2	//!
3	//! Sibling of `url` (URL/target parsing); this module hosts
4	//! the local-state mechanics that don't depend on URL form. As of
5	//! 0.41.1-6.5 it carries `create_local_repo` (steps 1-5 of the
6	//! init lifecycle), `cross_ref_ochids` (step 6 — rewrite both
7	//! placeholders once each side's chid is known), and the
8	//! `OchidStrategy` policy enum.
9	
10	use std::path::Path;
11	
12	use log::{debug, info};
13	
14	use crate::common::{mkdir_p, run};
15	use crate::init::{copy_template_recursive, jj_chid, rewrite_readme_first_line};
16	
17	/// Initial-commit ochid policy used by `create_local_repo`.
18	///
19	/// - `None` — POR: plain `Initial commit` message.
20	/// - `Placeholder` — Dual: `Initial commit\n\nochid: /none`,
21	///   rewritten via cross-ref `jj describe` once both sides have
22	///   committed and their chids are known.
23	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
24	pub enum OchidStrategy {
25	    None,
26	    Placeholder,
27	}
28	
29	/// Create a new local repo at `target`. Returns the chid of the
30	/// new initial commit (`jj @-`).
31	///
32	/// Performs:
33	/// - Creates `target` (and its parent if needed).
34	/// - `git init` + `jj git init --colocate`.
35	/// - Optionally copies a template tree, rewriting any
36	///   `README.md`'s first line to `# <name>`.
37	/// - Commits `Initial commit` (with an `ochid: /none` trailer
38	///   when `ochid_strategy` is `Placeholder`).
39	///
40	/// Role-agnostic — does not write `.vc-config.toml` or
41	/// `.gitignore`. Callers that need workspace metadata write it
42	/// after this returns (e.g. `init`'s POR/dual orchestrators
43	/// add their schema-specific configs separately).
44	///
45	/// Parameters:
46	/// - `target` — destination directory for the new repo. Created
47	///   (along with its parent if needed); must not already exist
48	///   as a populated repo.
49	/// - `info_label` — narration tag (`"code"`, `"bot"`, `"scratch"`,
50	///   etc.); appears in `info!()` lines.
51	/// - `template` — optional source dir. When present, copied
52	///   recursively (non-hidden only) and any `README.md`'s first
53	///   line is rewritten to `# <name>`.
54	/// - `name` — repo name used by the README rewrite.
55	/// - `ochid_strategy` — initial-commit message policy.
56	pub fn create_local_repo(
57	    target: &Path,
58	    info_label: &str,
59	    template: Option<&Path>,
60	    name: &str,
61	    ochid_strategy: OchidStrategy,
62	) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
63	    info!(
64	        "Creating local repo {info_label} directory at {}",
65	        target.display()
66	    );
67	
68	    if let Some(parent) = target.parent() {
69	        mkdir_p(parent)?;
70	    }
71	    mkdir_p(target)?;
72	
73	    info!("Initializing {info_label} repo (git + jj)...");
74	    run("git", &["init"], target)?;
75	    info!("colocate jj atop the {info_label} git repo");
76	    run("jj", &["git", "init", "--colocate"], target)?;
77	
78	    if let Some(t) = template {
79	        info!(
80	            "Copying {info_label} template: {} -> {}",
81	            t.display(),
82	            target.display()
83	        );
84	        copy_template_recursive(t, target)?;
85	        rewrite_readme_first_line(target, name)?;
86	    }
87	
88	    let msg = match ochid_strategy {
89	        OchidStrategy::None => "Initial commit",
90	        OchidStrategy::Placeholder => "Initial commit\n\nochid: /none",
91	    };
92	    info!("Committing {info_label}...");
93	    run("jj", &["commit", "-m", msg], target)?;
94	
95	    let chid = jj_chid("@-", target)?;
96	
97	    info!(
98	        "Created local repo {info_label} directory at {} chid = {chid}",
99	        target.display()
100	    );
101	    Ok(chid)
102	}
103	
104	/// Rewrite the placeholder `ochid: /none` trailers on both sides
105	/// of a dual-repo workspace so each initial commit points at its
106	/// counterpart's chid (init step 6).
107	///
108	/// Both sides must already have an initial commit shaped by
109	/// `OchidStrategy::Placeholder`. After the rewrite, each commit's
110	/// ochid trailer is a workspace-root-relative path: the code side
111	/// points at `/.claude/<session_chid>`; the session side points at
112	/// `/<code_chid>`.
113	///
114	/// Parameters:
115	/// - `code_dir` — code repo on disk; receives `/.claude/<chid>`.
116	/// - `code_chid` — code-side initial-commit chid; embedded into
117	///   the session-side trailer.
118	/// - `session_dir` — session repo on disk; receives `/<chid>`.
119	/// - `session_chid` — session-side initial-commit chid; embedded
120	///   into the code-side trailer.
121	pub fn cross_ref_ochids(
122	    code_dir: &Path,
123	    code_chid: &str,
124	    session_dir: &Path,
125	    session_chid: &str,
126	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
127	    info!("Setting ochid cross-references...");
128	    let code_desc = format!("Initial commit\n\nochid: /.claude/{session_chid}");
129	    let session_desc = format!("Initial commit\n\nochid: /{code_chid}");
130	
131	    debug!("code side: rewrite initial commit's ochid to point at session chid");
132	    run("jj", &["describe", "@-", "-m", &code_desc], code_dir)?;
133	    debug!("session side: rewrite initial commit's ochid to point at code chid");
134	    run("jj", &["describe", "@-", "-m", &session_desc], session_dir)?;
135	
136	    debug!("surface post-describe git hashes for the debug log");
137	    let hash = run("git", &["rev-parse", "HEAD"], code_dir)?;
138	    debug!("code repo: chid={code_chid} hash={hash}");
139	    let hash = run("git", &["rev-parse", "HEAD"], session_dir)?;
140	    debug!(".claude:   chid={session_chid} hash={hash}");
141	    Ok(())
142	}
143	
144	#[cfg(test)]
145	mod tests {
146	    use super::*;
147	
148	    /// `OchidStrategy::None` produces a plain `Initial commit`
149	    /// message (POR-shape).
150	    #[test]
151	    fn strategy_none_writes_plain_commit() {
152	        let base = crate::test_helpers::unique_base("create-repo-strategy-none");
153	        let target = base.join("work");
154	        std::fs::create_dir_all(&base).expect("mkdir base");
155	
156	        let chid = create_local_repo(&target, "code", None, "scratch", OchidStrategy::None)
157	            .expect("create_local_repo with strategy None");
158	
159	        assert!(!chid.is_empty(), "chid returned");
160	        assert!(target.join(".jj").exists(), "jj initialized");
161	        assert!(target.join(".git").exists(), "git initialized");
162	
163	        let log = run("git", &["log", "-1", "--format=%B"], &target).expect("git log");
164	        assert_eq!(
165	            log.trim(),
166	            "Initial commit",
167	            "OchidStrategy::None uses a plain message"
168	        );
169	
170	        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&base);
171	    }
172	
173	    /// `OchidStrategy::Placeholder` writes the `ochid: /none`
174	    /// placeholder in the initial commit message — what dual mode
175	    /// lands before the cross-ref rewrite in step 6.
176	    #[test]
177	    fn strategy_placeholder_writes_ochid_none() {
178	        let base = crate::test_helpers::unique_base("create-repo-strategy-placeholder");
179	        let target = base.join("work");
180	        std::fs::create_dir_all(&base).expect("mkdir base");
181	
182	        let _chid =
183	            create_local_repo(&target, "code", None, "scratch", OchidStrategy::Placeholder)
184	                .expect("create_local_repo with strategy Placeholder");
185	
186	        let log = run("git", &["log", "-1", "--format=%B"], &target).expect("git log");
187	        assert!(log.contains("Initial commit"));
188	        assert!(
189	            log.contains("ochid: /none"),
190	            "OchidStrategy::Placeholder leaves a placeholder ochid trailer"
191	        );
192	
193	        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&base);
194	    }
195	
196	    /// `create_local_repo` itself never writes `.vc-config.toml` or
197	    /// `.gitignore` — those are role-specific and live with the
198	    /// caller (the POR branch of `init_with_symlink` and
199	    /// `create_dual` in `init.rs`). Verifies the tree contains only
200	    /// `.jj/` and `.git/`.
201	    #[test]
202	    fn does_not_write_vc_config_or_gitignore() {
203	        let base = crate::test_helpers::unique_base("create-repo-no-files");
204	        let target = base.join("work");
205	        std::fs::create_dir_all(&base).expect("mkdir base");
206	
207	        let _chid = create_local_repo(&target, "scratch", None, "scratch", OchidStrategy::None)
208	            .expect("create_local_repo");
209	
210	        assert!(target.join(".jj").exists(), "jj still initialized");
211	        assert!(target.join(".git").exists(), "git still initialized");
212	        assert!(
213	            !target.join(".vc-config.toml").exists(),
214	            "create_local_repo does not write .vc-config.toml"
215	        );
216	        assert!(
217	            !target.join(".gitignore").exists(),
218	            "create_local_repo does not write .gitignore"
219	        );
220	
221	        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&base);
222	    }
223	}
224

  "new_string": "//! Repo lifecycle helpers — creation, finalization, cross-references.\n//!\n//! Sibling of `url` (URL/target parsing); this module hosts\n//! the local-state mechanics that don't depend on URL form. As of\n//! 0.41.1-6.5 it carries `prepare_local_repo` (mkdir + git/jj init\n//! + optional template copy) and `commit_initial` (jj commit of the\n//! prepared tree, returning its chid) for steps 1-5 of the init\n//! lifecycle, `cross_ref_ochids` (step 6 — rewrite both placeholders\n//! once each side's chid is known), and the `OchidStrategy` policy\n//! enum.\n//!\n//! Splitting prepare from commit lets callers write role-specific\n//! files (`.vc-config.toml`, `.gitignore`) into the prepared tree\n//! before the initial commit captures them."

Returns the chid of the\n/// new initial commit (`jj @-`).\n///\n/// Performs:\n/// - Creates `target` (and its parent if needed).\n/// - `git init` + `jj git init --colocate`.\n/// - Optionally copies a template tree, rewriting any\n///   `README.md`'s first line to `# <name>`.\n/// - Commits `Initial commit` (with an `ochid: /none` trailer\n///   when `ochid_strategy` is `Placeholder`).\n///\n/// Role-agnostic — does not write `.vc-config.toml` or\n/// `.gitignore`. Callers that need workspace metadata write it\n/// after this returns (e.g. `init`'s POR/dual orchestrators\n/// add their schema-specific configs separately).\n///\n/// Parameters:\n/// - `target` — destination directory for the new repo. Created\n///   (along with its parent if needed); must not already exist\n///   as a populated repo.\n/// - `info_label` — narration tag (`\"code\"`, `\"bot\"`, `\"scratch\"`,\n///   etc.); appears in `info!()` lines.\n/// - `template` — optional source dir. When present, copied\n///   recursively (non-hidden only) and any `README.md`'s first\n///   line is rewritten to `# <name>`.\n/// - `name` — repo name used by the README rewrite.\n/// - `ochid_strategy` — initial-commit message policy.\npub fn create_local_repo(\n    target: &Path,\n    info_label: &str,\n    template: Option<&Path>,\n    name: &str,\n    ochid_strategy: OchidStrategy,\n) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    info!(\n        \"Creating local repo {info_label} directory at {}\",\n        target.display()\n    );\n\n    if let Some(parent) = target.parent() {\n        mkdir_p(parent)?;\n    }\n    mkdir_p(target)?;\n\n    info!(\"Initializing {info_label} repo (git + jj)...\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"init\"], target)?;\n    info!(\"colocate jj atop the {info_label} git repo\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"], target)?;\n\n    if let Some(t) = template {\n        info!(\n            \"Copying {info_label} template: {} -> {}\",\n            t.display(),\n            target.display()\n        );\n        copy_template_recursive(t, target)?;\n        rewrite_readme_first_line(target, name)?;\n    }\n\n    let msg = match ochid_strategy {\n        OchidStrategy::None => \"Initial commit\",\n        OchidStrategy::Placeholder => \"Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /none\",\n    };\n    info!(\"Committing {info_label}...\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"commit\", \"-m\", msg], target)?;\n\n    let chid = jj_chid(\"@-\", target)?;\n\n    info!(\n        \"Created local repo {info_label} directory at {} chid = {chid}\",\n        target.display()\n    );\n    Ok(chid)\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Initial-commit ochid policy used by `commit_initial`.\n///\n/// - `None` — POR: plain `Initial commit` message.\n/// - `Placeholder` — Dual: `Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /none`,\n///   rewritten via cross-ref `jj describe` once both sides have\n///   committed and their chids are known.\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]\npub enum OchidStrategy {\n    None,\n    Placeholder,\n}\n\n/// Prepare a fresh local repo at `target`: directory + git/jj\n/// init + optional template copy. The working copy is left\n/// uncommitted so callers can drop role-specific files (e.g.\n/// `.vc-config.toml`, `.gitignore`) into the tree before\n/// `commit_initial` captures them in the initial commit.\n///\n/// Performs:\n/// - Creates `target` (and its parent if needed).\n/// - `git init` + `jj git init --colocate`.\n/// - Optionally copies a template tree, rewriting any\n///   `README.md`'s first line to `# <name>`.\n///\n/// Parameters:\n/// - `target` — destination directory for the new repo. Created\n///   (along with its parent if needed); must not already exist\n///   as a populated repo.\n/// - `info_label` — narration tag (`\"code\"`, `\"bot\"`, `\"scratch\"`,\n///   etc.); appears in `info!()` lines.\n/// - `template` — optional source dir. When present, copied\n///   recursively (non-hidden only) and any `README.md`'s first\n///   line is rewritten to `# <name>`.\n/// - `name` — repo name used by the README rewrite.\npub fn prepare_local_repo(\n    target: &Path,\n    info_label: &str,\n    template: Option<&Path>,\n    name: &str,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    info!(\n        \"Preparing local repo {info_label} directory at {}\",\n        target.display()\n    );\n\n    if let Some(parent) = target.parent() {\n        mkdir_p(parent)?;\n    }\n    mkdir_p(target)?;\n\n    info!(\"Initializing {info_label} repo (git + jj)...\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"init\"], target)?;\n    info!(\"colocate jj atop the {info_label} git repo\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"], target)?;\n\n    if let Some(t) = template {\n        info!(\n            \"Copying {info_label} template: {} -> {}\",\n            t.display(),\n            target.display()\n        );\n        copy_template_recursive(t, target)?;\n        rewrite_readme_first_line(target, name)?;\n    }\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Commit the prepared working copy as an initial commit and\n/// return its chid (`jj @-`).\n///\n/// Pairs with `prepare_local_repo`: caller calls `prepare_local_repo`,\n/// optionally writes role-specific files (e.g. `.vc-config.toml`,\n/// `.gitignore`) into the tree, then calls this to capture the\n/// whole snapshot in the initial commit.\n///\n/// Parameters:\n/// - `target` — repo working dir, already prepared.\n/// - `info_label` — narration tag, mirroring `prepare_local_repo`.\n/// - `ochid_strategy` — message policy: `None` writes a plain\n///   `Initial commit`; `Placeholder` writes\n///   `Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /none` for later rewrite by\n///   `cross_ref_ochids`.\npub fn commit_initial(\n    target: &Path,\n    info_label: &str,\n    ochid_strategy: OchidStrategy,\n) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let msg = match ochid_strategy {\n        OchidStrategy::None => \"Initial commit\",\n        OchidStrategy::Placeholder => \"Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /none\",\n    };\n    info!(\"Committing {info_label}...\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"commit\", \"-m\", msg], target)?;\n\n    let chid = jj_chid(\"@-\", target)?;\n    info!(\n        \"Committed {info_label} initial at {} chid = {chid}\",\n        target.display()\n    );\n    Ok(chid)\n}"
}

All 331 tests pass, clippy clean.

**Sub-unit (5) summary:**

- **`src/repo_utils.rs`** — Split `create_local_repo` into:
  - `prepare_local_repo(target, info_label, template, name) -> Result<()>` — mkdir, git/jj init, optional template copy + readme rewrite. Working copy left uncommitted.
  - `commit_initial(target, info_label, ochid_strategy) -> Result<String>` — jj commit, returns chid.
  - Tests now chain `prepare_local_repo` → `commit_initial` (with no role-config write between, since these tests don't exercise that path).
  - Module docstring rewritten as a bullet list of the four exposed items + a note explaining why prepare and commit are separate.
  - `OchidStrategy` doc comment updated to refer to `commit_initial`.

- **`src/init.rs`** — Updated import + 3 call sites:
  - POR: `prepare_local_repo` → `write_por_config` → `commit_initial(OchidStrategy::None)`
  - Dual code: `prepare_local_repo` → `write_code_config` → `commit_initial(OchidStrategy::Placeholder)`
  - Dual bot: `prepare_local_repo` → `write_session_config` → `commit_initial(OchidStrategy::Placeholder)`
  - `create_dual` and `push_repo` doc comments updated to reference the new pair.

The `push_happy_claude_clean` regression is fixed — config files now land in the initial commit instead of being left uncommitted in the post-commit `@`. Ready for review.

lgtm, I like it!

Sub-unit (5) signed off, all five substeps complete. Time for close-out. Plan:

1. **`cargo fmt`** — re-format any drift.
2. **Update `notes/todo.md`** — flip `-6.5 (current)` → `-6.5 (done)` and `(5) (current)` → `(5) (done)`.
3. **Update `notes/chores-08.md`** — add a `### ...(0.41.1-6.5)` section describing the refactor (extract `cross_ref_ochids`, split `create_local_repo` into `prepare_local_repo` + `commit_initial`, extract role-specific config-writing into `init.rs` helpers, eliminate `init_one`, drop stale step-N comments).
4. **Squash the five substep `@`s into one** — three sequential `jj squash`es (using `-r @-` after the first to keep folding the chain toward the base):
   ```
   jj squash -R .          # @ (5) → @- (4),  result: @-=(4+5)
   jj squash -r @- -R .    # @- (4+5) → @-- (3), result: @--=(3+4+5)
   jj squash -r @- -R .    # @- (3+4+5) → @-- (1+2), result: @-=(1+2+3+4+5)
   ```
   Per-substep diffs preserved in op-log; final commit has all of -6.5 as one unit.
5. **Run `vc-x1 push init-clone-refactor`** — propose the commit title + body for your second approval first.

`README.md` doesn't need an update — this cycle is purely a refactor with no user-visible behavior change.

Shall I proceed with steps 1–3 (fmt + the two notes edits) and then pause for review before the squash?

> DEVELOPER

Q first, how did you like separating the steps into individual changes, tracking down the bug by switching back and forth seemed convenient, thoughts as a working model?

> AGENT

Genuinely useful, recommend keeping it. Concrete observations:

**Wins:**

- **Bisection cost dropped to ~30 seconds.** Two `jj edit` jumps (`@---` for -6.4 baseline, `@--` for ss1+2) plus a test run each, and the bug was localized. With cumulative work in one `@`, I'd have had to manually revert chunks or rely on memory.
- **Per-substep diffs read cleanly.** Each `jj diff -R .` after a substep shows only that substep's changes. The cumulative diff from before my refactor of (1) was already noisy enough that *you* flagged it; ss3+ss4 in separate `@`s would have stayed crisp regardless.
- **The fix could be its own (5).** Adding the fix as a sibling substep instead of squashing-and-amending preserves the forensic trail: "(1) introduced the regression, (5) fixed it" is visible in the ladder. Useful for future-me reading op log or commit history (before the close-out squash flattens it).
- **No stash/branch dance.** jj's per-commit semantics make this protocol natural — `jj new` is the only mechanism, no special-case tools.

**Frictions:**

- **`cargo test --bins` per substep was missing.** I only ran build+clippy between substeps and discovered the regression at (4). Should be a per-substep discipline, not a close-out one. Marginal cost (~2s), high regression-catch value.
- **Empty descriptions hurt op-log readability mid-cycle.** Fine for a 5-substep run, but for anything longer a one-line `jj describe -m "ss5: split create_local_repo"` per substep would help. Optional — the substep ladder in `todo.md` already captures the gist.
- **Squash mechanics need a moment.** Three `jj squash` calls with the right `-r @-` positioning isn't hard but isn't muscle memory. Worth jotting the recipe in CLAUDE.md if we adopt this as the default.

**When I'd skip it:** trivially-coupled substeps (rename across two files, single test fix) where the cumulative diff is already small. The overhead doesn't pay back. Reserve it for refactors where substeps are genuinely separable concerns.

**Net:** the bisection win alone paid for the protocol on this cycle. Strong vote to keep as default for multi-substep work, with per-substep `cargo test --bins` added as a hard rule.

in `jj squash -h`:
  -f, --from <REVSETS>           Revision(s) to squash from (default: @)
  -t, --into <REVSET>            Revision to squash into (default: @) [aliases: --to]

> AGENT

Much cleaner — one command instead of three:

```
jj squash --from "@---..@" --into @--- -R .
```

The revset `@---..@` is "everything reachable from `@` that's not reachable from `@---`" — i.e., `@`, `@-`, `@--` (substeps 5, 4, 3). Folds all three into `@---` (substeps 1+2) in one shot. No positioning dance, no chained squashes that I have to reason about.

3. Update `notes/chores-08.md` (add -6.5 section)
4. **`jj squash --from "@---..@" --into @--- -R .`** — single squash
5. Propose commit title + body, then `vc-x1 push init-clone-refactor`

This is worth a CLAUDE.md note under the substep protocol once we adopt it formally. Shall I proceed with steps 1–3?

y, the syntax for the squash is not obvious are you sure about the order, also I think you can use a number/index rather than "---", here is what your internet sib said: https://claude.ai/share/b5149454-e886-426b-9374-e03c472e43c6

> AGENT

Order is confirmed: `--from` is **source** (where changes leave), `--into` is **destination** (where changes land). For our case we want the three top substeps' content to flow down into the (1+2) commit, so:

- `--from "@---..@"` = the three commits @, @-, @-- (everything reachable from @ excluding @---)
- `--into @---` = the (1+2) commit

After the command, @--- has everything; the three intermediate commits become empty (jj typically auto-abandons them).

On the index question: `@---` (literal triple-dash) I'm confident works. The numeric form would be a revset `~` operator if it exists in jj 0.40, but I haven't verified — I usually see the dash-repetition form. Two ways to nail it down:

```
jj help -- 'revsets'           # check syntax doc
jj log -r "@-3" -R .           # smoke test the form
```

If `@-3` (or whichever numeric form) parses, we can swap it in. Otherwise stay with `@---`. Want me to run the smoke test, or just stick with the dash form?

> DEVELOPER

I agree but should --from be "@---..@" or "@..@---". Previously we used --from @- --into @ multiple times, correct?

> AGENT

`@---..@` is correct. In jj revsets (matching git/hg convention) `A..B` means "ancestors of B excluding ancestors of A" — i.e., commits reachable from B but not from A. So:

- `@---..@` = `{@, @-, @--}` ✓ (the three commits we want to fold)
- `@..@---` = `{}` (empty — `@---` is an ancestor of `@`, nothing is reachable from `@---` that isn't already reachable from `@`)

Mnemonic: the older/ancestor commit sits on the **left**, the newer/descendant on the **right**. Same as `git log A..B`.

On the recollection: I think there's a small mix-up. We didn't run `--from @- --into @` — we ran the default `jj squash` (no args), which is `--from @ --into @-`. So source was the descendant (`@`, the working copy), destination was the parent (`@-`). That matches what we're doing now: source = newer commits, destination = older commit. Same direction, just expressed via a revset for the source instead of relying on the default.

So the final form stands: `jj squash --from "@---..@" --into @--- -R .` — fold the three substep commits down into the (1+2) commit at the base.

> DEVELOPER

I knew we actually did a individual squashes but your from into order is correct mine was wrong.
using --from "@---..@" what order will they be squashed I'm guessing @-- into @--- then @- into @--- then @ into @--- but then offset changes each squash? (I'm probably totally wrong)

> AGENT

Single atomic operation, not sequential squashes — so no offset shifting to worry about. Here's what jj actually does:

1. **Computes the diff for each source commit** against its parent:
   - `diff(@-- vs @---)` = ss3's changes
   - `diff(@-  vs @--)` = ss4's changes
   - `diff(@   vs @-)`  = ss5's changes
2. **Combines all three diffs and applies them to `@---`** in one transactional rewrite.
3. **Rebases descendants** (i.e., the chain @, @-, @-- gets rewritten on top of the new @---), which leaves them empty since their changes just moved.

End state:
- `@---` (now: `1+2+3+4+5`) — has everything
- `@`, `@-`, `@--` — empty commits stacked above it

The whole thing is one `jj op` entry, so `jj op restore` rolls it all back if it goes wrong.

**What about the empty commits?** jj 0.40's default behavior is to leave them in place (it doesn't auto-abandon by default in this version, as far as I'm aware). After the squash we'll likely want a follow-up:

```
jj abandon "@---..@" -R .     # remove the three now-empty commits
```

Or `jj log` first to confirm they're empty, then abandon. Either way, this isn't part of the squash operation — it's a tidy-up afterward.

Want me to run the squash and then `jj log` to inspect before deciding on the abandon?

### CLI subprocess integration tests + tempdir-root sharing (0.41.1-6.4)

First true CLI integration tests for the project. Adds a `tests/`
crate that spawns the `vc-x1` binary that Cargo built (via
`env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1")`) so argument parsing, exit codes, and
stdout/stderr are exercised end-to-end — distinct from the
in-process fixture tests under `src/init.rs::tests`, which call
`init::init_with_symlink` as a Rust function.

This step bumps the test-strategy floor from "in-process only" to
"both layers"; it doesn't yet retire any in-process tests. The
in-process tests stay as fast unit-ish coverage; subprocess tests
are added as state-mutating cases warrant a real binary spawn.

**New harness — `tests/common/mod.rs`:**

- `vc_x1() -> Command` — `Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1"))`.
- `run_ok` / `run_err` — wrap `Command::output`, panic with
  stdout+stderr embedded if exit status doesn't match expectation.
- `CliFixture` — RAII tempdir owner. Each subprocess invocation
  gets `HOME` overridden to `<base>/home/` so the user's real
  `~/.config/vc-x1/` can't leak in or get clobbered.
- `unique_base(tag)` — mirrors `src/test_helpers::unique_base` but
  uses a `vc-x1-cli-test-` prefix to distinguish from in-process
  fixtures' `vc-x1-test-` paths.
- Crate-level `#![allow(dead_code)]` — standard idiom for
  `tests/common/`: each `tests/*.rs` compiles as its own crate, so

Pairs with\n`OchidStrategy::Placeholder` from `commit_initial` (which writes\nthe placeholder) — the two together implement the dual-mode\ncross-reference dance.\n\n**`init_one` eliminated:**\n\nThe POR-branch wrapper inlined into `init_with_symlink`. After\nthe configs/gitignores split out into `write_por_config` and\nthe lifecycle split into prepare + commit, `init_one`'s body\ncollapsed to ~25 lines of straight-line composition — no\nper-side abstraction left to justify a separate function.\nReplaced the dispatch `if is_dual { create_dual } else {\ninit_one }` with `if is_dual { return create_dual(...); }`\nplus the inlined POR body.\n\n`create_dual` survives as a separate function (its body is\nlarger and the unwrap-extract-then-orchestrate shape is a\ndistinct unit).\n\n**`create_dual` cleanup:**\n\nStale \"Step N\" inline comments dropped — the function names\n(`prepare_local_repo`, `cross_ref_ochids`, `push_repo`) now\nnarrate the lifecycle on their own. Doc comment rewritten as a\nbulleted composition list referencing functions, not step\nnumbers (step numbers shift if the lifecycle reorders, function\nnames don't).\n\nThe `info!()` lines inside `create_local_repo` and `push_repo`\nstill carry `\"Step N: ...\"` labels — those land in -6.7 (single-\nword `label: body` convention).\n\n**Bug caught by the new substep protocol:**\n\nSubstep (1) extracted role-config writing out of\n`create_local_repo` into post-call helpers. That left\n`.vc-config.toml`/`.gitignore` written *after* `jj commit`\nfinalized the initial commit — i.e., they sat in the new empty\nworking copy as uncommitted files. `push_repo`'s subsequent\n`git clean -xdf` would have wiped them entirely, and the\n`push_happy_claude_clean` integration test caught the related\nsymptom (`.claude main` advanced because the session side had\npending config files at push time).\n\nResolved by substep (5): split the lifecycle (`prepare_local_repo`\n+ `commit_initial`) and reorder callers to write configs\nbetween the two. The seam now exists where role-config writing\nneeds to land — between prepare and commit.\n\nLesson logged for the substep protocol: run `cargo test --bins`\nafter each substep, not only at close-out — would have caught\nthe regression at (1) instead of (4).\n\n**Substep protocol — first use:**\n\nThis cycle was the first to use the per-substep `@` workflow:\neach substep got its own `jj new` working copy, with the chain\nsquashed into a single commit at close-out via\n`jj squash --from \"@---..@\" --into @---`. Wins on bisection\n(two `jj edit` jumps localized the (1)→(5) regression) and\nper-substep diff cleanliness. Frictions: missing\nper-substep `cargo test --bins` (above), and squash mechanics\nthat are worth jotting in CLAUDE.md if the protocol is adopted\nformally.\n\n**WIP ladder:**\n\nFive substeps, squashed at close-out:\n\n1. Drop `config`/`gitignore` params from `create_local_repo`;\n   add `write_{por,code,session}_config` helpers in `init.rs`.\n2. Extract `cross_ref_ochids` into `repo_utils.rs`.\n3. Eliminate `init_one` — inline into `init_with_symlink`'s POR\n   branch.\n4. `create_dual` collapse — drop stale `Step N` comments,\n   tighten doc.\n5. Fix: split `create_local_repo` into `prepare_local_repo` +\n   `commit_initial` (regression from (1) — role-config was\n   landing after the initial commit instead of in it).\n\n### Decisions made during design\n\n- **Version + cycle line.** This work + the sync `--check`"
}

> AGENT

Confirmed `0.41.1-6.5`. Phew.

Now on the next step — **setting the bookmark alone isn't enough** to fix this. Here's why:

`sumruqqo` still has **no description**. `jj git push` rejects pushing description-less commits, so even with the bookmark advanced to `sumruqqo`, the push will fail with the same "Won't push commit … since it has no description" error.

To go fully manual we'd need:
1. Get both sides' chids (app: `sumruqqomnzsrovvkryvwkywqkzyssst`, claude: from `jj log -R .claude`).
2. `jj describe sumruqqo -m title -m "body\n\nochid: /.claude/<claude_chid>" -R .`
3. `jj describe @ -m title -m "body\n\nochid: /<app_chid>" -R .claude`
4. Move bookmarks on both repos to point at the described commits.
5. `jj git push --bookmark init-clone-refactor -R .`
6. `jj git push --bookmark init-clone-refactor -R .claude`
7. Manually handle `.claude` finalize.

That's the manual path — works but you're hand-coordinating the ochid trailers and the dual-side bookmark/push dance that `vc-x1 push` normally handles.

The **simpler path** is the original Plan B: `jj edit sumruqqo -R .` then re-run `vc-x1 push --restart`. Push then handles description, ochid coordination, bookmark advance, and finalize automatically — same as for any normal cycle.

Which would you like to do — manual (steps 1–7) or `jj edit` + `vc-x1 push` (one command, push handles the rest)?

> DEVELOPER

I think we need to set the bookmark and it has to be tracking, correct?

> AGENT

Tracking is already set up from prior pushes — `init-clone-refactor` tracks `init-clone-refactor@origin`, and `jj op restore` doesn't undo tracking config. The `*` marker just means "local bookmark has commits ahead of origin"; it appears automatically once the bookmark is advanced past where origin sits, no setup needed.

What still needs to happen is **advancing the bookmark** from its current spot (`orxupkxs` = origin's position = -6.4) to wherever the described -6.5 commit ends up. Two ways that happens:

- **Manual path**: you run `jj bookmark set init-clone-refactor -r <described-sumruqqo> -R .` yourself.
- **`jj edit` + push path**: push's `bookmark-both` stage runs that command for you, on both repos.

So yes, the bookmark needs to advance — but it doesn't need to be "made tracking" since it already is. The `*` will appear automatically when local moves ahead.

Same question stands: which path do you want to take?

wink@3900x 26-04-30T04:28:46.434Z:~/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/.claude ((init-clone-refactor))

> AGENT

Right — important observation: **`.claude` is already in the state push expects** (work uncommitted in `@`). The op-restore on `.claude` brought back the session jsonl modifications as uncommitted, which is exactly what push wants to commit.

The asymmetry is **only on the app side**: the -6.5 work is sitting in `@-` (sumruqqo, committed) instead of in `@` (uncommitted). That's what `jj edit sumruqqo -R .` fixes — moves the work back into `@` so push's `commit-app` step has something to commit.

After `jj edit sumruqqo`:
- App `@` = sumruqqo content, uncommitted ← matches push's contract
- `.claude` `@` = session jsonl mod, uncommitted ← already matches

Then push's normal flow works on both repos: `commit-app` describes app's @, `commit-claude` describes .claude's @, `bookmark-both` advances both bookmarks, `push-app` + `push-claude` push them, `finalize-claude` cleans up.

This is the case for **Plan B** (`jj edit` + re-run push) over the manual path. Less to coordinate by hand. Want to proceed with `jj edit sumruqqo -R .`?

> DEVELOPER

Truthly I have no idea, the other option?

> AGENT

I'll just recommend: **Plan A, `jj edit sumruqqo` + re-run `vc-x1 push`**. Reasons:

- **One command** to fix the asymmetry; push handles everything else.
- Push has been doing this dual-repo coordination for cycles — title/body, both ochid trailers, both bookmarks, both pushes, finalize. Trust that machinery.
- The manual path has 7 steps where I'd be writing chids by hand into ochid trailers across both repos, advancing two bookmarks, doing two `jj git push`es, and manually invoking `vc-x1 finalize`. Lots of room for me to fat-finger something.

Plan B (manual) is mostly useful when push itself is broken or unavailable. Push works fine — we just had pre-committed work that didn't match its contract. Fix the contract, push works.

> DEVELOPER

Ok, the ladder worked but the multi `squash from into` didn't work as we thought, correct?

> AGENT

Right — the squash mechanic itself worked perfectly (folded ss3+ss4+ss5 into the (ss1+2) commit cleanly). The issue was downstream: it left the work **committed in `@-`** when `vc-x1 push` expects the work **uncommitted in `@`**. Push's `commit-app` step had nothing to commit, so the title/body landed on an empty commit on top, and the actual content commit (sumruqqo) stayed description-less.

Two ways to make the substep protocol play nicely with push:

1. **Squash + edit**: after `jj squash --from "@---..@" --into @---`, also run `jj edit @-` (the squashed commit). The squashed work is now in `@` uncommitted; push commits it normally.
2. **Skip the squash, use op-restore**: run `jj op log` to find the op right before the first `jj new` (the start of the substep ladder), then `jj op restore <op-id>`. That collapses all substep work back into the original `@` as uncommitted. No squash needed; everything is in `@` ready for push.

Option 2 is conceptually simpler ("rewind to before substeps"), but you lose the substep-by-substep op history. Option 1 keeps the substep ops in jj's op log for archaeology.

Worth a CLAUDE.md note under the substep protocol — the missing instruction that bit us this cycle.

> DEVELOPER

definitely, but it's somewhat speculation until we actuall try it in the future, but worth capturing.

> AGENT

Agreed. Let me draft the proposed CLAUDE.md note for review — keeping the speculation marker on the untested fix paths.

**Proposed addition under `## Pre-commit Requirements > ### Versioning`** (after the existing multi-step explanation, since substeps are a finer grain of the same idea):

```markdown
### Substep protocol (within a multi-step `X.Y.Z-N`)

For multi-step cycles where a single `X.Y.Z-N` covers several
separable concerns, work each substep on its own `@`:

```
jj new -R .   # start substep (1)
... do substep (1) work + cargo test --bins ...
jj new -R .   # start substep (2)
... do substep (2) work + cargo test --bins ...
... etc ...
```

At close-out, fold the substep `@`s into one commit and proceed
with the normal `vc-x1 push` flow.

**Wins observed (0.41.1-6.5):** per-substep diffs stay clean for
review; bisection is a single `jj edit` jump per candidate;
intermediate failure can be its own substep that's visible in
the substep ladder.

**Hard rule — `cargo test --bins` per substep.** Build + clippy
between substeps isn't enough. A regression introduced in (1)
of -6.5 wasn't caught until (4) ran the full test suite.

**Close-out — squash + edit.** The bot thinks the cleanest way
to reconcile per-substep `@`s with `vc-x1 push`'s contract
(work-uncommitted-in-`@`) is:

```
jj squash --from "@<base>..@" --into @<base> -R .
jj edit @- -R .         # move @ onto the squashed commit
vc-x1 push <bookmark> --yes --title "..." --body "..."
```

`@<base>` is `@---` for a 3-substep ladder, `@----` for 4, etc.
The bot thinks `jj op restore <op-before-first-substep>` is an
alternative that collapses all substep work back into `@`
uncommitted, but neither path has been re-validated since the
recovery in -6.5; treat as a working hypothesis until the next
multi-substep cycle exercises it.
```

A few notes on the draft:
- Marked the close-out recipe with "The bot thinks" since it's untested past the recovery itself.
- Anchored the lesson (`cargo test --bins` per substep) as a hard rule since we observed it directly.
- Kept the wins concise and grounded in the actual cycle.

Want this landed as its own micro-cycle (small chore commit), or piggybacked on the start of -6.6 / a future cycle? It's a CLAUDE.md-only edit — pure documentation.
